  --tools <tools...>                                Specify the list of available tools from the built-in set. Use "" to disable all tools, "default" to use all tools, or specify tool names (e.g. "Bash,Edit,Read").

1	"""``drain-cycle`` CLI entry point.
2	
3	Zero-arg invocation drains the current Linear cycle. Each issue's target
4	repo is resolved from a ``repo:<name>`` label against
5	``~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml``; the operator runs ``drain-cycle`` from
6	anywhere, not from inside a target repo. The ``grade`` subcommand reads
7	the run logs and prints a health read.
8	
9	Secrets load before any module reads ``os.environ``, first hit wins:
10	shell-exported vars → ``~/.drain-cycle/.env`` → the drain-cycle repo
11	root ``.env`` (dev-checkout fallback, absent once installed as a uv
12	tool). ``load_dotenv`` defaults to ``override=False``, so an
13	already-set var always beats a later source and the shell always wins.
14	
15	``repos.yml`` and the optional ``limits.yml`` are validated eagerly at
16	startup so a broken config halts exit 1 on stderr before any Linear
17	traffic or run-log file is written — there is no cycle yet to log
18	against. ``limits.yml`` is optional (its absence yields the baked-in
19	guardrail defaults); a present-but-malformed one still halts.
20	"""
21	from __future__ import annotations
22	
23	import sys
24	from pathlib import Path
25	
26	from dotenv import load_dotenv
27	
28	from . import grade, limits, orchestrator, repos
29	
30	_REPO_ENV = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent / ".env"
31	
32	
33	def _load_secrets() -> None:
34	    """Populate ``os.environ`` from the first ``.env`` that defines each key.
35	
36	    ``~/.drain-cycle/.env`` sits beside ``repos.yml`` and the run logs,
37	    so an installed tool finds its secret there. The repo-root ``.env``
38	    is a dev-checkout fallback only — once installed as a uv tool the
39	    package lives in an isolated env where that path has no ``.env``.
40	    ``$HOME`` is resolved per call so tests can redirect it.
41	    """
42	    load_dotenv(Path.home() / ".drain-cycle" / ".env")
43	    load_dotenv(_REPO_ENV)
44	
45	
46	_USAGE = (
47	    "usage: drain-cycle              drain the current Linear cycle\n"
48	    "       drain-cycle grade        print health read from run logs\n"
49	    "       drain-cycle --help"
50	)
51	
52	
53	def main() -> None:
54	    _load_secrets()
55	    argv = sys.argv[1:]
56	    if not argv:
57	        try:
58	            loaded_repos = repos.load()
59	            loaded_limits = limits.load()
60	        except (repos.RepoConfigError, limits.LimitsConfigError) as exc:
61	            print(f"drain-cycle: {exc}", file=sys.stderr)
62	            sys.exit(1)
63	        sys.exit(orchestrator.run(loaded_repos, loaded_limits))
64	    if argv == ["grade"]:
65	        sys.exit(grade.run(grade.default_runs_dir()))
66	    if argv in (["-h"], ["--help"]):
67	        print(_USAGE)
68	        sys.exit(0)
69	    print(f"drain-cycle: unknown invocation: {' '.join(argv)}", file=sys.stderr)
70	    print(_USAGE, file=sys.stderr)
71	    sys.exit(2)
72	
```

1	"""Prompt builder for spawned ``claude -p`` sessions.
2	
3	The prompt is the entire contract between the orchestrator and the spawned
4	agent — there's no system prompt, no multi-turn loop. The four-segment
5	ordering below is load-bearing: the agent reads top-down, so context
6	(title + body) comes before instructions (preamble + tail), and the tail
7	line is last so it stays in the trailing-tokens window the model attends
8	to most strongly.
9	"""
10	from __future__ import annotations
11	
12	from pathlib import Path
13	from typing import Any
14	
15	
16	_TAIL = (
17	    "before marking Done: run /code-review-and-quality on the working-tree "
18	    "changes, fix Critical/Required findings, commit + push, then post a "
19	    "review-summary comment on the issue and transition to Done."
20	)
21	
22	
23	def build(issue: dict[str, Any], worktree: Path) -> str:
24	    title = issue.get("title", "")
25	    description = issue.get("description") or ""
26	    identifier = issue.get("identifier", "")
27	
28	    preamble = (
29	        "---\n\n"
30	        "Execution instructions:\n"
31	        f"- Working directory: {worktree}\n"
32	        "- Base branch: main\n"
33	        f"- Completion sequence for issue {identifier} (run in this order, "
34	        "before marking Done):\n"
35	        "  1. Run `/code-review-and-quality` against the working-tree changes.\n"
36	        "  2. Fix any Critical or Required findings. Lower-severity findings "
37	        "are at your discretion.\n"
38	        "  3. Commit and push to main.\n"
39	        "  4. Post a short review-summary comment on the Linear issue via "
40	        "`mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_comment` (count of findings by severity, "
41	        "fixed vs deferred).\n"
42	        "  5. Transition issue to Done via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_issue` "
43	        '(state: "Done").\n'
44	    )
45	
46	    return (
47	        f"# {title}\n\n"
48	        f"{description}\n\n"
49	        f"{preamble}\n"
50	        f"{_TAIL}\n"
51	    )
52	
```

1	"""Unit tests for the streaming worker.
2	
3	What we substitute and why:
4	
5	* Spawned ``claude -p``: replaced with a real shell script that ``cat``s a
6	  canned stream-json file to stdout (the canned file sidesteps shell
7	  JSON-quoting). This is the only honest way to exercise the line-by-line
8	  Popen reader, the dedup-by-message-id accumulation, and the process-group
9	  kill — an in-process mock of ``Popen`` would prove none of those.
10	* Real OS process groups: the timeout test spawns a script that itself
11	  backgrounds a grandchild ``sleep``; after the worker times out we assert
12	  the grandchild pid is dead, which only holds if the *group* (not just the
13	  direct child) was killed.
14	
15	The canned ``assistant`` events deliberately repeat one message id across
16	two events — the real ``stream-json`` emits a turn once per content block
17	(thinking, text, tool_use), each copy carrying the identical usage — so
18	the happy-path test pins that a turn is counted exactly once.
19	"""
20	from __future__ import annotations
21	
22	import io
23	import json
24	import os
25	import time
26	from pathlib import Path
27	
28	from drain_cycle import worker
29	
30	
31	def _fake_claude_streaming(tmp_path: Path, lines: list[str], *, hang: bool = False) -> Path:
32	    """A ``claude -p`` stand-in that emits ``lines`` as stdout then exits.
33	
34	    With ``hang=True`` the script sleeps after emitting (holding stdout
35	    open and producing no terminal ``result``), so the worker hits its
36	    timeout with a partially-parsed stream.
37	    """
38	    stream_file = tmp_path / "stream.jsonl"
39	    stream_file.write_text("\n".join(lines) + "\n")
40	    script = tmp_path / "fake-claude.sh"
41	    body = f'#!/bin/sh\ncat "{stream_file}"\n'
42	    if hang:
43	        body += "sleep 30\n"
44	    script.write_text(body)
45	    script.chmod(0o755)
46	    return script
47	
48	
49	def _assistant(message_id: str, *, inp: int, out: int, cc: int, cr: int) -> str:
50	    return json.dumps(
51	        {
52	            "type": "assistant",
53	            "message": {
54	                "id": message_id,
55	                "usage": {
56	                    "input_tokens": inp,
57	                    "output_tokens": out,
58	                    "cache_creation_input_tokens": cc,
59	                    "cache_read_input_tokens": cr,
60	                },
61	            },
62	        }
63	    )
64	
65	
66	def _result(**fields: object) -> str:
67	    return json.dumps({"type": "result", **fields})
68	
69	
70	def _wait_dead(pid: int, timeout: float = 3.0) -> bool:
71	    """Poll until ``pid`` no longer exists (process-group kill reaped it)."""
72	    deadline = time.monotonic() + timeout
73	    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
74	        try:
75	            os.kill(pid, 0)
76	        except ProcessLookupError:
77	            return True
78	        time.sleep(0.05)
79	    try:
80	        os.kill(pid, 0)
81	        return False
82	    except ProcessLookupError:
83	        return True
84	
85	
86	def test_run_issue_accumulates_usage_deduped_by_message_id(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
87	    """Two turns, the first emitted twice under one id; the duplicate is
88	    counted once, totals sum across turns, peak_context is the max single
89	    turn, and the session summary comes from the ``result`` event."""
90	    lines = [
91	        json.dumps({"type": "system", "subtype": "init", "session_id": "sess-1"}),
92	        # Turn one, emitted twice (thinking block then text block) — same id.
93	        _assistant("msg_a", inp=10, out=4, cc=100, cr=0),
94	        _assistant("msg_a", inp=10, out=4, cc=100, cr=0),
95	        "this is not json and must be tolerated",
96	        # Turn two — distinct id, larger context.
97	        _assistant("msg_b", inp=5, out=20, cc=0, cr=300),
98	        _result(
99	            total_cost_usd=0.42,
100	            num_turns=2,
101	            session_id="sess-1",
102	            is_error=False,
103	            usage={"input_tokens": 5, "output_tokens": 20},
104	        ),
105	    ]
106	    script = _fake_claude_streaming(tmp_path, lines)
107	    passthrough = io.StringIO()
108	
109	    result = worker.run_issue(
110	        claude_cmd=[str(script)],
111	        model="claude-sonnet-4-6",
112	        prompt="ignored by the fake",
113	        cwd=tmp_path,
114	        token_limit=None,
115	        time_limit_seconds=30.0,
116	        cost_limit_usd=None,
117	        passthrough=passthrough,
118	    )
119	
120	    assert result.breach is None
121	    assert result.exit_code == 0
122	    assert result.model == "claude-sonnet-4-6"
123	
124	    # msg_a counted once: input 10+5=15, output 4+20=24, cc 100+0=100,
125	    # cr 0+300=300 → cumulative 439.
126	    assert result.usage == {
127	        "input_tokens": 15,
128	        "output_tokens": 24,
129	        "cache_creation_input_tokens": 100,
130	        "cache_read_input_tokens": 300,
131	        "cumulative": 439,
132	        "peak_context": 305,  # turn two: 5 + 300 + 0
133	    }
134	
135	    # Session summary from the terminal result event.
136	    assert result.cost_usd == 0.42
137	    assert result.num_turns == 2
138	    assert result.session_id == "sess-1"
139	    assert result.is_error is False
140	
141	    assert result.duration_seconds >= 0.0
142	    # The non-JSON line was echoed to the passthrough, not silently dropped.
143	    assert "this is not json and must be tolerated" in passthrough.getvalue()
144	
145	
146	def test_run_issue_times_out_and_kills_whole_process_group(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
147	    """A worker that overruns its time cap is killed at the process-group
148	    level, reaping a grandchild that a kill of the direct child alone would
149	    orphan — the bug ``subprocess.run(timeout=)`` had."""
150	    pidfile = tmp_path / "grandchild.pid"
151	    script = tmp_path / "hanging-claude.sh"
152	    script.write_text(
153	        "#!/bin/sh\n"
154	        "sleep 30 &\n"  # grandchild; would survive a direct-child-only kill
155	        f'echo $! > "{pidfile}"\n'
156	        "sleep 30\n"  # the worker itself hangs, emitting no output
157	    )
158	    script.chmod(0o755)
159	
160	    result = worker.run_issue(
161	        claude_cmd=[str(script)],
162	        model="claude-sonnet-4-6",
163	        prompt="ignored",
164	        cwd=tmp_path,
165	        token_limit=None,
166	        time_limit_seconds=0.5,
167	        cost_limit_usd=None,
168	        passthrough=io.StringIO(),
169	        poll_interval_seconds=0.05,
170	    )
171	
172	    assert result.breach is not None
173	    assert result.breach.scope == "per-issue"
174	    assert result.breach.metric == "time"
175	    assert result.breach.limit == 0.5
176	    assert result.breach.observed >= 0.5
177	    # Killed by signal → negative return code.
178	    assert result.exit_code < 0
179	    # No stream was emitted, so usage is all-zero and the summary is empty.
180	    assert result.usage["cumulative"] == 0
181	    assert result.usage["peak_context"] == 0
182	    assert result.cost_usd is None
183	    assert result.num_turns is None
184	    assert result.session_id is None
185	    assert result.is_error is None
186	
187	    grandchild_pid = int(pidfile.read_text().strip())
188	    assert _wait_dead(grandchild_pid), (
189	        f"grandchild {grandchild_pid} survived the time cap — process group "
190	        "was not killed"
191	    )
192	
193	
194	def test_run_issue_falls_back_to_accumulated_usage_when_killed_before_result(
195	    tmp_path: Path,
196	) -> None:
197	    """If the session is killed before a ``result`` event, the usage parsed
198	    from the partial stream is still recorded; the result-only summary
199	    fields are left ``None``."""
200	    lines = [
201	        _assistant("msg_a", inp=10, out=4, cc=100, cr=0),
202	        _assistant("msg_b", inp=5, out=20, cc=0, cr=300),
203	    ]
204	    script = _fake_claude_streaming(tmp_path, lines, hang=True)
205	
206	    result = worker.run_issue(
207	        claude_cmd=[str(script)],
208	        model="claude-opus-4-7",
209	        prompt="ignored",
210	        cwd=tmp_path,
211	        token_limit=None,
212	        time_limit_seconds=1.0,
213	        cost_limit_usd=None,
214	        passthrough=io.StringIO(),
215	        poll_interval_seconds=0.05,
216	    )
217	
218	    assert result.breach is not None
219	    assert result.breach.metric == "time"
220	    # Accumulated from the two partial-stream turns.
221	    assert result.usage["cumulative"] == 439
222	    assert result.usage["peak_context"] == 305
223	    assert result.usage["cache_read_input_tokens"] == 300
224	    # No result event arrived.
225	    assert result.cost_usd is None
226	    assert result.num_turns is None
227	    assert result.session_id is None
228	    assert result.is_error is None
229	
230	
231	def test_run_issue_kills_process_group_on_token_breach(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
232	    """The primary guardrail: a session whose live cumulative tokens cross
233	    the per-issue cap is killed at the process-group level (grandchild
234	    reaped), and the breach names the cap and the value at kill time."""
235	    stream_file = tmp_path / "stream.jsonl"
236	    # One turn far over the tiny cap, then the script hangs so the monitor's
237	    # poll catches the breach instead of the process exiting on its own.
238	    stream_file.write_text(_assistant("msg_big", inp=10_000, out=0, cc=0, cr=0) + "\n")
239	    pidfile = tmp_path / "grandchild.pid"
240	    script = tmp_path / "overshoot-claude.sh"
241	    script.write_text(
242	        "#!/bin/sh\n"
243	        f'cat "{stream_file}"\n'  # emit the overshooting usage
244	        "sleep 30 &\n"  # grandchild; survives a direct-child-only kill
245	        f'echo $! > "{pidfile}"\n'
246	        "sleep 30\n"  # hang past the cap
247	    )
248	    script.chmod(0o755)
249	
250	    result = worker.run_issue(
251	        claude_cmd=[str(script)],
252	        model="claude-sonnet-4-6",
253	        prompt="ignored",
254	        cwd=tmp_path,
255	        token_limit=100,
256	        time_limit_seconds=None,
257	        cost_limit_usd=None,
258	        passthrough=io.StringIO(),
259	        poll_interval_seconds=0.05,
260	    )
261	
262	    assert result.breach is not None
263	    assert result.breach.scope == "per-issue"
264	    assert result.breach.metric == "token"
265	    assert result.breach.limit == 100
266	    assert result.breach.observed >= 100
267	    assert result.exit_code < 0
268	    # The overshooting turn was recorded before the kill.
269	    assert result.usage["cumulative"] == 10_000
270	
271	    grandchild_pid = int(pidfile.read_text().strip())
272	    assert _wait_dead(grandchild_pid), (
273	        f"grandchild {grandchild_pid} survived the token cap — process group "
274	        "was not killed"
275	    )
276	
277	
278	def _argv_capturing_claude(tmp_path: Path, argv_file: Path) -> Path:
279	    """A ``claude -p`` stand-in that records its argv (one token per line)
280	    to ``argv_file`` then exits, so a test can assert which flags were passed."""
281	    script = tmp_path / "argv-claude.sh"
282	    script.write_text(
283	        "#!/bin/sh\n"
284	        f': > "{argv_file}"\n'
285	        f'for a in "$@"; do printf "%s\\n" "$a" >> "{argv_file}"; done\n'
286	    )
287	    script.chmod(0o755)
288	    return script
289	
290	
291	def test_run_issue_passes_max_budget_usd_flag(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
292	    """The per-issue cost cap is handed to ``claude`` as ``--max-budget-usd``
293	    (the native belt), and the prompt stays the trailing positional after the
294	    value-taking flags."""
295	    argv_file = tmp_path / "argv.txt"
296	    script = _argv_capturing_claude(tmp_path, argv_file)
297	
298	    result = worker.run_issue(
299	        claude_cmd=[str(script)],
300	        model="claude-sonnet-4-6",
301	        prompt="THE-PROMPT",

```
1	"""Per-issue and per-cycle resource guardrails.
2	
3	A spawned ``claude -p`` worker can run away — burning tokens, wall-clock,
4	and quota with no ceiling but the outer per-issue timeout. This module
5	defines the thresholds that bound that spend and the ``Breach`` value that
6	names which one was crossed.
7	
8	**Two layers, belt and suspenders.**
9	
10	* *Native belt* — ``per_issue_cost_usd`` is handed to ``claude`` as
11	  ``--max-budget-usd`` so the session self-terminates on cost without the
12	  orchestrator having to watch it.
13	* *Orchestrator suspenders* — ``per_issue_tokens`` and ``per_issue_seconds``
14	  are enforced by the worker against the live event stream: when either is
15	  crossed the worker SIGKILLs the session's process group (see
16	  ``worker.py``). The ``cycle_*`` caps are enforced by the orchestrator
17	  between issues via ``check_cycle`` — death-by-aggregate, where every issue
18	  is individually under its cap but their sum drains the quota, is exactly
19	  what the per-issue caps cannot catch.
20	
21	Token count is the primary guardrail (a subscription user pays in tokens,
22	not dollars); cost rides alongside it.
23	
24	**On/off per guardrail.** Any threshold may be ``None`` — that guardrail is
25	off. The defaults below are all live; an operator turns one off by setting
26	its key to ``null`` in ``~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml``.
27	
28	**Load precedence.** Baked-in defaults, overlaid by ``limits.yml`` if it
29	exists: a key absent from the file keeps its default, an explicit ``null``
30	turns the guardrail off, a positive number overrides it. A present-but-
31	malformed file raises ``LimitsConfigError`` rather than silently falling
32	back to defaults — a typo that quietly disabled a cap the operator believed
33	was active is worse than a loud startup halt. The defaults are deliberately
34	generous starting points; recalibrate against real run-log spend.
35	"""
36	from __future__ import annotations
37	
38	from dataclasses import dataclass, fields
39	from pathlib import Path
40	from typing import Any
41	
42	import yaml
43	
44	_CONFIG_DISPLAY = "~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml"
45	
46	
47	class LimitsConfigError(RuntimeError):
48	    """``limits.yml`` exists but is malformed."""
49	
50	
51	@dataclass(frozen=True)
52	class Breach:
53	    """A crossed guardrail, recorded into the halting run-log entry.
54	
55	    ``scope`` is ``"per-issue"`` or ``"cycle"``; ``metric`` is ``"token"``,
56	    ``"time"``, or ``"cost"``. ``limit`` is the cap that was set and
57	    ``observed`` the value at the moment of the breach. ``describe`` renders
58	    the operator-facing line — the worker uses it for a per-issue kill and
59	    the orchestrator for a cycle stop, so the wording cannot drift between
60	    the two surfaces.
61	    """
62	
63	    scope: str
64	    metric: str
65	    limit: float
66	    observed: float
67	
68	    def describe(self) -> str:
69	        return f"{self.scope} {self.metric} cap exceeded: {self._detail()}"
70	
71	    def _detail(self) -> str:
72	        if self.metric == "cost":
73	            return f"${self.observed:,.2f} ≥ ${self.limit:,.2f}"
74	        if self.metric == "time":
75	            return f"{self.observed:,.0f}s ≥ {self.limit:,.0f}s"
76	        return f"{self.observed:,.0f} ≥ {self.limit:,.0f} tokens"
77	
78	
79	@dataclass(frozen=True)
80	class Limits:
81	    """Resource caps. ``None`` on any field turns that guardrail off.
82	
83	    Defaults: per-issue 8M tokens · 20 min · $15; cycle 30M tokens ·
84	    90 min · $60.
85	    """
86	
87	    per_issue_tokens: int | None = 8_000_000
88	    per_issue_seconds: float | None = 20 * 60
89	    per_issue_cost_usd: float | None = 15.0
90	    cycle_tokens: int | None = 30_000_000
91	    cycle_seconds: float | None = 90 * 60
92	    cycle_cost_usd: float | None = 60.0
93	
94	
95	def default_config_path() -> Path:
96	    """Resolved per call so tests can redirect via ``monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", ...)``."""
97	    return Path.home() / ".drain-cycle" / "limits.yml"
98	
99	
100	def load(path: Path | None = None) -> Limits:
101	    """Read ``limits.yml`` over the baked-in defaults.
102	
103	    A missing or empty file yields the defaults unchanged — ``limits.yml``
104	    is optional. A present file maps limit names to positive numbers (or
105	    ``null`` to disable that guardrail); unknown keys, non-numeric values,
106	    booleans, and non-positive numbers each raise ``LimitsConfigError`` with
107	    a message naming the on-disk problem.
108	    """
109	    defaults = Limits()
110	    if path is None:
111	        path = default_config_path()
112	    if not path.exists():
113	        return defaults
114	    try:
115	        data = yaml.safe_load(path.read_text())
116	    except yaml.YAMLError as exc:
117	        raise LimitsConfigError(f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} is not valid YAML: {exc}") from None
118	    if data is None:
119	        return defaults
120	    if not isinstance(data, dict):
121	        raise LimitsConfigError(
122	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} must be a mapping of limit names to numbers"
123	        )
124	
125	    valid = [f.name for f in fields(Limits)]
126	    unknown = set(data) - set(valid)
127	    if unknown:
128	        raise LimitsConfigError(
129	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} has unknown keys: {', '.join(sorted(unknown))}; "
130	            f"valid keys: {', '.join(valid)}"
131	        )
132	
133	    values: dict[str, Any] = {}
134	    for name in valid:
135	        if name not in data:
136	            values[name] = getattr(defaults, name)
137	            continue
138	        values[name] = _coerce(name, data[name])
139	    return Limits(**values)
140	
141	
142	def _coerce(name: str, raw: Any) -> float | None:
143	    """Validate one ``limits.yml`` value: ``None`` (off) or a positive number."""
144	    if raw is None:
145	        return None
146	    # ``bool`` is a subclass of ``int``; ``per_issue_tokens: true`` would
147	    # otherwise slip through as ``1`` and silently arm an absurd cap.
148	    if isinstance(raw, bool) or not isinstance(raw, (int, float)) or raw <= 0:
149	        raise LimitsConfigError(
150	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} entry {name!r}: must be a positive number "
151	            f"or null (got {raw!r})"
152	        )
153	    return raw
154	
155	
156	def check_cycle(
157	    limits: Limits, *, tokens: int, cost_usd: float, seconds: float
158	) -> Breach | None:
159	    """Return the first cycle-wide cap the running totals breach, else ``None``.
160	
161	    Tokens are checked first (the primary guardrail), then cost, then
162	    wall-clock. A guardrail set to ``None`` is skipped.
163	    """
164	    if limits.cycle_tokens is not None and tokens >= limits.cycle_tokens:
165	        return Breach("cycle", "token", limits.cycle_tokens, tokens)
166	    if limits.cycle_cost_usd is not None and cost_usd >= limits.cycle_cost_usd:
167	        return Breach("cycle", "cost", limits.cycle_cost_usd, cost_usd)
168	    if limits.cycle_seconds is not None and seconds >= limits.cycle_seconds:
169	        return Breach("cycle", "time", limits.cycle_seconds, seconds)
170	    return None
171	
```

> AGENT

This is the smoking gun. `.claude/` is **gitignored** (last line of `.gitignore`), so `.claude/settings.json` — where entire.io registers all its hooks (SessionStart, etc.) — is **untracked**. A git worktree branched off `main` checks out only tracked files, and git treats the worktree dir as its own root. So the worktree has no `.claude/settings.json`, and entire's project-scoped hooks are absent there.

The acceptance criteria require a reproduction run to confirm. Let me run `claude -p --debug-file` from the repo root first, then from a worktree, and diff. Starting with the repo root:

> AGENT

`timeout` isn't installed on macOS (it's `gtimeout`). That was the 127, not `claude`. The orchestrator spawns bare `claude`; an operator's normal shell resolves that to `~/.local/bin/claude` (the standard install). Let me use the Bash tool's own timeout and the explicit standard binary.

```
1	"""Thin wrapper around ``git worktree``.
2	
3	Each issue gets ``.worktrees/<issue-identifier>/`` branched off ``main`` per
4	README §3, used once, then removed on Done.
5	
6	``git worktree`` stderr is captured and surfaced in the raised
7	``RuntimeError`` on failure. The orchestrator's pre-spawn try/except
8	threads the message into the runlog's ``halt_reason`` so the operator
9	sees git's actual diagnostic (dirty tree, branch already exists,
10	missing ``main``) rather than just a non-zero exit code.
11	"""
12	from __future__ import annotations
13	
14	import subprocess
15	from pathlib import Path
16	
17	BASE_BRANCH = "main"
18	WORKTREE_DIR = ".worktrees"
19	
20	
21	def add(repo: Path, identifier: str) -> Path:
22	    """Create a worktree branched off ``main`` for ``identifier``.
23	
24	    Returns the absolute path to the new worktree.
25	    """
26	    worktree_path = repo / WORKTREE_DIR / identifier
27	    _run_git(
28	        ["worktree", "add", "-b", identifier, str(worktree_path), BASE_BRANCH],
29	        cwd=repo,
30	    )
31	    return worktree_path
32	
33	
34	def remove(repo: Path, worktree_path: Path) -> None:
35	    """Remove a worktree previously created by :func:`add`."""
36	    _run_git(["worktree", "remove", str(worktree_path)], cwd=repo)
37	
38	
39	def _run_git(args: list[str], *, cwd: Path) -> None:
40	    result = subprocess.run(
41	        ["git", *args],
42	        cwd=cwd,
43	        check=False,
44	        capture_output=True,
45	        text=True,
46	    )
47	    if result.returncode != 0:
48	        raise RuntimeError(
49	            f"git {' '.join(args)} failed (exit {result.returncode}): "
50	            f"{result.stderr.strip() or result.stdout.strip() or '<no output>'}"
51	        )
52	
```

> AGENT

Complete and definitive. The evidence:

| | Repo root | Worktree |
|---|---|---|
| Settings files watched | user + **project** `.claude/settings.json` + local | **user only** |
| Project `.claude/settings.json` | loaded | "Broken symlink or missing file" |
| entire references | 5 (SessionStart + SessionEnd fire, project deny rule) | **0** |
| Plugins (crit/hookify/agent-skills/security-guidance) | "Registered 7 hooks from 15 plugins" | **identical: "Registered 7 hooks from 15 plugins"** |

The hypothesis is **confirmed and refined**: it's not the cwd per se, and it's not user-scoped plugins (those load identically). The precise mechanism is that entire registers its hooks in the **project-scoped `.claude/settings.json`, which is gitignored** — so the fresh worktree checkout omits it, and git treats the worktree as its own project root, leaving Claude Code with no project settings to load there.

```
{"id":"ABA-238","title":"drain-cycle: plugins (entire.io) don't load headless — capture and document the divergence","description":"## Problem\n\nThe operator noticed some normal plugins (e.g. [entire.io](<http://entire.io>)'s checkpointing) didn't take effect during the headless drain, hinting the spawn environment diverges from an interactive session more than intended. A verification spike showed hooks *do* fire under `claude -p` (entire's SessionStart hook fired when run from a normal repo root) — so the likely difference is the worker's **worktree cwd** (`.worktrees/<id>` of the target repo) and/or where entire's hook is registered. Root cause needs runtime confirmation, not a guess.\n\n## Change (observability-first — no blind fix)\n\n* Add an opt-in `--debug-file <path>` to the worker spawn, written next to the run log, so the next run records exactly which plugins / MCP servers / hooks initialised.\n* Add a documented one-shot reproduction step to `docs/design-decisions.md`: run `claude -p` headless in a worktree vs a repo root and diff the loaded plugins/hooks.\n* Add a design-decision entry naming the headless-vs-interactive divergence as a known constraint, with the open question and the spike evidence (hooks fire under `-p`; 1	"""Drain-the-cycle iteration test for the orchestrator.
2	
3	The walking skeleton covered one issue end-to-end. This test pins the
4	behaviour that *every* sorted Todo/Backlog issue is processed in order, each
5	in its own worktree, and that every worktree is removed when the spawned
6	session signals completion.
7	
8	What we substitute and why:
9	
10	* Linear: stubbed in-process. The orchestrator imports the linear module by
11	  attribute, so monkey-patching ``cycle_id`` / ``pending_issues`` / ``get_issue``
12	  on it is sufficient. We do **not** stub the GraphQL transport — the layer
13	  under test here is the orchestrator loop, not the wire format (the wire
14	  format is exercised separately).
15	* Spawned ``claude -p``: replaced with a real shell script via
16	  ``_CLAUDE_CMD``. The script writes the basename of its cwd (the issue
17	  identifier — that's how the orchestrator names worktrees) into a shared
18	  marker file. The stubbed ``get_issue`` reads that file to decide which
19	  issues are Done. This satisfies the spec's "no-op script that calls back
20	  into the stubbed Linear" — the callback path is the marker file rather
21	  than an in-process call, because the script runs in a separate process.
22	* Git: a real ``git init`` repo with one commit on ``main``. ``git worktree``
23	  is exercised for real; this is the cheapest way to be sure the orchestrator
24	  doesn't paper over a worktree problem.
25	"""
26	from __future__ import annotations
27	
28	import subprocess
29	from pathlib import Path
30	
31	import pytest
32	
33	from drain_cycle import linear, orchestrator, repos
34	
35	
36	def _issue(
37	    identifier: str,
38	    priority: int,
39	    sort_order: float,
40	    *,
41	    repo_name: str = "test-repo",
42	) -> dict:
43	    return {
44	        "id": f"id-{identifier}",
45	        "identifier": identifier,
46	        "title": f"Title for {identifier}",
47	        "description": f"Body for {identifier}",
48	        "priority": priority,
49	        "sortOrder": sort_order,
50	        "state": {"type": "unstarted", "name": "Todo"},
51	        "labels": [f"repo:{repo_name}"],
52	    }
53	
54	
55	def _init_repo(repo: Path) -> None:
56	    """Create a real git repo with one commit on ``main`` for worktree tests."""
57	    subprocess.run(["git", "init", "-b", "main"], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True)
58	    subprocess.run(
59	        ["git", "config", "user.email", "test@example.com"], cwd=repo, check=True
60	    )
61	    subprocess.run(["git", "config", "user.name", "Test"], cwd=repo, check=True)
62	    (repo / "README.md").write_text("seed\n")
63	    subprocess.run(["git", "add", "."], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True)
64	    subprocess.run(
65	        ["git", "commit", "-m", "seed"], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True
66	    )
67	
68	
69	def test_orchestrator_drains_every_issue_in_sorted_order(
70	    tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch
71	) -> None:
72	    repo = tmp_path / "repo"
73	    repo.mkdir()
74	    _init_repo(repo)
75	    monkeypatch.chdir(repo)
76	    monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", str(tmp_path))
77	
78	    # Three issues — chosen so a "naïve" iteration order (input order) and
79	    # the priority sort disagree, proving the loop consumes the *sorted*
80	    # list rather than the unsorted one.
81	    raw_issues = [
82	        _issue("ABA-X", priority=3, sort_order=2.0),  # Medium
83	        _issue("ABA-Y", priority=1, sort_order=1.0),  # Urgent — runs first
84	        _issue("ABA-Z", priority=2, sort_order=3.0),  # High
85	    ]
86	    sorted_issues = linear._sort_pending_issues(raw_issues)
87	    expected_order = [i["identifier"] for i in sorted_issues]
88	    assert expected_order == ["ABA-Y", "ABA-Z", "ABA-X"]
89	
90	    issues_by_id = {i["id"]: i for i in raw_issues}
91	    done_marker = tmp_path / "done-identifiers.txt"
92	
93	    def fake_current_cycle_id() -> str:
94	        return "stub-cycle"
95	
96	    def fake_pending_issues(cycle_id: str) -> list[dict]:
97	        # Re-sort each call (mirrors the real client, which sorts the wire
98	        # response) and exclude anything the fake claude script has already
99	        # marked Done — so an accidental re-fetch can't re-feed the same
100	        # issue back into the loop.
101	        completed = _completed_identifiers(done_marker)
102	        return linear._sort_pending_issues(
103	            [i for i in raw_issues if i["identifier"] not in completed]
104	        )
105	
106	    def fake_get_issue(issue_id: str) -> dict:
107	        issue = issues_by_id[issue_id]
108	        if issue["identifier"] in _completed_identifiers(done_marker):
109	            return {**issue, "state": {"type": "completed", "name": "Done"}}
110	        return issue
111	
112	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "current_cycle_id", fake_current_cycle_id)
113	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "pending_issues", fake_pending_issues)
114	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "get_issue", fake_get_issue)
115	    # set_state is exercised by tests/test_orchestrator_set_state.py; here it's
116	    # a no-op so this test stays focused on iteration order + worktree cleanup.
117	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "set_state", lambda issue_id, state_name: None)
118	
119	    fake_claude = _write_fake_claude_script(tmp_path, done_marker)
120	    monkeypatch.setattr(orchestrator, "_CLAUDE_CMD", [str(fake_claude)])
121	
122	    exit_code = orchestrator.run(repos.Repos(mapping={"test-repo": repo}))
123	
124	    assert exit_code == 0
125	    # The script appends the worktree-basename (== issue identifier) on each
126	    # run, so the file contents are the exact processing order.
127	    assert done_marker.read_text().splitlines() == expected_order
128	    # Every worktree was removed on success.
129	    worktrees_dir = repo / ".worktrees"
130	    assert not worktrees_dir.exists() or not any(worktrees_dir.iterdir())
131	    # ``git worktree list`` should show only the main checkout.
132	    listed = subprocess.run(
133	        ["git", "worktree", "list", "--porcelain"],
134	        cwd=repo,
135	        check=True,
136	        capture_output=True,
137	        text=True,
138	    ).stdout
139	    assert listed.count("worktree ") == 1
140	
141	
142	def test_orchestrator_passes_resolved_model_to_worker(
143	    tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch
144	) -> None:
145	    """The model resolved from the issue's ``model:`` label reaches the
146	    spawned command as ``--model <resolved>`` — default Sonnet when absent,
147	    the labelled override otherwise."""
148	    repo = tmp_path / "repo"
149	    repo.mkdir()
150	    _init_repo(repo)
151	    monkeypatch.chdir(repo)
152	    monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", str(tmp_path))
153	
154	    default_issue = _issue("ABA-DEF", priority=1, sort_order=1.0)
155	    opus_issue = _issue("ABA-OPUS", priority=2, sort_order=2.0)
156	    opus_issue["labels"] = ["repo:test-repo", "model:opus"]
157	    raw_issues = [default_issue, opus_issue]
158	
159	    issues_by_id = {i["id"]: i for i in raw_issues}
160	    done_marker = tmp_path / "done-identifiers.txt"
161	    argv_dir = tmp_path / "argv"
162	    argv_dir.mkdir()
163	
164	    def fake_pending_issues(cycle_id: str) -> list[dict]:
165	        completed = _completed_identifiers(done_marker)
166	        return linear._sort_pending_issues(
167	            [i for i in raw_issues if i["identifier"] not in completed]
168	        )
169	
170	    def fake_get_issue(issue_id: str) -> dict:
171	        issue = issues_by_id[issue_id]
172	        if issue["identifier"] in _completed_identifiers(done_marker):
173	            return {**issue, "state": {"type": "completed", "name": "Done"}}
174	        return issue
175	
176	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "current_cycle_id", lambda: "stub-cycle")
177	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "pending_issues", fake_pending_issues)
178	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "get_issue", fake_get_issue)
179	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "set_state", lambda issue_id, state_name: None)
180	
181	    fake_claude = _write_argv_capturing_claude_script(tmp_path, done_marker, argv_dir)
182	    monkeypatch.setattr(orchestrator, "_CLAUDE_CMD", [str(fake_claude)])
183	
184	    exit_code = orchestrator.run(repos.Repos(mapping={"test-repo": repo}))
185	    assert exit_code == 0
186	
187	    assert _model_arg(argv_dir / "ABA-DEF.txt") == "claude-sonnet-4-6"
188	    assert _model_arg(argv_dir / "ABA-OPUS.txt") == "claude-opus-4-7"
189	
190	
191	def _model_arg(argv_file: Path) -> str:
192	    """Return the token following ``--model`` in a captured argv file."""
193	    argv = argv_file.read_text().splitlines()
194	    return argv[argv.index("--model") + 1]
195	
196	
197	def _write_argv_capturing_claude_script(
198	    tmp_path: Path, done_marker: Path, argv_dir: Path
199	) -> Path:
200	    """A ``claude -p`` stand-in that records its argv (one token per line)
201	    to ``argv_dir/<identifier>.txt`` and marks the issue Done."""
202	    script = tmp_path / "fake-claude-argv.sh"
203	    script.write_text(
204	        "#!/bin/sh\n"
205	        'id="$(basename "$PWD")"\n'
206	        f': > "{argv_dir}/$id.txt"\n'
207	        f'for a in "$@"; do printf "%s\\n" "$a" >> "{argv_dir}/$id.txt"; done\n'
208	        f'printf "%s\\n" "$id" >> "{done_marker}"\n'
209	    )
210	    script.chmod(0o755)
211	    return script
212	
213	
214	def _completed_identifiers(marker: Path) -> set[str]:
215	    if not marker.exists():
216	        return set()
217	    return {line for line in marker.read_text().splitlines() if line}
218	
219	
220	def _write_fake_claude_script(tmp_path: Path, done_marker: Path) -> Path:
221	    """A no-op stand-in for ``claude -p``.
222	
223	    Records the basename of its cwd (the issue identifier — orchestrator
224	    names worktrees ``.worktrees/<identifier>/``) into ``done_marker``.
225	    The stubbed ``get_issue`` reads this file to learn which issues the
226	    'agent' has completed.
227	    """
228	    script = tmp_path / "fake-claude.sh"
229	    script.write_text(
230	        "#!/bin/sh\n"
231	        f'printf "%s\\n" "$(basename "$PWD")" >> "{done_marker}"\n'
232	    )
233	    script.chmod(0o755)
234	    return script
235

  "old_string": "    cost_limit_usd: float | None,\n    passthrough: TextIO | None = None,\n    poll_interval_seconds: float = _POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS,\n) -> WorkerResult:\n    \"\"\"Spawn one streaming ``claude -p`` session and return its usage.\n\n    ``claude_cmd`` is the base argv (``[\"claude\", \"-p\",\n    \"--dangerously-skip-permissions\"]``); the streaming flags, ``--model``,\n    ``--max-budget-usd`` (when ``cost_limit_usd`` is set), and the prompt are\n    appended here. The session runs in its own process group; if its\n    cumulative tokens cross ``token_limit`` or its wall-clock crosses\n    ``time_limit_seconds`` the whole group is killed and the returned\n    ``WorkerResult.breach`` names the cap. A limit passed as ``None`` is not\n    enforced. ``cost_limit_usd`` is enforced by ``claude`` itself, not here.\n\n    Non-JSON lines on the merged stdout/stderr stream (claude warnings,\n    hook stderr) are written to ``passthrough`` (default ``sys.stderr``) so\n    the operator still sees diagnostics; recognised JSON events are\n    consumed by the usage parser.\n    \"\"\"\n    # The prompt is the trailing positional; it must follow the value-taking\n    # ``--model`` / ``--max-budget-usd`` options, not sit between an option\n    # and its value.\n    argv = [*claude_cmd, *_STREAM_FLAGS, \"--model\", model]\n    if cost_limit_usd is not None:\n        argv += [\"--max-budget-usd\", f\"{cost_limit_usd:g}\"]\n    argv.append(prompt)",
  "new_string": "    cost_limit_usd: float | None,\n    passthrough: TextIO | None = None,\n    debug_file: Path | None = None,\n    poll_interval_seconds: float = _POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS,\n) -> WorkerResult:\n    \"\"\"Spawn one streaming ``claude -p`` session and return its usage.\n\n    ``claude_cmd`` is the base argv (``[\"claude\", \"-p\",\n    \"--dangerously-skip-permissions\"]``); the streaming flags, ``--model``,\n    ``--max-budget-usd`` (when ``cost_limit_usd`` is set), and the prompt are\n    appended here. The session runs in its own process group; if its\n    cumulative tokens cross ``token_limit`` or its wall-clock crosses\n    ``time_limit_seconds`` the whole group is killed and the returned\n    ``WorkerResult.breach`` names the cap. A limit passed as ``None`` is not\n    enforced. ``cost_limit_usd`` is enforced by ``claude`` itself, not here.\n\n    Non-JSON lines on the merged stdout/stderr stream (claude warnings,\n    hook stderr) are written to ``passthrough`` (default ``sys.stderr``) so\n    the operator still sees diagnostics; recognised JSON events are\n    consumed by the usage parser.\n\n    ``debug_file``, when set, is passed to ``claude`` as ``--debug-file`` so\n    the session writes its startup diagnostics — which settings sources,\n    plugins, MCP servers, and hooks initialised — to that path. Debug logs\n    go to the file, not stderr, so the merged stream the usage parser reads\n    is unaffected. Opt-in only; ``None`` (the default) passes no flag and the\n    session behaves exactly as before. See ``docs/design-decisions.md`` §10.\n    \"\"\"\n    # The prompt is the trailing positional; it must follow the value-taking\n    # ``--model`` / ``--max-budget-usd`` / ``--debug-file`` options, not sit\n    # between an option and its value.\n    argv = [*claude_cmd, *_STREAM_FLAGS, \"--model\", model]\n    if cost_limit_usd is not None:\n        argv += [\"--max-budget-usd\", f\"{cost_limit_usd:g}\"]\n    if debug_file is not None:\n        argv += [\"--debug-file\", str(debug_file)]\n    argv.append(prompt)"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/orchestrator.py",
  "old_string": "def run(repos: Repos, limits: Limits | None = None) -> int:\n    if limits is None:\n        limits = Limits()\n    cycle_id = linear.current_cycle_id()\n    log = runlog.RunLog(cycle_id=cycle_id)\n    issues = linear.pending_issues(cycle_id)\n    if not issues:\n        print(f\"Cycle {cycle_id} has no Todo/Backlog issues — nothing to do.\")\n        return 0",
  "new_string": "def _debug_enabled() -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Whether per-issue ``--debug-file`` capture is switched on.\n\n    Read from the environment (``os.environ`` already carries any\n    ``~/.drain-cycle/.env`` value loaded at CLI startup), so the operator\n    turns it on for one investigative run with ``DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1\n    drain-cycle`` and leaves it off otherwise.\n    \"\"\"\n    return bool(os.environ.get(_DEBUG_ENV_VAR))\n\n\ndef run(repos: Repos, limits: Limits | None = None) -> int:\n    if limits is None:\n        limits = Limits()\n    debug = _debug_enabled()\n    cycle_id = linear.current_cycle_id()\n    log = runlog.RunLog(cycle_id=cycle_id)\n    issues = linear.pending_issues(cycle_id)\n    if not issues:\n        print(f\"Cycle {cycle_id} has no Todo/Backlog issues — nothing to do.\")\n        return 0"
}

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/docs/design-decisions.md",
  "old_string": "- *A separate hardcoded outer timeout kept alongside the configurable caps.* Rejected — two time concepts where one suffices (see above).",
  "new_string": "- *A separate hardcoded outer timeout kept alongside the configurable caps.* Rejected — two time concepts where one suffices (see above).\n\n## 10. Headless workers lose project-scoped hooks; the cause is named, not yet fixed\n\nThe operator noticed entire.io's checkpointing didn't take effect during a headless drain. The hypothesis was that the worker's worktree cwd (`.worktrees/<id>`) diverges from an interactive session at the repo root. A reproduction run confirmed the divergence and sharpened the cause — and this round names the constraint rather than fixing it (observability-first: no blind fix to hook/plugin loading).\n\n**What was observed (claude 2.1.150).** Running `claude -p --debug-file <path>` from the repo root vs. from a fresh `git worktree` of the same repo, then diffing the debug logs:\n\n| | Repo root | Worktree |\n|---|---|---|\n| Settings files watched | user `~/.claude/settings.json` + **project** `.claude/settings.json` + `.claude/settings.local.json` | **user only** |\n| Project `.claude/settings.json` | loaded | \"Broken symlink or missing file encountered\" |\n| entire.io hooks | SessionStart + SessionEnd fire (\"Entire CLI will link this conversation to your next commit\") | **absent — zero references** |\n| User-scoped plugins (crit, hookify, agent-skills, security-guidance) | \"Registered 7 hooks from 15 plugins\" | **identical: \"Registered 7 hooks from 15 plugins\"** |\n\n**The cause is project-scoped registration in a gitignored file — not cwd alone, and not plugins generally.** entire registers its hooks in the project-scoped `.claude/settings.json`. That file is gitignored (`.gitignore` ends with `.claude`). A `git worktree` is a fresh checkout of *tracked* files only, and git reports the worktree directory as its own `--show-toplevel`, so Claude Code resolves the project root to the worktree and finds no `.claude/` there. User-scoped plugins and MCP servers — registered under `~/.claude/` — are cwd-independent and load identically in both, which is why the symptom looked like \"some plugins\" rather than \"all hooks\": only the project-scoped ones drop out. The original hypothesis (worktree cwd) was right that cwd is involved, but the operative mechanism is the gitignored project-settings file, not cwd by itself; were `.claude/settings.json` tracked, the worktree checkout would carry it.\n\n**Reproduction step (one-shot).** From a target repo with project-scoped hooks registered in `.claude/settings.json`:\n\n```bash\n# Repo root — interactive-equivalent project root\nclaude -p --debug-file /tmp/root.debug.log --model claude-sonnet-4-6 \\\n  --max-budget-usd 0.50 \"Reply with exactly: ok\"\n\n# Fresh worktree — the worker's actual cwd\ngit worktree add -b repro .worktrees/repro main\n( cd .worktrees/repro && claude -p --debug-file /tmp/worktree.debug.log \\\n    --model claude-sonnet-4-6 --max-budget-usd 0.50 \"Reply with exactly: ok\" )\ngit worktree remove --force .worktrees/repro && git branch -D repro\n\n# Diff the loaded settings/hooks. The worktree run is missing the project\n# settings file and any hook registered in it.\ngrep -iE 'settings.json|Registered .* hooks|<your-plugin-name>' /tmp/root.debug.log\ngrep -iE 'settings.json|Registered .* hooks|<your-plugin-name>' /tmp/worktree.debug.log\n```\n\nThe same capture is wired into the worker as an opt-in: `DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1 drain-cycle` passes `--debug-file` to every spawned session, landing one `<run-log-stem>-<issue>.debug.log` per issue beside the run log in `~/.drain-cycle/runs/`. It is off by default — the diagnostic is for one-shot investigation, not steady-state overhead, and debug output goes to the file rather than stderr so the usage parser's stream is unaffected.\n\n**Open question — fix it, or accept it?** A fix would be cheap (any of: track `.claude/settings.json` so worktrees inherit it; register entire at the user scope; copy/symlink the repo's `.claude/` into each worktree at spawn; or pass `--settings <repo>/.claude/settings.json` to the worker). The reason none ships yet is upstream of the mechanism: it isn't obvious headless checkpointing is even *wanted*. entire's value is linking a conversation to the commit it produced — but a `drain-cycle` worktree is a throwaway branch removed on Done. Linking a session to a branch that's about to be deleted may be pointless or actively confusing. Until the operator decides whether project-scoped hooks *should* run inside throwaway worktrees, the divergence is a documented constraint, not a defect: workers run with user-scoped context only, and anything a target repo registers solely in its gitignored `.claude/` will not be present."
}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/README.md",
  "old_string": "Every invocation writes one JSON file: `~/.drain-cycle/runs/<cycle-id>-<run-timestamp>.json`. One entry per attempted issue, including timestamps, exit code, final Linear state, worktree path, per-issue token usage and cost, and `halt_reason` on the halting entry. The file also carries per-cycle totals (`cycle_tokens_cumulative`, `cycle_cost_usd`, `cycle_duration_seconds`) and a `cycle_halt_reason` when a cycle-wide cap stopped the run. Use it to gauge how cleanly your runs complete and what they cost — see [`drain_cycle/runlog.py`](drain_cycle/runlog.py) for the schema.\n\n## Design",
  "new_string": "Every invocation writes one JSON file: `~/.drain-cycle/runs/<cycle-id>-<run-timestamp>.json`. One entry per attempted issue, including timestamps, exit code, final Linear state, worktree path, per-issue token usage and cost, and `halt_reason` on the halting entry. The file also carries per-cycle totals (`cycle_tokens_cumulative`, `cycle_cost_usd`, `cycle_duration_seconds`) and a `cycle_halt_reason` when a cycle-wide cap stopped the run. Use it to gauge how cleanly your runs complete and what they cost — see [`drain_cycle/runlog.py`](drain_cycle/runlog.py) for the schema.\n\n### Debug capture\n\nWorkers spawn in an isolated worktree, whose environment differs from an interactive session at the repo root — most notably, **project-scoped hooks registered in a gitignored `.claude/settings.json` (e.g. entire.io's checkpointing) don't load inside the worktree.** [`docs/design-decisions.md`](docs/design-decisions.md) §10 documents why.\n\nTo see exactly which settings sources, plugins, MCP servers, and hooks a worker initialised, run with debug capture on:\n\n```bash\nDRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1 drain-cycle\n```\n\nEach spawned session then gets `claude`'s `--debug-file`, writing one `<cycle-id>-<run-timestamp>-<issue>.debug.log` per issue beside the run log in `~/.drain-cycle/runs/`. It is off by default — a one-shot investigative switch, not steady-state overhead. Debug output goes to the file, so it doesn't disturb the token-usage stream the run log records.\n\n## Design"
}
```

4	``cycle_id`` / ``cycle_duration_seconds`` / ``entries`` keys, and the
5	six required per-entry fields — without spinning up the orchestrator.
6	Integration of the runlog into ``orchestrator.run()`` is exercised
7	separately in ``test_orchestrator_runlog.py``.

146	        assert isinstance(entry["worktree_path"], str)
147	
148	    # Spot-check non-zero exit code and non-Done final state survived the
149	    # round-trip — those are the load-bearing fields for the halt path.
150	    assert second["exit_code"] == 1
151	    assert second["final_linear_state"] == "Todo"
152	
153	    # halt_reason round-trip: the Done entry's default-None is
154	    # persisted as JSON `null`, and the halt entry's string survives
155	    # verbatim — those are the on-disk shapes kill-condition tooling
156	    # reads.
157	    assert first["halt_reason"] is None
158	    assert second["halt_reason"] == (
159	        "Halt: ABA-Y (final state: Todo) at /tmp/repo/.worktrees/ABA-Y"
160	    )
161	    # The raw JSON text carries `null` (not "null"), so consumers that
162	    # treat the string "null" specially are not misled.
163	    assert '"halt_reason": null' in log.path.read_text()
164	
165	
166	def test_two_runlogs_same_cycle_id_write_to_distinct_files(
167	    tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch
168	) -> None:
169	    """Regression pin: distinct files per run on the same cycle.
170	
171	    Re-running ``drain-cycle`` against the same cycle (after fixing the
172	    cause of a mid-cycle halt) used to clobber the first run's on-disk
173	    artefact, silently losing every entry. With per-run filenames
174	    (``<cycle-id>-<run-timestamp>.json``), both runs survive on disk and
175	    downstream readers can merge across them by grouping on ``cycle_id``.
176	    """
177	    monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", str(tmp_path))
178	
179	    first = runlog.RunLog(cycle_id="re-run-cycle")
180	    first.append_entry(
181	        issue_identifier="ABA-1",
182	        started_at="2026-05-22T10:00:00+00:00",
183	        finished_at="2026-05-22T10:01:00+00:00",
184	        exit_code=0,
185	        final_linear_state="Done",
186	        worktree_path="/tmp/repo/.worktrees/ABA-1",
187	    )
188	
189	    second = runlog.RunLog(cycle_id="re-run-cycle")
190	    second.append_entry(
191	        issue_identifier="ABA-2",
192	        started_at="2026-05-22T11:00:00+00:00",
193	        finished_at="2026-05-22T11:02:00+00:00",
194	        exit_code=0,
195	        final_linear_state="Done",
196	        worktree_path="/tmp/repo/.worktrees/ABA-2",
197	    )
198	
199	    assert first.path != second.path
200	
201	    runs_dir = tmp_path / ".drain-cycle" / "runs"
202	    on_disk = sorted(p.name for p in runs_dir.glob("re-run-cycle-*.json"))
203	    assert len(on_disk) == 2
204	
205	    # First run's data survived — not clobbered by the second construction.
206	    first_payload = json.loads(first.path.read_text())
207	    assert [e["issue_identifier"] for e in first_payload["entries"]] == ["ABA-1"]
208	
209	    # Second run's file carries only its own entry.
210	    second_payload = json.loads(second.path.read_text())
211	    assert [e["issue_identifier"] for e in second_payload["entries"]] == ["ABA-2"]
212	
213	    # Both files share the cycle_id, so a downstream merger can group
214	    # them without reading the filename.
215	    assert first_payload["cycle_id"] == second_payload["cycle_id"] == "re-run-cycle"
216

121	def run_issue(
122	    *,
123	    claude_cmd: list[str],
124	    model: str,
125	    prompt: str,
126	    cwd: Path,
127	    token_limit: int | None,
128	    time_limit_seconds: float | None,
129	    cost_limit_usd: float | None,
130	    passthrough: TextIO | None = None,
131	    debug_file: Path | None = None,
132	    poll_interval_seconds: float = _POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS,
133	) -> WorkerResult:
134	    """Spawn one streaming ``claude -p`` session and return its usage.
135	
136	    ``claude_cmd`` is the base argv (``["claude", "-p",
137	    "--dangerously-skip-permissions"]``); the streaming flags, ``--model``,
138	    ``--max-budget-usd`` (when ``cost_limit_usd`` is set), and the prompt are
139	    appended here. The session runs in its own process group; if its
140	    cumulative tokens cross ``token_limit`` or its wall-clock crosses
141	    ``time_limit_seconds`` the whole group is killed and the returned
142	    ``WorkerResult.breach`` names the cap. A limit passed as ``None`` is not
143	    enforced. ``cost_limit_usd`` is enforced by ``claude`` itself, not here.
144	
145	    Non-JSON lines on the merged stdout/stderr stream (claude warnings,
146	    hook stderr) are written to ``passthrough`` (default ``sys.stderr``) so
147	    the operator still sees diagnostics; recognised JSON events are
148	    consumed by the usage parser.
149	
150	    ``debug_file``, when set, is passed to ``claude`` as ``--debug-file`` so
151	    the session writes its startup diagnostics — which settings sources,
152	    plugins, MCP servers, and hooks initialised — to that path. Debug logs
153	    go to the file, not stderr, so the merged stream the usage parser reads
154	    is unaffected. Opt-in only; ``None`` (the default) passes no flag and the
155	    session behaves exactly as before. See ``docs/design-decisions.md`` §10.
156	    """
157	    # The prompt is the trailing positional; it must follow the value-taking
158	    # ``--model`` / ``--max-budget-usd`` / ``--debug-file`` options, not sit
159	    # between an option and its value.
160	    argv = [*claude_cmd, *_STREAM_FLAGS, "--model", model]

165	    argv.append(prompt)

167	    accumulator = _UsageAccumulator()

```
154	## Logs & grading
155	
156	Every invocation writes one JSON file: `~/.drain-cycle/runs/<cycle-id>-<run-timestamp>.json`. One entry per attempted issue, including timestamps, exit code, final Linear state, worktree path, per-issue token usage and cost, and `halt_reason` on the halting entry. The file also carries per-cycle totals (`cycle_tokens_cumulative`, `cycle_cost_usd`, `cycle_duration_seconds`) and a `cycle_halt_reason` when a cycle-wide cap stopped the run. Use it to gauge how cleanly your runs complete and what they cost — see [`drain_cycle/runlog.py`](drain_cycle/runlog.py) for the schema.
157	
158	### Debug capture
159	
160	Workers spawn in an isolated worktree, whose environment differs from an interactive session at the repo root — most notably, **project-scoped hooks registered in a gitignored `.claude/settings.json` (e.g. entire.io's checkpointing) don't load inside the worktree.** [`docs/design-decisions.md`](docs/design-decisions.md) §10 documents why.
161	
162	To see exactly which settings sources, plugins, MCP servers, and hooks a worker initialised, run with debug capture on:
163	
164	```bash
165	DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1 drain-cycle
166	```
167	
168	Each spawned session then gets `claude`'s `--debug-file`, writing one `<cycle-id>-<run-timestamp>-<issue>.debug.log` per issue beside the run log in `~/.drain-cycle/runs/`. It is off by default — a one-shot investigative switch, not steady-state overhead. Debug output goes to the file, so it doesn't disturb the token-usage stream the run log records.
169	
170	## Design
171	
```

> AGENT

**Review verdict: Approve.** Five-axis summary of my own change (no external reviewer available; this is the self-review the workflow requires):

- **Correctness:** `--debug-file` is inserted as a value-taking option before the trailing positional prompt; debug output goes to the file (confirmed empty stderr in the live reproduction), so the usage parser's merged stream is untouched. Path naming and on/off gating verified by 5 new tests. No findings.
- **Readability:** Names and docstrings cross-reference design-decisions §10; the worktree comment lists `--debug-file` alongside the other value-taking flags. No findings.
- **Architecture:** Additive `debug_file` param (default `None`, backward-compatible); `debug_path` co-located on `RunLog` where path conventions live; env-var opt-in consistent with decision 9's rejection of CLI-flag plumbing. **FYI:** the orchestrator now reads `os.environ` directly — a small new responsibility, but it's a runtime diagnostic toggle (not config), documented, and mirrors the lazy `Path.home()` reads elsewhere.
- **Security:** Debug captures are operator-local in `~/.drain-cycle/runs/`; added a README note that they're verbose and not auto-pruned. No secrets logged to code/VCS.
- **Performance:** Negligible — one env read per run, one extra file per opted-in issue.

No Critical/Required findings. Now committing and pushing to main.

ABA-238 is **Done** — committed `6c70a3e`, pushed to main, Linear updated.

## What I found

The reproduction (the third acceptance criterion) confirmed the hypothesis and sharpened it. Running `claude -p --debug-file` from the repo root vs. a fresh worktree and diffing the debug logs:

- **Repo root:** project `.claude/settings.json` loads; entire.io's SessionStart/SessionEnd hooks fire.
- **Worktree:** "Broken symlink or missing file" for `.claude/settings.json`; **zero** entire references.
- **User-scoped plugins** (crit, hookify, agent-skills, security-guidance) load **identically** in both.

**Root cause:** not cwd alone, and not plugins generally — entire registers its hooks in the project-scoped `.claude/settings.json`, which is **gitignored** (`.gitignore` ends with `.claude`). A worktree is a fresh checkout of tracked files only, and git reports the worktree as its own toplevel, so Claude Code finds no project settings there. Only project-scoped hooks drop out; user-scoped plugins/MCP are unaffected — which is why the symptom looked like "some plugins."

## What I shipped (observability-first, no fix to hook loading)

- `worker.run_issue` — opt-in `debug_file` param → `claude --debug-file`; debug goes to the file, not stderr, so the usage stream is untouched (verified live: stderr stayed empty).
- Orchestrator gates on `DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1`, writes one `<run-log-stem>-<issue>.debug.log` per issue beside the run log; `RunLog.debug_path` owns the naming.
- `docs/design-decisions.md` §10 — the constraint, reproduction step, evidence table, and the real open question: *fix it, or accept that throwaway worktrees probably shouldn't run project hooks at all* (entire links a conversation to a commit, but a `drain-cycle` branch is deleted on Done).
- `README.md` — debug-capture section.

All 125 tests pass (5 new). The fix itself is deliberately deferred — the open question is upstream of the mechanism, and it's yours to decide.

```
Good, so passthrough defaults to None in the orchestrator call, which means it defaults to sys.stderr. Now let me create a comprehensive summary document by collecting all the information I've found:

Based on my systematic exploration of the drain-cycle repository, here is the precise trace of how a worker session is spawned inside a git worktree:

## 1. Worktree Lifecycle

**Location:** `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/worktree.py`

**Worktree creation (`add` function, lines 21–31):**
- **Git command:** `git worktree add -b <identifier> <worktree_path> main`
  - `-b <identifier>` creates a new branch named after the issue identifier
  - `<worktree_path>` resolves to `<repo>/.worktrees/<identifier>/` (line 26)
  - Base ref is hardcoded as `main` (line 17: `BASE_BRANCH = "main"`)
- **Path computation (line 26):** `worktree_path = repo / WORKTREE_DIR / identifier` where `WORKTREE_DIR = ".worktrees"` (line 18)
- **Removal (`remove` function, lines 34–36):** `git worktree remove <worktree_path>` is called only on success (orchestrator.py:246, inside the `is_done` branch). On halt (not-Done, breach, or setup failure), the worktree is left in place on disk.
- **Setup steps before worker spawn:** None. The worktree is created, Linear state is transitioned to "In Progress" (orchestrator.py:152), then the prompt is built (orchestrator.py:176) and worker is spawned immediately without any additional file copies or env setup.

**Worktree command details (worktree.py:27–30):**
```python
_run_git(
    ["worktree", "add", "-b", identifier, str(worktree_path), BASE_BRANCH],
    cwd=repo,
)
```

## 2. Worker Spawn

**Location:** `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/worker.py`, `run_issue()` function (lines 121–211)

**Full argv construction (lines 160–165):**
```python
argv = [*claude_cmd, *_STREAM_FLAGS, "--model", model]
if cost_limit_usd is not None:
    argv += ["--max-budget-usd", f"{cost_limit_usd:g}"]
if debug_file is not None:
    argv += ["--debug-file", str(debug_file)]
argv.append(prompt)
```

**Constants:**
- `_STREAM_FLAGS = ["--verbose", "--output-format", "stream-json"]` (line 68) — required for line-delimited JSON event streaming
- `_CLAUDE_CMD = ["claude", "-p", "--dangerously-skip-permissions"]` (orchestrator.py:22)

**Exact argv order (example without optional flags):**
```
["claude", "-p", "--dangerously-skip-permissions", "--verbose", "--output-format", "stream-json", "--model", "<model>", "<prompt>"]
```

With optional flags (cost_limit_usd and debug_file):
```
["claude", "-p", "--dangerously-skip-permissions", "--verbose", "--output-format", "stream-json", "--model", "<model>", "--max-budget-usd", "0.5", "--debug-file", "/path/to/debug.log", "<prompt>"]
```

**Subprocess invocation (lines 170–178):**
```python
proc = subprocess.Popen(
    argv,
    cwd=str(cwd),
    stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
    stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
    text=True,
    bufsize=1,
    start_new_session=True,
)
```

**Key subprocess parameters:**
- `cwd` — the worktree path (passed from orchestrator.py:190)
- `stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT` — merged stdout/stderr piped to reader thread
- `text=True, bufsize=1` — text mode, line-buffered
- `start_new_session=True` — worker leads its own process group for group-kill on breach
- **No `env` parameter** — process inherits parent's `os.environ` unchanged (no curated/filtered environment passed)

**Process lifecycle:**
- Reader thread spawned immediately (lines 179–184) to drain merged output as JSON events
- Monitor thread polls token/time caps every 1 second (line 192, with `poll_interval_seconds` defaulting to `_POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS = 1.0`, line 81)
- On breach, entire process group is killed via `os.killpg(pgid, signal.SIGKILL)` (worker.py:262)
- Reader joined with 5-second timeout (line 197, `_READER_JOIN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 5.0`)

## 3. Orchestrator Wiring

**Location:** `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/orchestrator.py`, `run()` function (lines 108–295)

**Order of operations per issue (lines 119–292):**

1. **Issue retrieval (lines 119–121):** Fetch next issue from Linear
2. **Repo resolution (lines 124–145):** Resolve issue to target repo via `repo:<name>` label; on failure, halt pre-spawn with no worktree
3. **Worktree creation (line 148):** `worktree_path = worktree.add(target_repo, identifier)`
4. **Linear state transition (line 152):** `linear.set_state(issue["id"], "In Progress")`
5. **Prompt and model resolution (lines 176–177):**
   - `agent_prompt = prompt.build(issue, worktree_path)`
   - `worker_model = model.resolve(issue)`
6. **Optional debug-file setup (lines 179–184):** If `DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG` env var is set, compute debug path and pass to worker
7. **Worker spawn (lines 186–195):**
   ```python
   result = worker.run_issue(
       claude_cmd=_CLAUDE_CMD,
       model=worker_model,
       prompt=agent_prompt,
       cwd=worktree_path,
       token_limit=limits.per_issue_tokens,
       time_limit_seconds=limits.per_issue_seconds,
       cost_limit_usd=limits.per_issue_cost_usd,
       debug_file=debug_file,
   )
   ```
8. **Post-spawn state checks (lines 230–292):**
   - If breach: revert Linear state, halt, leave worktree
   - If done: log entry, remove worktree, check cycle-wide caps, continue
   - If not-done: revert Linear state, halt, leave worktree

**Arguments flowing from orchestrator → worker:**
- `claude_cmd` — constant `_CLAUDE_CMD`
- `model` — resolved per issue (from `model.resolve(issue)`)
- `prompt` — built per issue and worktree (from `prompt.build(issue, worktree_path)`)
- `cwd` — the worktree path
- `token_limit` — from `limits.per_issue_tokens`
- `time_limit_seconds` — from `limits.per_issue_seconds`
- `cost_limit_usd` — from `limits.per_issue_cost_usd`
- `debug_file` — computed per issue if `DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG` is set
- `passthrough` — omitted (defaults to `sys.stderr` in worker)

## 4. Config/Secrets Loading

**Location:** `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/cli.py`, `_load_secrets()` function (lines 33–43)

**Loading order (first hit wins, `override=False`):**
1. `load_dotenv(Path.home() / ".drain-cycle" / ".env")` (line 42)
2. `load_dotenv(_REPO_ENV)` where `_REPO_ENV = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent / ".env"` (lines 30, 43)

These are called from `main()` at line 54, before any module reads `os.environ`.

**Environment inheritance chain:**
- CLI loads secrets into `os.environ` via `_load_secrets()` (cli.py:54)
- Orchestrator reads debug flag from `os.environ.get("DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG")` (orchestrator.py:105)
- Worker subprocess **inherits parent's `os.environ` unchanged** — no explicit `env` dict passed to `subprocess.Popen` (worker.py:170)
- Linear module reads API key from `os.environ.get("LINEAR_API_KEY")` at CLI time (happens during `linear.pending_issues()` call in orchestrator.py:114)

**Critical gap:** The worker process inherits the orchestrator's `os.environ`, which includes secrets loaded at CLI startup. **However, there is no mechanism to pass project-root `.env` values into the worktree session if they were not already in the shell environment or `~/.drain-cycle/.env` at CLI startup.** If a `.env` exists only at the repo root, the orchestrator sees it, but the worker in a worktree does not have a copy of it unless explicitly copied.

## Known Design Constraint (from docs/design-decisions.md §10)

The worktree's Claude Code session sees **only user-scoped settings** (`~/.claude/settings.json`), not project-scoped settings (`.claude/settings.json`), because:
- Git worktrees check out only tracked files
- `.claude/` is gitignored
- Claude Code resolves the project root to the worktree's `--show-toplevel`, finds no `.claude/` there
- Project-scoped hooks (e.g., entire.io) do not run in the worktree; user-scoped plugins run identically

No fix has shipped because the desired behavior (whether headless sessions should trigger project-scoped hooks in throwaway branches) is unresolved. Workarounds noted: track `.claude/settings.json`, register at user scope, copy `.claude/` to worktree, or pass `--settings` flag.

Perfect! Now I have a complete picture. Let me create a comprehensive report with all the findings:

## Exploration Report: drain-cycle Worktree Settings/Hooks Loading Issue

I've thoroughly investigated the drain-cycle repository to understand how project-scoped settings and hooks are loaded in headless workers spawned in git worktrees. Here are my findings:

### 1. **Design Decision 10 — Complete Text** (`/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/docs/design-decisions.md`, lines 125–162)

**Full decision text:**

> ## 10. Headless workers lose project-scoped hooks; the cause is named, not yet fixed
> 
> The operator noticed entire.io's checkpointing didn't take effect during a headless drain. The hypothesis was that the worker's worktree cwd (`.worktrees/<id>`) diverges from an interactive session at the repo root. A reproduction run confirmed the divergence and sharpened the cause — and this round names the constraint rather than fixing it (observability-first: no blind fix to hook/plugin loading).
> 
> **What was observed (claude 2.1.150).** Running `claude -p --debug-file <path>` from the repo root vs. from a fresh `git worktree` of the same repo, then diffing the debug logs:
> 
> | | Repo root | Worktree |
> |---|---|---|
> | Settings files watched | user `~/.claude/settings.json` + **project** `.claude/settings.json` + `.claude/settings.local.json` | **user only** |
> | Project `.claude/settings.json` | loaded | "Broken symlink or missing file encountered" |
> | entire.io hooks | SessionStart + SessionEnd fire ("Entire CLI will link this conversation to your next commit") | **absent — zero references** |
> | User-scoped plugins (crit, hookify, agent-skills, security-guidance) | "Registered 7 hooks from 15 plugins" | **identical: "Registered 7 hooks from 15 plugins"** |
> 
> **The cause is project-scoped registration in a gitignored file — not cwd alone, and not plugins generally.** entire registers its hooks in the project-scoped `.claude/settings.json`. That file is gitignored (`.gitignore` ends with `.claude`). A `git worktree` is a fresh checkout of *tracked* files only, and git reports the worktree directory as its own `--show-toplevel`, so Claude Code resolves the project root to the worktree and finds no `.claude/` there. User-scoped plugins and MCP servers — registered under `~/.claude/` — are cwd-independent and load identically in both, which is why the symptom looked like "some plugins" rather than "all hooks": only the project-scoped ones drop out. The original hypothesis (worktree cwd) was right that cwd is involved, but the operative mechanism is the gitignored project-settings file, not cwd by itself; were `.claude/settings.json` tracked, the worktree checkout would carry it.
> 
> **Reproduction step (one-shot).** From a target repo with project-scoped hooks registered in `.claude/settings.json`:
> 
> ```bash
> # Repo root — interactive-equivalent project root
> claude -p --debug-file /tmp/root.debug.log --model claude-sonnet-4-6 \
>   --max-budget-usd 0.50 "Reply with exactly: ok"
> 
> # Fresh worktree — the worker's actual cwd
> git worktree add -b repro .worktrees/repro main
> ( cd .worktrees/repro && claude -p --debug-file /tmp/worktree.debug.log \
>     --model claude-sonnet-4-6 --max-budget-usd 0.50 "Reply with exactly: ok" )
> git worktree remove --force .worktrees/repro && git branch -D repro
> 
> # Diff the loaded settings/hooks. The worktree run is missing the project
> # settings file and any hook registered in it.
> grep -iE 'settings.json|Registered .* hooks|<your-plugin-name>' /tmp/root.debug.log
> grep -iE 'settings.json|Registered .* hooks|<your-plugin-name>' /tmp/worktree.debug.log
> ```
> 
> The same capture is wired into the worker as an opt-in: `DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1 drain-cycle` passes `--debug-file` to every spawned session, landing one `<run-log-stem>-<issue>.debug.log` per issue beside the run log in `~/.drain-cycle/runs/`. It is off by default — the diagnostic is for one-shot investigation, not steady-state overhead, and debug output goes to the file rather than stderr so the usage parser's stream is unaffected.
> 
> **Open question — fix it, or accept it?** A fix would be cheap (any of: track `.claude/settings.json` so worktrees inherit it; register entire at the user scope; copy/symlink the repo's `.claude/` into each worktree at spawn; or pass `--settings <repo>/.claude/settings.json` to the worker). The reason none ships yet is upstream of the mechanism: it isn't obvious headless checkpointing is even *wanted*. entire's value is linking a conversation to the commit it produced — but a `drain-cycle` worktree is a throwaway branch removed on Done. Linking a session to a branch that's about to be deleted may be pointless or actively confusing. Until the operator decides whether project-scoped hooks *should* run inside throwaway worktrees, the divergence is a documented constraint, not a defect: workers run with user-scoped context only, and anything a target repo registers solely in its gitignored `.claude/` will not be present.

**Key elements extracted:**
- **Cause explanation** (lines 138–139): "entire registers its hooks in the project-scoped `.claude/settings.json`. That file is gitignored (`.gitignore` ends with `.claude`). A `git worktree` is a fresh checkout of *tracked* files only, and git reports the worktree directory as its own `--show-toplevel`, so Claude Code resolves the project root to the worktree and finds no `.claude/` there."
- **Reproduction snippet** (lines 140–157): Copy-paste ready bash with `git worktree add`, two `claude -p` runs, and grep diff commands
- **Listed fix options** (line 161): "track `.claude/settings.json` so worktrees inherit it; register entire at the user scope; copy/symlink the repo's `.claude/` into each worktree at spawn; or pass `--settings <repo>/.claude/settings.json` to the worker"
- **Open question** (lines 159–162): Whether project-scoped hooks should run in throwaway worktrees at all, given the branches are deleted on Done

### 4. **File-Copy or Worktree-Prep Helpers — None Exist**

**Search results:**
- No utility function or helper script copies `.claude/` files into worktrees
- No code references `--settings` flag to override settings path
- No symlink or copy logic in worktree creation path

**Worktree creation code** (`/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/worktree.py`, lines 21–31):
```python
def add(repo: Path, identifier: str) -> Path:
    """Create a worktree branched off ``main`` for ``identifier``."""
    worktree_path = repo / WORKTREE_DIR / identifier
    _run_git(
        ["worktree", "add", "-b", identifier, str(worktree_path), BASE_BRANCH],
        cwd=repo,
    )
    return worktree_path
```
- **Only action**: Calls `git worktree add` with no post-creation setup
- **No file prep**: Returns the path immediately; no copy/symlink/chmod steps follow

**Worker spawn code** (`/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/orchestrator.py`, lines 148–195):
- Line 148: `worktree_path = worktree.add(target_repo, identifier)` — creates worktree
- Lines 176–195: `worker.run_issue(...)` spawned with `cwd=worktree_path` — runs in worktree
- **No settings prep** between these two steps

**Spawn mechanism** (`/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/worker.py`, lines 170–178):
```python
proc = subprocess.Popen(
    argv,
    cwd=str(cwd),  # cwd is the worktree_path
    stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
    stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
    text=True,
    bufsize=1,
    start_new_session=True,
)
```
- Sets `cwd` to the worktree but passes no special `--settings` or environment variables to `claude`
- No Claude-specific setup in the parent process
