# Implementation Plan: Worktree Config Parity for drain-cycle Workers

## Summary of the problem (confirmed by exploration)

`git worktree add` checks out only **tracked** files. In `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle`, `.gitignore` line 24 is a blanket `.claude`, and `git ls-files .claude` returns nothing — so the entire project-scoped Claude config is invisible to any worktree. The worker spawned by `drain_cycle/worker.py:run_issue()` runs with `cwd=<repo>/.worktrees/<id>/`; Claude Code resolves the project root from git toplevel (the worktree), finds no `.claude/`, and loads user-scoped config only. Project hooks (entire.io's checkpointing in `.claude/settings.json`) silently never fire. This is documented as an open question in `docs/design-decisions.md` §10.

I confirmed: no `.mcp.json` exists here; `.gitignore` itself **is** tracked, so it carries into every worktree checkout — which is load-bearing for the teardown/`git add` safety reasoning below.

---

## 1. Mechanism recommendation: replicate, don't `--settings`

**Recommended: replicate the source repo's gitignored project config into the worktree at spawn time (symlink by default).** Reject the `--settings`-only path as the primary mechanism.

### Why `--settings` alone is insufficient

The `claude` CLI's `--settings <file-or-json>` loads exactly **one** additional settings file. There is **no flag to relocate the project root** — project scope always resolves from git toplevel = the worktree cwd. So `--settings <repo>/.claude/settings.json` patches in one file's worth of `hooks`/`permissions`/`env`, but leaves these gaps:

- **`settings.local.json`** is not loaded (it's a separate project-local file; only one `--settings` arg). Moot here under `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, but a real divergence in general.
- **`.claude/agents/`, `.claude/skills/`, `.claude/commands/`** — project-scoped subagents, skills and slash commands are discovered by directory walk from the project root, not from a settings file. The prompt in `prompt.py` literally instructs the agent to run `/code-review-and-quality`; if that command is project-scoped it won't resolve. (In this repo it's plugin-provided, so it survives — but the parity goal is general, and project-scoped commands are a supported pattern.) `.claude/agents/entire-search.md` exists here and would be lost.
- **`$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`-relative hook scripts** — hooks commonly invoke `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR/.claude/hooks/foo.sh`. With `--settings` pointing at the real file but cwd in the worktree, `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` resolves to the worktree, and the relative script path doesn't exist there. The hook entry loads but its command can't find its script. (entire's hooks shell out to `entire` on `PATH`, so they dodge this — but again, general parity.)
- **`.mcp.json`** — needs a *separate* `--mcp-config` flag (plus `--strict-mcp-config` semantics to reason about). Two flags, two failure surfaces, and ordering/merge rules to get right.

So `--settings` is a partial patch covering one of five config surfaces, and even that one surface has a relative-path failure mode. Replicating the directory makes the worktree look like the repo root to *every* discovery mechanism at once — settings, local settings, agents, skills, commands, hook scripts, and (with `.mcp.json` placed alongside) MCP. That is what "comprehensive" means here: one operation restores parity for all surfaces instead of one flag per surface.

A pragmatic note for the implementer: replication and `--settings` are **not mutually exclusive**. The plan below is replication-only because it's sufficient and simpler. If a future repo tracks part of `.claude/` and gitignores the rest, replication still handles it (see edge cases). Keep `--settings` in the back pocket only if a repo's hooks turn out to hard-code absolute paths that must point back at the real repo — not expected here.

---

## 2. The replication approach — concrete design

### 2a. Which paths to replicate (fixed default set)

A module-level constant naming the project-scoped config entries to replicate from `<target_repo>` into `<worktree>`:

```
_PROJECT_CONFIG_PATHS = (".claude", ".mcp.json")
```

Rationale for this exact set:
- `.claude/` (whole directory) covers `settings.json`, `settings.local.json`, `agents/`, `skills/`, `commands/`, `hooks/` in one entry — the comprehensive win.
- `.mcp.json` is the only top-level project config file outside `.claude/`. It doesn't exist in this repo, so it's a clean no-op here, but including it makes the set correct for repos that have it.

Deliberately **excluded** from the default set: `.entire/` (entire's stateful checkpoint dir). That's a design fork — see §3(ii). The boring default is generic config only.

### 2b. Copy vs symlink — recommend **symlink the top-level entries**

For each name in `_PROJECT_CONFIG_PATHS` that exists in the source repo and does **not** already exist in the worktree, create a **symlink** in the worktree pointing at the absolute path in the source repo:

```
<worktree>/.claude  ->  <target_repo>/.claude
<worktree>/.mcp.json -> <target_repo>/.mcp.json   (only if it exists)
```

Why symlink as the default:
- **Live shared state.** Stateful hooks like entire write into `<repo>/.entire/`. The hook *scripts and registration* live behind `<repo>/.claude/`; with a symlink, the worker reads the real settings and the hooks run against the real repo paths. A copy would let hooks *register* but, if any hook resolves `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR/.claude/...` and writes there, those writes land in the throwaway worktree copy and vanish on teardown. (This is the central tradeoff — see §3(i).)
- **Cheap and atomic.** One `os.symlink` per entry; no recursive tree copy of a directory that may contain large `skills/` trees.
- **No drift.** If the operator edits `.claude/settings.json` mid-cycle, every subsequent worker sees it.

Symlink at the **top-level entry** granularity (link `.claude` itself), not per-file inside it — so newly added agents/skills/hooks in the source automatically appear, and there's exactly one link to create and one teardown concern per entry.

### 2c. Where this hooks into the code

Add a new function in `drain_cycle/worktree.py` (it already owns the worktree lifecycle and the "no post-creation setup exists" gap is exactly here):

```python
# worktree.py
PROJECT_CONFIG_PATHS = (".claude", ".mcp.json")

def link_project_config(repo: Path, worktree_path: Path) -> list[Path]:
    """Symlink gitignored project-scoped Claude config from `repo` into
    `worktree_path` so a worker has the same project settings/hooks/agents/
    skills/MCP as an interactive session at the repo root. Returns the links
    created (for logging/teardown reasoning). No-op for entries absent in
    `repo` or already present in the worktree (tracked-and-checked-out)."""
```

Behavior:
- For each name in `PROJECT_CONFIG_PATHS`: if `(repo / name)` exists **and** `(worktree_path / name)` does **not** already exist (`os.path.lexists`, so a tracked file git already checked out is never clobbered), create `os.symlink(repo / name, worktree_path / name)`.
- Resolve `repo` to an absolute path first so the symlink target is absolute and survives the worker's `cwd`.

Call it from `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py` `run()` immediately after the worktree is created and before the worker is spawned — inside the existing pre-spawn `try` (orchestrator.py ~line 147-152), right after `worktree.add(...)`:

```python
worktree_path = worktree.add(target_repo, identifier)
worktree.link_project_config(target_repo, worktree_path)   # NEW
linear.set_state(issue["id"], _IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME)
```

Placing it inside that `try` means any failure (e.g. a permission error creating the link) is converted into the existing recorded "setup failed" halt with a run-log entry and the planned worktree path (orchestrator.py lines ~153-174) — no new error-handling branch needed, and consistent with how `worktree.add` failures are already surfaced. This reuses the established failure contract rather than inventing one.

Do **not** create a new module. `worktree.py` is the natural home; a new module would split worktree lifecycle across two files for no benefit. The function is small and the existing module docstring already frames worktree setup/teardown.

### 2d. Teardown interaction

On Done, the orchestrator calls `worktree.remove(target_repo, worktree_path)` (orchestrator.py line ~246), which runs `git worktree remove`. With **symlinks**, this is safe and clean:
- `git worktree remove` deletes the worktree directory and its contents. Removing a **symlink** deletes the link, **not** the target — the real `<repo>/.claude` and `<repo>/.entire` are untouched.
- No new teardown code is needed. The symlinks live inside the worktree dir and are reaped wholesale with it.
- One caveat to verify in implementation: `git worktree remove` refuses if the worktree is "dirty" (untracked/modified files). The symlinks are gitignored (see §2e), so they don't count as dirty. But the orchestrator already faces this for any files the worker leaves behind on a non-Done path; on Done the worker is expected to have committed. The symlinks specifically do **not** add a dirtiness risk because they're ignored. If `git worktree remove` ever needs `--force`, that's a pre-existing concern, not introduced here. (On the halt path the worktree is intentionally left in place for inspection — the symlinks remain, still pointing at live config, which is fine and arguably useful for the operator inspecting the halted worktree.)

If the design fork lands on **copy** instead (§3(i)), teardown is still automatic (the copy is inside the worktree dir), but the copy discards any hook-written state — which is the whole point of the fork.

### 2e. Does gitignore protect the replicated paths from the worker's `git add`?

**Yes — confirmed by the repo state.** Reasoning chain:
1. `.gitignore` is a **tracked** file (`git ls-files .gitignore` returns it), so it is checked out into every worktree by `git worktree add`.
2. That `.gitignore` contains blanket `.claude` (line 24) and `.entire` (line 23). Worktrees share the same ignore rules.
3. Therefore a `.claude` symlink (or copy) inside the worktree matches the ignore pattern and is invisible to `git status` / `git add .` run by the worker from the worktree cwd.
4. `.mcp.json` is **not** currently in `.gitignore`. In this repo it doesn't exist so it's moot. **But this is a real edge case for other repos**: if a target repo has a tracked-and-committed `.mcp.json`, git already checks it out and `link_project_config` skips it (lexists guard) — no symlink, no problem. If a repo has a gitignored `.mcp.json`, the symlink is correctly ignored. The only gap is a repo with an **untracked, non-ignored** `.mcp.json` at root — then a worker `git add .` could stage the symlink. This is unusual; the conservative mitigation is to document that project MCP config should be either tracked or gitignored, OR (heavier) have `link_project_config` skip any entry that isn't ignored in the worktree. Recommend documenting rather than coding the check, to keep the function boring. Flag for the operator to decide.

The protection is therefore **inherited from the source repo's existing ignore rules**, not something the new code must establish. The new code relies on it; it doesn't create it.

---

## 3. Genuine design forks the operator must decide

These are the real decisions. I recommend a default for each but they're judgment calls.

### (i) Copy vs symlink — isolation/ephemerality vs. live shared state
- **Symlink (recommended default):** worker shares the operator's real `.claude/` and (if linked) `.entire/`. Stateful hooks persist their checkpoints into the real dirs, surviving teardown. Cost: the worker can **mutate the operator's real config** — e.g. a misbehaving hook or the agent itself writing to `.claude/settings.local.json` changes the operator's live file. Under `--dangerously-skip-permissions` the agent has unrestricted write access, so this is a real (if low-probability) blast-radius concern.
- **Copy:** worker gets an isolated snapshot; teardown discards everything including hook-written state. entire's checkpoints written into a copied `.entire/` evaporate on Done — which defeats entire's purpose (linking a conversation to a commit). But it guarantees the worker can't corrupt the operator's real config.
- **The deciding question is the one §10 already raises:** is headless checkpointing even *wanted* for a throwaway branch removed on Done? If yes → symlink + replicate `.entire/`. If the operator only wants *hooks to fire* (so agents/skills/commands resolve) but doesn't care about persisting entire's state → copy is cleaner and safer. **My recommendation: symlink**, because it's the only option that delivers true parity, and the mutation risk is bounded (the agent works in a worktree branch; config corruption is recoverable from git for tracked files, and `.claude` is gitignored so the operator's copy is their own working state anyway).

### (ii) Replicate `.entire/` too, or only generic `.claude/` + `.mcp.json`?
- entire keeps state in `<repo>/.entire/`. If hooks fire (symlinked `.claude/`) but `.entire/` is absent in the worktree, entire's `session-start`/`stop` hooks run from cwd=worktree and look for `.entire/` relative to `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` = worktree → not found → they no-op or error. So **to actually make entire work**, `.entire` must also be in the replicated set.
- This is **entire-specific**, which is the argument *against* hardcoding it: drain-cycle's other modules (repos.py, prompt.py) are tool-agnostic. Putting `.entire` in a generic constant couples drain-cycle to one vendor.
- **Recommendation:** keep the default set generic (`.claude`, `.mcp.json`) and treat `.entire` as the operator's call. If they want entire to work end-to-end, the cleanest expression is to add `.entire` to the constant (it's a one-line edit) — which segues into fork (iii).

### (iii) Fixed constant vs. configurable
- The codebase **deliberately avoids argparse** (design decision 9) and uses YAML config (`repos.yml`, `limits.yml`, with `.example.yml` siblings). So if this were configurable it'd be a YAML key, not a flag.
- **Recommendation: fixed module-level constant** (`worktree.PROJECT_CONFIG_PATHS`). It's the boring default, it's trivially overridable in code by an operator who forks, and it avoids adding a new config-file surface (with its own load/validate/error-path machinery — see how much `repos.py` spends on `RepoConfigError`) for a list that almost nobody will change. If demand for per-repo config sets emerges, promote it to `repos.yml` later (it's already per-repo-keyed). YAGNI says start with the constant.

---

## 4. Test strategy (fits existing patterns)

The existing pattern is **real git tmp repo + fake `claude` shell script + monkeypatched Linear**, and the key constraint is to verify config *would load* by asserting **file/link presence and argv**, never by firing real hooks or spending tokens. Three layers:

### Layer 1 — `tests/test_worktree.py` (unit, the new function)
Reuse the existing `_init_repo(repo)` helper (real `git init -b main` + seed commit). Add tests:

1. **`test_link_project_config_symlinks_gitignored_claude`**: in the seed repo, create `<repo>/.claude/settings.json` with content, and add `.claude` to the repo's `.gitignore` (and commit the gitignore). `worktree.add(repo, "ABA-1")` then `worktree.link_project_config(repo, wt)`. Assert `(wt/".claude").is_symlink()`, `os.readlink` resolves to `repo/".claude"`, and `(wt/".claude/settings.json").read_text()` round-trips the content through the link.
2. **`test_link_project_config_noop_when_no_claude`**: seed repo with no `.claude/`. Call the function; assert no link created, no error (clean no-op — edge case from §5).
3. **`test_link_project_config_skips_existing_tracked_entry`**: commit a `.claude/settings.json` as a *tracked* file (partially-tracked repo). After `worktree.add`, git has already checked out the real file. Call `link_project_config`; assert `(wt/".claude")` is **not** a symlink (the lexists guard left git's checkout alone) and the tracked content is intact. This pins the don't-clobber-tracked-files edge case.
4. **`test_link_project_config_includes_mcp_json_when_present`**: create gitignored `<repo>/.mcp.json`; assert it's symlinked. And the inverse: absent `.mcp.json` → no link, no error.
5. **`test_linked_config_is_gitignored_in_worktree`** (the safety property): with `.claude` gitignored and symlinked into the worktree, run `git status --porcelain` (or `git check-ignore`) from the worktree cwd and assert `.claude` does **not** appear as untracked — proving the worker's `git add .` won't stage it. This directly tests the §2e reasoning.
6. **(If symlink chosen) `test_remove_preserves_symlink_target`**: create the link, `worktree.remove`, then assert `<repo>/.claude` and its contents **still exist** — i.e. removing the worktree didn't follow the link and delete the operator's real config. This pins the teardown safety claim in §2d.

### Layer 2 — `tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py` (integration, the wiring)
Follow the `_captured_argv_for_one_issue` / fake-claude pattern. Modify `_init_repo` (or add a variant) to seed a gitignored `.claude/settings.json` in the test repo. Add:

7. **`test_orchestrator_links_project_config_into_worktree`**: use a fake `claude` script that, instead of (or in addition to) marking done, records whether `.claude/settings.json` is **readable from its own cwd** (e.g. `[ -r .claude/settings.json ] && echo present >> marker`). Run a one-issue cycle. Assert the marker says `present` — i.e. by the time the worker runs, the worktree cwd resolves the project config. This verifies the orchestrator calls the new step at the right point **without any real claude tokens** (the fake script just stats a file).

This is the strongest "hooks would load" signal achievable cheaply: project hooks load iff `.claude/settings.json` is resolvable from the worker cwd, and the fake script proves resolvability. We assert the *precondition* for hook loading, not live firing.

### Layer 3 — regression guard on existing tests
The existing iteration/argv tests use repos with **no** `.claude/`, so `link_project_config` is a no-op for them (edge case 1) and they must still pass unchanged. Run the full suite to confirm. The debug-capture argv tests (`test_orchestrator_debug_capture_*`) assert exact argv; since this change touches `worktree.py`/orchestrator wiring and **not** the worker argv, those should be unaffected — confirm they still pass.

**Token-spend guardrail:** no test invokes the real `claude` binary; all use the fake shell scripts already established. The "would hooks load" question is answered by file/link-presence and cwd-resolvability assertions, never by observing hook side effects from a live session.

---

## 5. Edge cases to handle

1. **Source repo has no `.claude/`** → `link_project_config` must be a clean no-op (the `(repo/name).exists()` guard). Covered by test 2. This is the common case for target repos that don't use project-scoped config — no behavior change for them.
2. **`.claude/` partially tracked + partially gitignored** → git already checked out the tracked part into the worktree. The `lexists` guard means the function skips any entry already present, so it **never clobbers** git's checkout. Subtlety: if `.claude/` (the dir) is tracked but `.claude/settings.local.json` is individually gitignored, git creates the dir, the dir exists in the worktree, the top-level `.claude` lexists → function skips, and the gitignored `settings.local.json` is **not** replicated. This is a real partial-parity gap. **Decision needed:** for the boring default, top-level-entry granularity is acceptable (the common case here is the whole `.claude` gitignored, which works perfectly). If per-file parity is required for partially-tracked repos, the function would need to descend — recommend **not** doing this initially (YAGNI; document the limitation). Covered by test 3 for the don't-clobber half.
3. **Broken / pre-existing symlinks** → use `os.path.lexists` (not `os.path.exists`, which follows links and returns False for a dangling link) for the "already present?" check, so a pre-existing broken link in the worktree isn't silently overwritten or double-created. A fresh `git worktree add` won't contain stray links, but defensive `lexists` is correct.
4. **Worker mutating the operator's real config** (symlink only) → documented blast-radius in §3(i). Bound it by noting in docs that workers run `--dangerously-skip-permissions` and a symlinked `.claude` is the operator's live config. If unacceptable, the operator chooses copy.
5. **Untracked, non-ignored `.mcp.json` at repo root** → only repo where a symlinked project config could be staged by the worker's `git add .` (§2e item 4). Document that project MCP config should be tracked or gitignored; don't code a check unless it bites.
6. **Absolute vs relative symlink target** → resolve `repo` to absolute before `os.symlink` so the link is valid regardless of the worker's cwd or later directory moves.

### Documentation updates (part of this change)
- **`docs/design-decisions.md` §10** must flip from "the cause is named, not yet fixed" / "Open question — fix it, or accept it?" to **"decided + implemented."** Record: the chosen mechanism (replicate via symlink), the rejected alternatives (`--settings`-only and its five-surface gap; tracking `.claude`; user-scoping entire), the copy-vs-symlink fork and why symlink won, and the `.entire`/configurability decisions. Keep the reproduction section (still useful) but reframe the conclusion. The §10 title itself ("...not yet fixed") needs rewording.
- **`README.md` "Debug capture" section** (lines ~158-168) currently states as a standing fact that "project-scoped hooks ... don't load inside the worktree." Update to reflect that workers now inherit project config via symlinked `.claude`/`.mcp.json`, and reframe `--debug-file` as the verification tool for *confirming* parity rather than documenting a known gap. Line 160's parenthetical and the §10 cross-reference both need editing.
- The worker.py docstring mention of §10 (lines ~150-155) and orchestrator.py `_DEBUG_ENV_VAR` docstring (lines ~23-28) reference the gap; verify they don't now contradict the fix. They describe debug capture, which remains valid, but the framing "environment differs from an interactive session" softens.

---

## Recommended decisions (defaults to confirm before implementation)

| Fork | Recommended default | Confidence / why |
|---|---|---|
| Mechanism | Replicate (symlink), not `--settings` | High — only replication covers all 5 config surfaces |
| Copy vs symlink | Symlink | Medium — delivers true parity incl. stateful hooks; accept bounded mutation risk. Operator may prefer copy for isolation |
| Replicate `.entire/`? | No (keep set generic); operator adds it if they want entire to work | Medium — vendor-coupling vs. functional parity tradeoff |
| Fixed vs configurable set | Fixed constant `worktree.PROJECT_CONFIG_PATHS` | High — matches design-decision-9 (no argparse) + YAGNI; promote to `repos.yml` later if needed |
| Partial-tracking granularity | Top-level entry (don't descend) | Medium — common case works; document the partially-tracked limitation |

The two genuine one-way-ish decisions are **copy vs symlink** (changes teardown semantics and blast radius) and **whether headless checkpointing is wanted at all** (the upstream question §10 names). Everything else is reversible in a single edit. I'd surface those two to the operator before coding.

1	"""Thin wrapper around ``git worktree``.
2	
3	Each issue gets ``.worktrees/<issue-identifier>/`` branched off ``main`` per
4	README §3, used once, then removed on Done.
5	
6	``git worktree`` stderr is captured and surfaced in the raised
7	``RuntimeError`` on failure. The orchestrator's pre-spawn try/except
8	threads the message into the runlog's ``halt_reason`` so the operator
9	sees git's actual diagnostic (dirty tree, branch already exists,
10	missing ``main``) rather than just a non-zero exit code.
11	"""
12	from __future__ import annotations
13	
14	import subprocess
15	from pathlib import Path
16	
17	BASE_BRANCH = "main"
18	WORKTREE_DIR = ".worktrees"
19	
20	
21	def add(repo: Path, identifier: str) -> Path:
22	    """Create a worktree branched off ``main`` for ``identifier``.
23	
24	    Returns the absolute path to the new worktree.
25	    """
26	    worktree_path = repo / WORKTREE_DIR / identifier
27	    _run_git(
28	        ["worktree", "add", "-b", identifier, str(worktree_path), BASE_BRANCH],
29	        cwd=repo,
30	    )
31	    return worktree_path
32	
33	
34	def remove(repo: Path, worktree_path: Path) -> None:
35	    """Remove a worktree previously created by :func:`add`."""
36	    _run_git(["worktree", "remove", str(worktree_path)], cwd=repo)
37	
38	
39	def _run_git(args: list[str], *, cwd: Path) -> None:
40	    result = subprocess.run(
41	        ["git", *args],
42	        cwd=cwd,
43	        check=False,
44	        capture_output=True,
45	        text=True,
46	    )
47	    if result.returncode != 0:
48	        raise RuntimeError(
49	            f"git {' '.join(args)} failed (exit {result.returncode}): "
50	            f"{result.stderr.strip() or result.stdout.strip() or '<no output>'}"
51	        )
52	
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/anton/.claude/plans/groovy-greeting-alpaca.md",
  "content": "# Worktree config parity for drain-cycle workers\n\n## Context\n\n`drain-cycle` spawns each Claude Code worker with `cwd` set to a fresh git worktree\nat `<target-repo>/.worktrees/<issue-id>/`. Git worktrees check out only *tracked*\nfiles, and project-scoped Claude config (`.claude/` — settings, hooks, agents, skills,\ncommands — plus a possible root `.mcp.json`) is blanket-gitignored in this repo\n(`.gitignore:23-24` ignore `.entire` and `.claude`). So the worktree has no `.claude/`,\nClaude Code resolves the project root to the worktree, finds nothing, and loads\nuser-scoped config only. Project hooks (entire.io's checkpointing, registered in\n`.claude/settings.json`) silently never fire. `docs/design-decisions.md` §10 documents\nthis as an open question; the operator has now decided to **fix it**.\n\nGoal: a worker in the worktree loads the same project-scoped settings/hooks/agents/\nskills/MCP as a normal in-repo run on a non-worktree branch — i.e. true environment parity.\n\n**Decision (operator-confirmed):** symlink the repo's real config dirs/files into each\nworktree. A normal in-repo run reads *and writes* the repo's actual `.claude/` and\n`.entire/`; symlinking reproduces that exactly, so entire's checkpointing genuinely works\nand persists (it links to the commit, which gets pushed). Accepted tradeoff: the worker\nruns `--dangerously-skip-permissions` and therefore shares — and could mutate — the live\nconfig dirs, which is precisely what a non-worktree run would also do.\n\nRejected: `--settings <repo>/.claude/settings.json` — it loads only one file and leaves\nfour other surfaces broken (`settings.local.json`, project agents/skills/commands,\n`$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`-relative hook scripts, `.mcp.json`), so it isn't comprehensive.\nAlso rejected: copy (isolates the worker but discards entire's checkpoint writes on\nteardown) and tracking `.claude/` in git (commits machine-specific config).\n\n## Implementation\n\n### 1. `drain_cycle/worktree.py` — new symlink step\n\nAdd a module constant and a function next to `add`/`remove`:\n\n```python\nPROJECT_CONFIG_PATHS = (\".claude\", \".entire\", \".mcp.json\")\n\ndef link_project_config(repo: Path, worktree_path: Path) -> list[Path]:\n    \"\"\"Symlink gitignored project-scoped config from ``repo`` into the\n    worktree so a worker sees the same settings/hooks/agents/skills/MCP as an\n    interactive session at the repo root. Skips entries absent in ``repo`` or\n    already present in the worktree (a tracked file git already checked out).\n    Returns the links created.\"\"\"\n```\n\nBehavior, per name in `PROJECT_CONFIG_PATHS`:\n- `source = repo.resolve() / name` — resolve `repo` absolute so the link target is\n  valid regardless of the worker's cwd.\n- Skip if `not source.exists()` (clean no-op for repos without that config).\n- Skip if `os.path.lexists(worktree_path / name)` — `lexists` (not `exists`) so a\n  tracked entry git already checked out is never clobbered and a dangling link is\n  detected.\n- Otherwise `os.symlink(source, worktree_path / name)`.\n\nThese symlinked entries are gitignored in the worktree (the worktree shares the repo's\ntracked `.gitignore`), so the worker's `git add` never stages them. `.mcp.json` doesn't\nexist in this repo (clean no-op) but is included for correctness on other repos.\n\n### 2. `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py` — wire it in\n\nIn `run()`, inside the existing pre-spawn `try` (orchestrator.py:147-152), call the new\nstep immediately after the worktree is created:\n\n```python\nworktree_path = worktree.add(target_repo, identifier)\nworktree.link_project_config(target_repo, worktree_path)   # NEW\nlinear.set_state(issue[\"id\"], _IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME)\n```\n\nPlacing it inside that `try` reuses the established failure contract: any error becomes a\nrecorded \"setup failed\" halt with the planned worktree path (orchestrator.py:153-174) —\nno new error branch. No teardown code needed: `git worktree remove` on Done\n(orchestrator.py) deletes the worktree dir and its *symlinks* but not their targets, so\nthe operator's real `.claude/`/`.entire/` are untouched. On halt the worktree (with live\nsymlinks) is left in place for inspection, which is fine.\n\n### Edge cases\n- **No `.claude/` in source repo** → no-op (the `exists()` guard).\n- **Partially tracked `.claude/`** → top-level `lexists` guard skips it, never clobbering\n  git's checkout. Granularity is the top-level entry; per-file parity for partially-tracked\n  dirs is out of scope (document, don't code — YAGNI).\n- **Dangling/pre-existing link** → `lexists` detects it; not double-created.\n- **Untracked, non-ignored root `.mcp.json`** (no such repo today) → could be staged by a\n  worker `git add .`; document that project MCP config should be tracked or gitignored\n  rather than coding a check.\n\n### Configurability\nFixed module constant `worktree.PROJECT_CONFIG_PATHS`, not a config knob — matches\ndesign-decision 9 (no argparse; YAML only) and YAGNI. Promote to `repos.yml` later only\nif a per-repo set is ever needed.\n\n## Docs (part of this change)\n- **`docs/design-decisions.md` §10**: flip from \"the cause is named, not yet fixed\" /\n  \"Open question — fix it, or accept it?\" to **decided + implemented**. Reword the title\n  (\"...not yet fixed\"). Keep the cause explanation and reproduction snippet; replace the\n  open-question section with the chosen mechanism (symlink the real `.claude`/`.entire`/\n  `.mcp.json`), why symlink beat copy and `--settings`, and that this restores parity with\n  a non-worktree run.\n- **`README.md` \"Debug capture\"** (lines 158-168): the standing claim that \"project-scoped\n  hooks ... don't load inside the worktree\" is now false. Reframe: workers inherit project\n  config via symlinks; `--debug-file`/`DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1` becomes the tool to *confirm*\n  parity rather than to document a gap. Edit line 160's parenthetical and the §10 cross-ref.\n\n## Tests (real git tmp repo + fake claude + monkeypatched Linear — existing patterns; no real tokens)\n\n`tests/test_worktree.py` (reuse `_init_repo`):\n- `test_link_project_config_symlinks_gitignored_claude` — seed `.claude/settings.json`,\n  gitignore `.claude`; assert `(wt/\".claude\").is_symlink()`, `readlink` → `repo/.claude`,\n  and `settings.json` reads through the link.\n- `test_link_project_config_noop_when_no_claude` — no config → no link, no error.\n- `test_link_project_config_skips_existing_tracked_entry` — tracked `.claude/` → not a\n  symlink after `add`; content intact (don't-clobber).\n- `test_link_project_config_includes_mcp_and_entire_when_present` — gitignored `.mcp.json`\n  and `.entire/` get symlinked; absent → no link.\n- `test_linked_config_is_gitignored_in_worktree` — `git status --porcelain` from the\n  worktree shows `.claude` is NOT untracked (proves `git add` won't stage it).\n- `test_remove_preserves_symlink_target` — after `worktree.remove`, `repo/.claude` and its\n  contents still exist (teardown follows the link to delete nothing real).\n\n`tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py` (fake-claude pattern): seed a gitignored\n`.claude/settings.json` in the test repo; fake `claude` records whether\n`.claude/settings.json` is readable from its own cwd (`[ -r .claude/settings.json ]`).\nAssert the marker says \"present\" — proving the orchestrator links config in before spawn,\nwithout spending tokens. This asserts the *precondition* for hook loading (resolvability\nfrom worker cwd), not live hook firing.\n\nRegression: existing iteration/argv tests use repos with no `.claude/` → `link_project_config`\nis a clean no-op; full suite must stay green. Worker argv is untouched, so the\n`test_orchestrator_debug_capture_*` argv assertions are unaffected.\n\n## Verification\n1. `uv run pytest` — all tests green (existing + new).\n2. Manual parity check on this repo:\n   ```bash\n   DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1 drain-cycle   # against a cycle with a repo:drain-cycle issue\n   ```\n   Then diff the per-issue `~/.drain-cycle/runs/*-<issue>.debug.log` against the §10\n   baseline: the worktree run should now watch project `.claude/settings.json` and fire\n   entire's SessionStart/SessionEnd hooks (\"Entire CLI will link this conversation...\") —\n   matching the repo-root column of the §10 table.\n3. Confirm a completed issue's worktree removal left `<repo>/.claude` and `<repo>/.entire`\n   intact (`ls` after a Done run).\n\n## Critical files\n- `drain_cycle/worktree.py` — add `PROJECT_CONFIG_PATHS` + `link_project_config`\n- `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:148` — call it after `worktree.add`, inside the pre-spawn try\n- `tests/test_worktree.py`, `tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py` — new tests\n- `docs/design-decisions.md` §10, `README.md` \"Debug capture\" — flip gap → fix\n"
}

---

# Worktree config parity for drain-cycle workers

## Context

`drain-cycle` spawns each Claude Code worker with `cwd` set to a fresh git worktree
at `<target-repo>/.worktrees/<issue-id>/`. Git worktrees check out only *tracked*
files, and this repo blanket-gitignores project-scoped Claude config — `.claude/`
(settings, hooks, agents, skills, commands) plus a possible root `.mcp.json`
(`.gitignore:23-24` ignore `.entire` and `.claude`). So the worktree has no `.claude/`:
Claude Code resolves the project root to the worktree, finds nothing, and loads
user-scoped config only. Project hooks (entire.io's checkpointing, registered in
`.claude/settings.json`) silently never fire. `docs/design-decisions.md` §10 records
this as an open question; the operator has now decided to **fix it**.

Goal: a worker in the worktree loads the same project-scoped settings/hooks/agents/
skills/MCP as a normal in-repo run on a non-worktree branch — true environment parity.

**Decision (operator-confirmed):** symlink the repo's real config dirs/files into each
worktree. A normal in-repo run reads *and writes* the repo's actual `.claude/` and
`.entire/`; symlinking reproduces that exactly, so entire's checkpointing works and
persists (it links to the commit, which gets pushed). Accepted tradeoff: the worker runs
`--dangerously-skip-permissions` and so shares — and could mutate — the live config dirs,
exactly as a non-worktree run would.

Rejected: `--settings <repo>/.claude/settings.json` loads only one file and leaves four
other surfaces broken (`settings.local.json`, project agents/skills/commands,
`$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`-relative hook scripts, `.mcp.json`). Also rejected: copy (isolates
the worker but discards entire's checkpoint writes on teardown) and tracking `.claude/`
in git (commits machine-specific config).

## Implementation

### 1. `drain_cycle/worktree.py` — new symlink step

Add a module constant and a function next to `add`/`remove`:

```python
PROJECT_CONFIG_PATHS = (".claude", ".entire", ".mcp.json")

def link_project_config(repo: Path, worktree_path: Path) -> list[Path]:
    """Symlink gitignored project-scoped config from ``repo`` into the
    worktree so a worker sees the same settings/hooks/agents/skills/MCP as an
    interactive session at the repo root. Skips entries absent in ``repo`` or
    already present in the worktree (a tracked file git already checked out).
    Returns the links created."""
```

Behavior, per name in `PROJECT_CONFIG_PATHS`:
- `source = repo.resolve() / name` — resolve `repo` absolute so the link target stays
  valid regardless of the worker's cwd.
- Skip if `not source.exists()` (clean no-op for repos without that config).
- Skip if `os.path.lexists(worktree_path / name)` — `lexists`, not `exists`, so a
  tracked entry git already checked out is never clobbered and a dangling link is
  detected.
- Otherwise `os.symlink(source, worktree_path / name)`.

These symlinked entries are gitignored in the worktree (the worktree shares the repo's
tracked `.gitignore`), so the worker's `git add` never stages them. `.mcp.json` doesn't
exist in this repo (clean no-op) but is included for correctness on other repos.

### 2. `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py` — wire it in

In `run()`, inside the existing pre-spawn `try` (orchestrator.py:147-152), call the new
step right after the worktree is created:

```python
worktree_path = worktree.add(target_repo, identifier)
worktree.link_project_config(target_repo, worktree_path)   # NEW
linear.set_state(issue["id"], _IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME)
```

Placing it inside that `try` reuses the established failure contract: any error becomes a
recorded "setup failed" halt with the planned worktree path (orchestrator.py:153-174) —
no new error branch. Teardown needs no new code: `git worktree remove` on Done
(orchestrator.py) deletes the worktree dir and its *symlinks* but not their targets, so
the operator's real `.claude/`/`.entire/` survive. On halt the worktree, with live
symlinks, is left in place for inspection.

### Edge cases
- **No `.claude/` in source repo** → no-op (the `exists()` guard).
- **Partially tracked `.claude/`** → the top-level `lexists` guard skips it, never
  clobbering git's checkout. Granularity is the top-level entry; per-file parity for
  partially-tracked dirs is out of scope (document, don't code — YAGNI).
- **Dangling/pre-existing link** → `lexists` detects it; not double-created.
- **Untracked, non-ignored root `.mcp.json`** (no such repo today) → a worker `git add .`
  could stage it; document that project MCP config should be tracked or gitignored rather
  than coding a check.

### Configurability
Fixed module constant `worktree.PROJECT_CONFIG_PATHS`, not a config knob — matches
design-decision 9 (no argparse; YAML only) and YAGNI. Promote to `repos.yml` later only
if a per-repo set is ever needed.

## Docs (part of this change)
- **`docs/design-decisions.md` §10**: flip from "the cause is named, not yet fixed" /
  "Open question — fix it, or accept it?" to **decided + implemented**. Reword the title
  ("...not yet fixed"). Keep the cause explanation and reproduction snippet; replace the
  open-question section with the chosen mechanism (symlink the real `.claude`/`.entire`/
  `.mcp.json`), why symlink beat copy and `--settings`, and the restored parity with a
  non-worktree run.
- **`README.md` "Debug capture"** (lines 158-168): the standing claim that "project-scoped
  hooks ... don't load inside the worktree" is now false. Reframe: workers inherit project
  config via symlinks, so `--debug-file`/`DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1` now *confirms* parity rather
  than documenting a gap. Edit line 160's parenthetical and the §10 cross-ref.

## Tests (real git tmp repo + fake claude + monkeypatched Linear — existing patterns; no real tokens)

`tests/test_worktree.py` (reuse `_init_repo`):
- `test_link_project_config_symlinks_gitignored_claude` — seed `.claude/settings.json`,
  gitignore `.claude`; assert `(wt/".claude").is_symlink()`, `readlink` → `repo/.claude`,
  and `settings.json` reads through the link.
- `test_link_project_config_noop_when_no_claude` — no config → no link, no error.
- `test_link_project_config_skips_existing_tracked_entry` — tracked `.claude/` → not a
  symlink after `add`; content intact (don't-clobber).
- `test_link_project_config_includes_mcp_and_entire_when_present` — gitignored `.mcp.json`
  and `.entire/` get symlinked; absent → no link.
- `test_linked_config_is_gitignored_in_worktree` — `git status --porcelain` from the
  worktree shows `.claude` is NOT untracked (proves `git add` won't stage it).
- `test_remove_preserves_symlink_target` — after `worktree.remove`, `repo/.claude` and its
  contents still exist (teardown follows the link to delete nothing real).

`tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py` (fake-claude pattern): seed a gitignored
`.claude/settings.json` in the test repo; the fake `claude` records whether
`.claude/settings.json` is readable from its own cwd (`[ -r .claude/settings.json ]`).
Assert the marker says "present", proving the orchestrator links config in before spawn
without spending tokens. This asserts the *precondition* for hook loading (resolvability
from worker cwd), not live hook firing.

Regression: existing iteration/argv tests use repos with no `.claude/`, so
`link_project_config` is a clean no-op; the full suite must stay green. Worker argv is
untouched, so the `test_orchestrator_debug_capture_*` argv assertions are unaffected.

## Verification
1. `uv run pytest` — all tests green (existing + new).
2. Manual parity check on this repo:
   ```bash
   DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1 drain-cycle   # against a cycle with a repo:drain-cycle issue
   ```
   Then diff the per-issue `~/.drain-cycle/runs/*-<issue>.debug.log` against the §10
   baseline: the worktree run should now watch project `.claude/settings.json` and fire
   entire's SessionStart/SessionEnd hooks ("Entire CLI will link this conversation...") —
   matching the repo-root column of the §10 table.
3. Confirm a completed issue's worktree removal left `<repo>/.claude` and `<repo>/.entire`
   intact (`ls` after a Done run).

## Critical files
- `drain_cycle/worktree.py` — add `PROJECT_CONFIG_PATHS` + `link_project_config`
- `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:148` — call it after `worktree.add`, inside the pre-spawn try
- `tests/test_worktree.py`, `tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py` — new tests
- `docs/design-decisions.md` §10, `README.md` "Debug capture" — flip gap → fix

  "plan": "# Worktree config parity for drain-cycle workers\n\n## Context\n\n`drain-cycle` spawns each Claude Code worker with `cwd` set to a fresh git worktree\nat `<target-repo>/.worktrees/<issue-id>/`. Git worktrees check out only *tracked*\nfiles, and this repo blanket-gitignores project-scoped Claude config — `.claude/`\n(settings, hooks, agents, skills, commands) plus a possible root `.mcp.json`\n(`.gitignore:23-24` ignore `.entire` and `.claude`). So the worktree has no `.claude/`:\nClaude Code resolves the project root to the worktree, finds nothing, and loads\nuser-scoped config only. Project hooks (entire.io's checkpointing, registered in\n`.claude/settings.json`) silently never fire. `docs/design-decisions.md` §10 records\nthis as an open question; the operator has now decided to **fix it**.\n\nGoal: a worker in the worktree loads the same project-scoped settings/hooks/agents/\nskills/MCP as a normal in-repo run on a non-worktree branch — true environment parity.\n\n**Decision (operator-confirmed):** symlink the repo's real config dirs/files into each\nworktree. A normal in-repo run reads *and writes* the repo's actual `.claude/` and\n`.entire/`; symlinking reproduces that exactly, so entire's checkpointing works and\npersists (it links to the commit, which gets pushed). Also rejected: copy (isolates\nthe worker but discards entire's checkpoint writes on teardown) and tracking `.claude/`\nin git (commits machine-specific config).\n\n## Implementation\n\n### 1. `drain_cycle/worktree.py` — new symlink step\n\nAdd a module constant and a function next to `add`/`remove`:\n\n```python\nPROJECT_CONFIG_PATHS = (\".claude\", \".entire\", \".mcp.json\")\n\ndef link_project_config(repo: Path, worktree_path: Path) -> list[Path]:\n    \"\"\"Symlink gitignored project-scoped config from ``repo`` into the\n    worktree so a worker sees the same settings/hooks/agents/skills/MCP as an\n    interactive session at the repo root. Skips entries absent in ``repo`` or\n    already present in the worktree (a tracked file git already checked out).\n    Returns the links created.\"\"\"\n```\n\nBehavior, per name in `PROJECT_CONFIG_PATHS`:\n- `source = repo.resolve() / name` — resolve `repo` absolute so the link target stays\n  valid regardless of the worker's cwd.\n- Skip if `not source.exists()` (clean no-op for repos without that config).\n- Skip if `os.path.lexists(worktree_path / name)` — `lexists`, not `exists`, so a\n  tracked entry git already checked out is never clobbered and a dangling link is\n  detected.\n- Otherwise `os.symlink(source, worktree_path / name)`.\n\nThese symlinked entries are gitignored in the worktree (the worktree shares the repo's\ntracked `.gitignore`), so the worker's `git add` never stages them. `.mcp.json` doesn't\nexist in this repo (clean no-op) but is included for correctness on other repos.\n\n### 2. `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py` — wire it in\n\nIn `run()`, inside the existing pre-spawn `try` (orchestrator.py:147-152), call the new\nstep right after the worktree is created:\n\n```python\nworktree_path = worktree.add(target_repo, identifier)\nworktree.link_project_config(target_repo, worktree_path)   # NEW\nlinear.set_state(issue[\"id\"], _IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME)\n```\n\nPlacing it inside that `try` reuses the established failure contract: any error becomes a\nrecorded \"setup failed\" halt with the planned worktree path (orchestrator.py:153-174) —\nno new error branch. ```
1	"""Per-issue target-repo resolution.
2	
3	The orchestrator used to be single-repo by construction: every worktree
4	landed under ``Path.cwd()``. Cycles in this workspace span multiple repos
5	by design — ``linear-workflow.md`` makes "Affected repos" part of the
6	six initiative-readiness fields, and the Ops slot deliberately holds
7	cross-repo maintenance issues — so each issue now carries a
8	``repo:<name>`` label and ``~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml`` maps the name to
9	an absolute path on disk.
10	
11	Two distinct error types so the two halt paths can branch cleanly:
12	
13	* ``RepoConfigError`` — startup-time problems (missing file, malformed
14	  YAML, empty, wrong top-level shape). The CLI catches it before any
15	  Linear traffic, prints to stderr, exits 1, and does **not** write a
16	  run-log entry — there is no cycle to log against yet.
17	* ``RepoResolutionError`` — per-issue problems (no ``repo:`` label,
18	  multiple ``repo:`` labels, name not in config, resolved path missing).
19	  The orchestrator catches it as a pre-spawn halt: write a run-log
20	  entry, print the matching ``Halt:`` line, exit 1. No revert is
21	  attempted because no Linear state was moved yet.
22	"""
23	from __future__ import annotations
24	
25	from dataclasses import dataclass
26	from pathlib import Path
27	from typing import Any
28	
29	import yaml
30	
31	_CONFIG_DISPLAY = "~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml"
32	_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX = "repo:"
33	
34	_MISSING_CONFIG_HINT = (
35	    "create it mapping each repo: label to an absolute path, e.g.:\n\n"
36	    "  repos:\n"
37	    "    drain-cycle: /Users/you/src/drain-cycle\n"
38	    "    pde-skills:  /Users/you/src/pde-skills\n\n"
39	    "see docs/repos.example.yml for a template."
40	)
41	
42	
43	class RepoConfigError(RuntimeError):
44	    """``repos.yml`` is missing or malformed."""
45	
46	
47	class RepoResolutionError(RuntimeError):
48	    """An issue cannot be mapped to a target repo."""
49	
50	
51	def default_config_path() -> Path:
52	    """Resolved per call so tests can redirect via ``monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", ...)``."""
53	    return Path.home() / ".drain-cycle" / "repos.yml"
54	
55	
56	@dataclass(frozen=True)
57	class Repos:
58	    """Loaded ``repos.yml`` mapping (``name`` → absolute ``Path``)."""
59	
60	    mapping: dict[str, Path]
61	
62	    def resolve(self, issue: dict[str, Any]) -> Path:
63	        labels = [
64	            label
65	            for label in issue.get("labels", [])
66	            if label.startswith(_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX)
67	        ]
68	        if not labels:
69	            raise RepoResolutionError(f"no {_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX} label on issue")
70	        if len(labels) > 1:
71	            names = ", ".join(sorted(label.removeprefix(_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX) for label in labels))
72	            raise RepoResolutionError(f"multiple {_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX} labels: {names}")
73	        name = labels[0].removeprefix(_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX)
74	        try:
75	            path = self.mapping[name]
76	        except KeyError:
77	            raise RepoResolutionError(
78	                f'repo "{name}" not in {_CONFIG_DISPLAY}'
79	            ) from None
80	        if not path.exists():
81	            raise RepoResolutionError(f"resolved path {path} does not exist")
82	        return path
83	
84	
85	def load(path: Path | None = None) -> Repos:
86	    """Read ``repos.yml`` and return a validated ``Repos``.
87	
88	    Tilde-prefixed paths inside the file are expanded against ``$HOME``.
89	    Every failure mode raises ``RepoConfigError`` with a message that
90	    names the on-disk problem so the operator can fix it without
91	    re-reading source.
92	    """
93	    if path is None:
94	        path = default_config_path()
95	    if not path.exists():
96	        raise RepoConfigError(
97	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} not found at {path}\n{_MISSING_CONFIG_HINT}"
98	        )
99	    try:
100	        data = yaml.safe_load(path.read_text())
101	    except yaml.YAMLError as exc:
102	        raise RepoConfigError(
103	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} is not valid YAML: {exc}"
104	        ) from None
105	    if data is None:
106	        raise RepoConfigError(f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} is empty")
107	    if not isinstance(data, dict) or "repos" not in data:
108	        raise RepoConfigError(
109	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} must have a top-level 'repos:' key"
110	        )
111	    block = data["repos"]
112	    if not isinstance(block, dict) or not block:
113	        raise RepoConfigError(
114	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} 'repos:' must be a non-empty mapping"
115	        )
116	    mapping: dict[str, Path] = {}
117	    for name, value in block.items():
118	        if not isinstance(value, str):
119	            raise RepoConfigError(
120	                f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} entry {name!r}: path must be a string"
121	            )
122	        mapping[str(name)] = Path(value).expanduser()
123	    return Repos(mapping=mapping)
124	
```

1	"""Per-issue and per-cycle resource guardrails.
2	
3	A spawned ``claude -p`` worker can run away — burning tokens, wall-clock,
4	and quota with no ceiling but the outer per-issue timeout. This module
5	defines the thresholds that bound that spend and the ``Breach`` value that
6	names which one was crossed.
7	
8	**Two layers, belt and suspenders.**
9	
10	* *Native belt* — ``per_issue_cost_usd`` is handed to ``claude`` as
11	  ``--max-budget-usd`` so the session self-terminates on cost without the
12	  orchestrator having to watch it.
13	* *Orchestrator suspenders* — ``per_issue_tokens`` and ``per_issue_seconds``
14	  are enforced by the worker against the live event stream: when either is
15	  crossed the worker SIGKILLs the session's process group (see
16	  ``worker.py``). The ``cycle_*`` caps are enforced by the orchestrator
17	  between issues via ``check_cycle`` — death-by-aggregate, where every issue
18	  is individually under its cap but their sum drains the quota, is exactly
19	  what the per-issue caps cannot catch.
20	
21	Token count is the primary guardrail (a subscription user pays in tokens,
22	not dollars); cost rides alongside it.
23	
24	**On/off per guardrail.** Any threshold may be ``None`` — that guardrail is
25	off. The defaults below are all live; an operator turns one off by setting
26	its key to ``null`` in ``~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml``.
27	
28	**Load precedence.** Baked-in defaults, overlaid by ``limits.yml`` if it
29	exists: a key absent from the file keeps its default, an explicit ``null``
30	turns the guardrail off, a positive number overrides it. A present-but-
31	malformed file raises ``LimitsConfigError`` rather than silently falling
32	back to defaults — a typo that quietly disabled a cap the operator believed
33	was active is worse than a loud startup halt. The defaults are deliberately
34	generous starting points; recalibrate against real run-log spend.
35	"""
36	from __future__ import annotations
37	
38	from dataclasses import dataclass, fields
39	from pathlib import Path
40	from typing import Any
41	
42	import yaml
43	
44	_CONFIG_DISPLAY = "~/.drain-cycle/limits.yml"
45	
46	
47	class LimitsConfigError(RuntimeError):
48	    """``limits.yml`` exists but is malformed."""
49	
50	
51	@dataclass(frozen=True)
52	class Breach:
53	    """A crossed guardrail, recorded into the halting run-log entry.
54	
55	    ``scope`` is ``"per-issue"`` or ``"cycle"``; ``metric`` is ``"token"``,
56	    ``"time"``, or ``"cost"``. ``limit`` is the cap that was set and
57	    ``observed`` the value at the moment of the breach. ``describe`` renders
58	    the operator-facing line — the worker uses it for a per-issue kill and
59	    the orchestrator for a cycle stop, so the wording cannot drift between
60	    the two surfaces.
61	    """
62	
63	    scope: str
64	    metric: str
65	    limit: float
66	    observed: float
67	
68	    def describe(self) -> str:
69	        return f"{self.scope} {self.metric} cap exceeded: {self._detail()}"
70	
71	    def _detail(self) -> str:
72	        if self.metric == "cost":
73	            return f"${self.observed:,.2f} ≥ ${self.limit:,.2f}"
74	        if self.metric == "time":
75	            return f"{self.observed:,.0f}s ≥ {self.limit:,.0f}s"
76	        return f"{self.observed:,.0f} ≥ {self.limit:,.0f} tokens"
77	
78	
79	@dataclass(frozen=True)
80	class Limits:
81	    """Resource caps. ``None`` on any field turns that guardrail off.
82	
83	    Defaults: per-issue 8M tokens · 20 min · $15; cycle 30M tokens ·
84	    90 min · $60.
85	    """
86	
87	    per_issue_tokens: int | None = 8_000_000
88	    per_issue_seconds: float | None = 20 * 60
89	    per_issue_cost_usd: float | None = 15.0
90	    cycle_tokens: int | None = 30_000_000
91	    cycle_seconds: float | None = 90 * 60
92	    cycle_cost_usd: float | None = 60.0
93	
94	
95	def default_config_path() -> Path:
96	    """Resolved per call so tests can redirect via ``monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", ...)``."""
97	    return Path.home() / ".drain-cycle" / "limits.yml"
98	
99	
100	def load(path: Path | None = None) -> Limits:
101	    """Read ``limits.yml`` over the baked-in defaults.
102	
103	    A missing or empty file yields the defaults unchanged — ``limits.yml``
104	    is optional. A present file maps limit names to positive numbers (or
105	    ``null`` to disable that guardrail); unknown keys, non-numeric values,
106	    booleans, and non-positive numbers each raise ``LimitsConfigError`` with
107	    a message naming the on-disk problem.
108	    """
109	    defaults = Limits()
110	    if path is None:
111	        path = default_config_path()
112	    if not path.exists():
113	        return defaults
114	    try:
115	        data = yaml.safe_load(path.read_text())
116	    except yaml.YAMLError as exc:
117	        raise LimitsConfigError(f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} is not valid YAML: {exc}") from None
118	    if data is None:
119	        return defaults
120	    if not isinstance(data, dict):
121	        raise LimitsConfigError(
122	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} must be a mapping of limit names to numbers"
123	        )
124	
125	    valid = [f.name for f in fields(Limits)]
126	    unknown = set(data) - set(valid)
127	    if unknown:
128	        raise LimitsConfigError(
129	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} has unknown keys: {', '.join(sorted(unknown))}; "
130	            f"valid keys: {', '.join(valid)}"
131	        )
132	
133	    values: dict[str, Any] = {}
134	    for name in valid:
135	        if name not in data:
136	            values[name] = getattr(defaults, name)
137	            continue
138	        values[name] = _coerce(name, data[name])
139	    return Limits(**values)
140	
141	
142	def _coerce(name: str, raw: Any) -> float | None:
143	    """Validate one ``limits.yml`` value: ``None`` (off) or a positive number."""
144	    if raw is None:
145	        return None
146	    # ``bool`` is a subclass of ``int``; ``per_issue_tokens: true`` would
147	    # otherwise slip through as ``1`` and silently arm an absurd cap.
148	    if isinstance(raw, bool) or not isinstance(raw, (int, float)) or raw <= 0:
149	        raise LimitsConfigError(
150	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} entry {name!r}: must be a positive number "
151	            f"or null (got {raw!r})"
152	        )
153	    return raw
154	
155	
156	def check_cycle(
157	    limits: Limits, *, tokens: int, cost_usd: float, seconds: float
158	) -> Breach | None:
159	    """Return the first cycle-wide cap the running totals breach, else ``None``.
160	
161	    Tokens are checked first (the primary guardrail), then cost, then
162	    wall-clock. A guardrail set to ``None`` is skipped.
163	    """
164	    if limits.cycle_tokens is not None and tokens >= limits.cycle_tokens:
165	        return Breach("cycle", "token", limits.cycle_tokens, tokens)
166	    if limits.cycle_cost_usd is not None and cost_usd >= limits.cycle_cost_usd:
167	        return Breach("cycle", "cost", limits.cycle_cost_usd, cost_usd)
168	    if limits.cycle_seconds is not None and seconds >= limits.cycle_seconds:
169	        return Breach("cycle", "time", limits.cycle_seconds, seconds)
170	    return None
171	
```

> AGENT

The config patterns are clear: `repos.yml` (required, top-level `repos:` map, `RepoConfigError`) and `limits.yml` (optional, overlays dataclass defaults, per-key validation). The path set fits naturally as an optional top-level key in `repos.yml` — that file already owns repo/worktree setup, is always loaded, and its `load()` ignores unknown top-level keys today, so the extension is backward-compatible. `.entire` drops out of the default and becomes opt-in. Revising the plan.

```json
{
  "allowedPrompts": [
    {
      "tool": "Bash",
      "prompt": "run the test suite with uv run pytest"
    }
  ],
  "plan": "# Worktree config parity for drain-cycle workers\n\n## Context\n\n`drain-cycle` spawns each Claude Code worker with `cwd` set to a fresh git worktree\nat `<target-repo>/.worktrees/<issue-id>/`. Git worktrees check out only *tracked*\nfiles, and this repo blanket-gitignores project-scoped Claude config — `.claude/`\n(settings, hooks, agents, skills, commands) plus a possible root `.mcp.json`\n(`.gitignore:23-24` ignore `.entire` and `.claude`). So the worktree has no `.claude/`:\nClaude Code resolves the project root to the worktree, finds nothing, and loads\nuser-scoped config only. Project hooks (entire.io's checkpointing, registered in\n`.claude/settings.json`) silently never fire. `docs/design-decisions.md` §10 records\nthis as an open question; the operator has now decided to **fix it**.\n\nGoal: a worker in the worktree loads the same project-scoped settings/hooks/agents/\nskills/MCP as a normal in-repo run on a non-worktree branch — true environment parity.\n\n**Decision (operator-confirmed):** symlink the repo's real config dirs/files into each\nworktree. A normal in-repo run reads *and writes* the repo's actual config; symlinking\nreproduces that exactly, so a stateful hook like entire's checkpointing works and persists\n(it links to the commit, which gets pushed). Accepted tradeoff: the worker runs\n`--dangerously-skip-permissions` and so shares — and could mutate — the live config dirs,\nexactly as a non-worktree run would.\n\nThe symlinked set is **configurable** (operator round-2 call). Default `[.claude, .mcp.json]`\n— sensible for any repo. `.entire` is *not* a default (not everyone installs entire); this\noperator adds it via config. See Configurability below.\n\nRejected: `--settings <repo>/.claude/settings.json` loads only one file and leaves four\nother surfaces broken (`settings.local.json`, project agents/skills/commands,\n`$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`-relative hook scripts, `.mcp.json`). Also rejected: copy (isolates\nthe worker but discards entire's checkpoint writes on teardown) and tracking `.claude/`\nin git (commits machine-specific config).\n\n## Implementation\n\n### 1. `drain_cycle/worktree.py` — new symlink step\n\nAdd a pure function next to `add`/`remove` that takes the names to link (the default\nlives in the config layer, `repos.py` — see Configurability):\n\n```python\ndef link_project_config(\n    repo: Path, worktree_path: Path, names: Iterable[str]\n) -> list[Path]:\n    \"\"\"Symlink gitignored project-scoped config from ``repo`` into the\n    worktree so a worker sees the same settings/hooks/agents/skills/MCP as an\n    interactive session at the repo root. Skips entries absent in ``repo`` or\n    already present in the worktree (a tracked file git already checked out).\n    Returns the links created.\"\"\"\n```\n\nBehavior, per name in `names`:\n- `source = repo.resolve() / name` — resolve `repo` absolute so the link target stays\n  valid regardless of the worker's cwd.\n- Skip if `not source.exists()` (clean no-op for repos without that config).\n- Skip if `os.path.lexists(worktree_path / name)` — `lexists`, not `exists`, so a\n  tracked entry git already checked out is never clobbered and a dangling link is\n  detected.\n- Otherwise `os.symlink(source, worktree_path / name)`.\n\nThese symlinked entries are gitignored in the worktree (the worktree shares the repo's\ntracked `.gitignore`), so the worker's `git add` never stages them. `.mcp.json` doesn't\nexist in this repo (clean no-op) but is included for correctness on other repos.\n\n### 2. `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py` — wire it in\n\nIn `run()`, inside the existing pre-spawn `try` (orchestrator.py:147-152), call the new\nstep right after the worktree is created:\n\n```python\nworktree_path = worktree.add(target_repo, identifier)\nworktree.link_project_config(                              # NEW\n    target_repo, worktree_path, repos.worktree_config_paths\n)\nlinear.set_state(issue[\"id\"], _IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME)\n```\n\n(`repos` is the loaded `Repos` object the orchestrator already holds for\n`repos.resolve(issue)`; it now also carries `worktree_config_paths`.)\n\nPlacing it inside that `try` reuses the established failure contract: any error becomes a\nrecorded \"setup failed\" halt with the planned worktree path (orchestrator.py:153-174) —\nno new error branch. Teardown needs no new code: `git worktree remove` on Done\n(orchestrator.py) deletes the worktree dir and its *symlinks* but not their targets, so\nthe operator's real `.claude/`/`.entire/` survive. On halt the worktree, with live\nsymlinks, is left in place for inspection.\n\n### Edge cases\n- **No `.claude/` in source repo** → no-op (the `exists()` guard).\n- **Partially tracked `.claude/`** → the top-level `lexists` guard skips it, never\n  clobbering git's checkout. Granularity is the top-level entry; per-file parity for\n  partially-tracked dirs is out of scope (document, don't code — YAGNI).\n- **Dangling/pre-existing link** → `lexists` detects it; not double-created.\n- **Untracked, non-ignored root `.mcp.json`** (no such repo today) → a worker `git add .`\n  could stage it; document that project MCP config should be tracked or gitignored rather\n  than coding a check.\n\n### Configurability — `drain_cycle/repos.py`\n\nThe path set is an optional top-level key in the existing `repos.yml`, not a new file or\na flag (matches design-decision 9: YAML only, no argparse). `repos.yml` already owns\nrepo/worktree setup, is always loaded, and its `load()` ignores unknown top-level keys\ntoday — so this is a backward-compatible extension.\n\n```yaml\nrepos:\n  drain-cycle: /Users/you/src/drain-cycle\n\nworktree_config_paths:   # optional; defaults to [.claude, .mcp.json]\n  - .claude\n  - .mcp.json\n  - .entire              # this operator uses entire, so opts it in\n```\n\n- Add a module default `DEFAULT_WORKTREE_CONFIG_PATHS = (\".claude\", \".mcp.json\")` and a\n  field `worktree_config_paths: tuple[str, ...] = DEFAULT_WORKTREE_CONFIG_PATHS` on the\n  frozen `Repos` dataclass.\n- In `load()`, read optional `data.get(\"worktree_config_paths\")`. Absent → default. Present\n  → must be a non-empty list of relative-path strings; reject non-list, non-string entries,\n  absolute paths, and `..` components, each raising `RepoConfigError` with a message naming\n  the on-disk problem (same contract as the existing `repos:` validation).\n- Update `docs/repos.example.yml` with the commented optional block above.\n\n## Docs (part of this change)\n- **`docs/design-decisions.md` §10**: flip from \"the cause is named, not yet fixed\" /\n  \"Open question — fix it, or accept it?\" to **decided + implemented**. Reword the title\n  (\"...not yet fixed\"). Keep the cause explanation and reproduction snippet; replace the\n  open-question section with the chosen mechanism (symlink a configurable set of project\n  config — default `.claude`/`.mcp.json`, `.entire` opt-in via `repos.yml`), why symlink\n  beat copy and `--settings`, and the restored parity with a non-worktree run.\n- **`README.md` \"Debug capture\"** (lines 158-168): the standing claim that \"project-scoped\n  hooks ... don't load inside the worktree\" is now false. Reframe: workers inherit project\n  config via symlinks, so `--debug-file`/`DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1` now *confirms* parity rather\n  than documenting a gap. Edit line 160's parenthetical and the §10 cross-ref.\n\n## Tests (real git tmp repo + fake claude + monkeypatched Linear — existing patterns; no real tokens)\n\n`tests/test_repos.py` — config parsing for the new key:\n- `worktree_config_paths` absent → `Repos.worktree_config_paths == (\".claude\", \".mcp.json\")`.\n- present custom list (incl. `.entire`) → parsed verbatim.\n- malformed (non-list, non-string entry, absolute path, `..`) → `RepoConfigError`.\n\n`tests/test_worktree.py` (reuse `_init_repo`; calls pass explicit `names`):\n- `test_link_project_config_symlinks_gitignored_claude` — seed `.claude/settings.json`,\n  gitignore `.claude`; assert `(wt/\".claude\").is_symlink()`, `readlink` → `repo/.claude`,\n  and `settings.json` reads through the link.\n- `test_link_project_config_noop_when_no_claude` — no config → no link, no error.\n- `test_link_project_config_skips_existing_tracked_entry` — tracked `.claude/` → not a\n  symlink after `add`; content intact (don't-clobber).\n- `test_link_project_config_includes_mcp_and_entire_when_present` — gitignored `.mcp.json`\n  and `.entire/` get symlinked; absent → no link.\n- `test_linked_config_is_gitignored_in_worktree` — `git status --porcelain` from the\n  worktree shows `.claude` is NOT untracked (proves `git add` won't stage it).\n- `test_remove_preserves_symlink_target` — after `worktree.remove`, `repo/.claude` and its\n  contents still exist (teardown follows the link to delete nothing real).\n\n`tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py` (fake-claude pattern): seed a gitignored\n`.claude/settings.json` in the test repo; the fake `claude` records whether\n`.claude/settings.json` is readable from its own cwd (`[ -r .claude/settings.json ]`).\nAssert the marker says \"present\", proving the orchestrator links config in before spawn\nwithout spending tokens. This asserts the *precondition* for hook loading (resolvability\nfrom worker cwd), not live hook firing.\n\nRegression: existing iteration/argv tests use repos with no `.claude/`, so\n`link_project_config` is a clean no-op; the full suite must stay green. Worker argv is\nuntouched, so the `test_orchestrator_debug_capture_*` argv assertions are unaffected.\n\n## Verification\n1. `uv run pytest` — all tests green (existing + new).\n2. Confirm a completed issue's worktree removal left `<repo>/.claude` and `<repo>/.entire`\n   intact (`ls` after a Done run).\n\n## Critical files\n- `drain_cycle/worktree.py` — add `link_project_config(repo, worktree_path, names)`\n- `drain_cycle/repos.py` — `DEFAULT_WORKTREE_CONFIG_PATHS` + `Repos.worktree_config_paths`\n  field + `load()` parsing/validation of the optional key\n- `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:148` — call it after `worktree.add`, inside the pre-spawn try\n- `docs/repos.example.yml` — document the optional `worktree_config_paths` block\n- `tests/test_repos.py`, `tests/test_worktree.py`, `tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py` — new tests\n- `docs/design-decisions.md` §10, `README.md` \"Debug capture\" — flip gap → fix\n",

  "new_string": "## Linear issue to create (first execution step)\n\nPlan mode is read-only, so creating this is the first step after approval (team **Personal /\nABA**, project **Autonomous cycle drain**). **One issue, not split:** the slices are tightly\ncoupled — the config key, the symlink function, and the orchestrator wiring must land\ntogether to be end-to-end testable and mergeable, and the whole change is ~200-300 LOC\n(incl. tests + docs), a single reviewable batch. The five ACs below are the sub-tasks in\nexecution order.\n\n**Title:** Workers inherit the repo's project config inside worktrees\n\n**Labels:** `repo:drain-cycle`, `model:sonnet`\n\n**Outcome / user story:**\n> As an operator running an unattended drain, I want each worker to load the same\n> project-scoped settings, hooks, agents and skills as a normal session at the repo root —\n> so the tooling I rely on interactively (e.g. entire's checkpointing) also works headless,\n> instead of silently dropping out because the worktree checkout omits gitignored `.claude/`.\n\n**Acceptance criteria (ordered sub-tasks):**\n1. **Config** — `repos.yml` accepts an optional `worktree_config_paths` list (default\n   `[.claude, .mcp.json]`); `repos.py` parses + validates it (`RepoConfigError` on\n   malformed); `tests/test_repos.py` covers default / custom / malformed.\n2. **Symlink helper** — `worktree.link_project_config(repo, worktree_path, names)` symlinks\n   present, not-already-checked-out entries; `tests/test_worktree.py` covers symlink, no-op,\n   skip-tracked, gitignored-in-worktree, and remove-preserves-target.\n3. **Wiring** — orchestrator calls it after `worktree.add`, inside the pre-spawn `try`,\n   passing `repos.worktree_config_paths`; integration test asserts the worker's cwd resolves\n   `.claude/settings.json`.\n4. **Docs** — all four surfaces in the Docs section (design-decisions §10 flip, README\n   debug-capture reframe, README config-key documentation, `repos.example.yml`).\n5. **Verify + ship** — full suite green; manual `DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1` parity check; then the\n   AGENTS.md completion sequence (review → fix → commit+push to main → Linear comment → Done).\n\n**Model:** Sonnet. The plan is prescriptive (file paths, signatures, test names all named),\nso ambiguity is low and Sonnet — the repo's cost-efficient default — is sufficient. Bump to\n`model:opus` only if the operator wants extra care on the §10 / README prose rewrites.\n\n**Size estimate (against the 8M-token / 20-min per-issue caps):** ~200-300 LOC across 4\nsource files + 3 test files + 3 docs. Expected run ~2-4M tokens and ~10-15 min wall-clock —\ncomfortably inside the caps, with the doc rewrites the most token-variable part.\n\n## Verification\n1. `uv run pytest` — all tests green (existing + new)."

  "plan": "# Worktree config parity for drain-cycle workers\n\n## Context\n\n`drain-cycle` spawns each Claude Code worker with `cwd` set to a fresh git worktree\nat `<target-repo>/.worktrees/<issue-id>/`. Git worktrees check out only *tracked*\nfiles, and this repo blanket-gitignores project-scoped Claude config — `.claude/`\n(settings, hooks, agents, skills, commands) plus a possible root `.mcp.json`\n(`.gitignore:23-24` ignore `.entire` and `.claude`). So the worktree has no `.claude/`:\nClaude Code resolves the project root to the worktree, finds nothing, and loads\nuser-scoped config only. Project hooks (entire.io's checkpointing, registered in\n`.claude/settings.json`) silently never fire. `docs/design-decisions.md` §10 records\nthis as an open question; the operator has now decided to **fix it**.\n\nGoal: a worker in the worktree loads the same project-scoped settings/hooks/agents/\nskills/MCP as a normal in-repo run on a non-worktree branch — true environment parity.\n\n**Decision (operator-confirmed):** symlink the repo's real config dirs/files into each\nworktree. A normal in-repo run reads *and writes* the repo's actual config; symlinking\nreproduces that exactly, so a stateful hook like entire's checkpointing works and persists\n(it links to the commit, which gets pushed). Accepted tradeoff: the worker runs\n`--dangerously-skip-permissions` and so shares — and could mutate — the live config dirs,\nexactly as a non-worktree run would.\n\nThe symlinked set is **configurable** (operator round-2 call). Default `[.claude, .mcp.json]`\n— sensible for any repo. `.entire` is *not* a default (not everyone installs entire); this\noperator adds it via config. See Configurability below.\n\nRejected: `--settings <repo>/.claude/settings.json` loads only one file and leaves four\nother surfaces broken (`settings.local.json`, project agents/skills/commands,\n`$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`-relative hook scripts, `.mcp.json`). Also rejected: copy (isolates\nthe worker but discards entire's checkpoint writes on teardown) and tracking `.claude/`\nin git (commits machine-specific config).\n\n## Implementation\n\n### 1. `drain_cycle/worktree.py` — new symlink step\n\nAdd a pure function next to `add`/`remove` that takes the names to link (the default\nlives in the config layer, `repos.py` — see Configurability):\n\n```python\ndef link_project_config(\n    repo: Path, worktree_path: Path, names: Iterable[str]\n) -> list[Path]:\n    \"\"\"Symlink gitignored project-scoped config from ``repo`` into the\n    worktree so a worker sees the same settings/hooks/agents/skills/MCP as an\n    interactive session at the repo root. Skips entries absent in ``repo`` or\n    already present in the worktree (a tracked file git already checked out).\n    Returns the links created.\"\"\"\n```\n\nBehavior, per name in `names`:\n- `source = repo.resolve() / name` — resolve `repo` absolute so the link target stays\n  valid regardless of the worker's cwd.\n- Skip if `not source.exists()` (clean no-op for repos without that config).\n- Skip if `os.path.lexists(worktree_path / name)` — `lexists`, not `exists`, so a\n  tracked entry git already checked out is never clobbered and a dangling link is\n  detected.\n- Otherwise `os.symlink(source, worktree_path / name)`.\n\nThese symlinked entries are gitignored in the worktree (the worktree shares the repo's\ntracked `.gitignore`), so the worker's `git add` never stages them. `.mcp.json` doesn't\nexist in this repo (clean no-op) but is included for correctness on other repos.\n\n### 2. `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py` — wire it in\n\nIn `run()`, inside the existing pre-spawn `try` (orchestrator.py:147-152), call the new\nstep right after the worktree is created:\n\n```python\nworktree_path = worktree.add(target_repo, identifier)\nworktree.link_project_config(                              # NEW\n    target_repo, worktree_path, repos.worktree_config_paths\n)\nlinear.set_state(issue[\"id\"], _IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME)\n```\n\n(`repos` is the loaded `Repos` object the orchestrator already holds for\n`repos.resolve(issue)`; it now also carries `worktree_config_paths`.)\n\nPlacing it inside that `try` reuses the established failure contract: any error becomes a\nrecorded \"setup failed\" halt with the planned worktree path (orchestrator.py:153-174) —\nno new error branch. Teardown needs no new code: `git worktree remove` on Done\n(orchestrator.py) deletes the worktree dir and its *symlinks* but not their targets, so\nthe operator's real `.claude/`/`.entire/` survive. On halt the worktree, with live\nsymlinks, is left in place for inspection.\n\n### Edge cases\n- **No `.claude/` in source repo** → no-op (the `exists()` guard).\n- **Partially tracked `.claude/`** → the top-level `lexists` guard skips it, never\n  clobbering git's checkout. Granularity is the top-level entry; per-file parity for\n  partially-tracked dirs is out of scope (document, don't code — YAGNI).\n- **Dangling/pre-existing link** → `lexists` detects it; not double-created.\n- **Untracked, non-ignored root `.mcp.json`** (no such repo today) → a worker `git add .`\n  could stage it; document that project MCP config should be tracked or gitignored rather\n  than coding a check.\n\n### Configurability — `drain_cycle/repos.py`\n\nThe path set is an optional top-level key in the existing `repos.yml`, not a new file or\na flag (matches design-decision 9: YAML only, no argparse). `repos.yml` already owns\nrepo/worktree setup, is always loaded, and its `load()` ignores unknown top-level keys\ntoday — so this is a backward-compatible extension.\n\n```yaml\nrepos:\n  drain-cycle: /Users/you/src/drain-cycle\n\nworktree_config_paths:   # optional; defaults to [.claude, .mcp.json]\n  - .claude\n  - .mcp.json\n  - .entire              # this operator uses entire, so opts it in\n```\n\n- Add a module default `DEFAULT_WORKTREE_CONFIG_PATHS = (\".claude\", \".mcp.json\")` and a\n  field `worktree_config_paths: tuple[str, ...] = DEFAULT_WORKTREE_CONFIG_PATHS` on the\n  frozen `Repos` dataclass.\n- In `load()`, read optional `data.get(\"worktree_config_paths\")`. Absent → default. Present\n  → must be a non-empty list of relative-path strings; reject non-list, non-string entries,\n  absolute paths, and `..` components, each raising `RepoConfigError` with a message naming\n  the on-disk problem (same contract as the existing `repos:` validation).\n- Update `docs/repos.example.yml` with the commented optional block above.\n\n## Docs (all doc surfaces in scope)\n- **`docs/design-decisions.md` §10**: flip from \"the cause is named, not yet fixed\" /\n  \"Open question — fix it, or accept it?\" to **decided + implemented**. Keep the cause explanation and reproduction snippet; replace the\n  open-question section with the chosen mechanism (symlink a configurable set of project\n  config — default `.claude`/`.mcp.json`, `.entire` opt-in via `repos.yml`), why symlink\n  beat copy and `--settings`, and the restored parity with a non-worktree run.\n- **`README.md` \"Debug capture\"** (lines 158-168): the standing claim that \"project-scoped\n  hooks ... don't load inside the worktree\" is now false. Reframe: workers inherit project\n  config via symlinks, so `--debug-file`/`DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1` now *confirms* parity rather\n  than documenting a gap.
