Add a short companion note (Prerequisites or a new \"Worktree\n  config parity\" line) so the behaviour is discoverable, not buried in the example.\n- **`docs/repos.example.yml`**: add the commented optional `worktree_config_paths` block\n  shown under Configurability, with `.entire` as a commented opt-in example.\n\n## Tests (real git tmp repo + fake claude + monkeypatched Linear — existing patterns; no real tokens)\n\n`tests/test_repos.py` — config parsing for the new key:\n- `worktree_config_paths` absent → `Repos.worktree_config_paths == (\".claude\", \".mcp.json\")`.\n- present custom list (incl. `.entire`) → parsed verbatim.\n- malformed (non-list, non-string entry, absolute path, `..`) → `RepoConfigError`.\n\n`tests/test_worktree.py` (reuse `_init_repo`; calls pass explicit `names`):\n- `test_link_project_config_symlinks_gitignored_claude` — seed `.claude/settings.json`,\n  gitignore `.claude`; assert `(wt/\".claude\").is_symlink()`, `readlink` → `repo/.claude`,\n  and `settings.json` reads through the link.\n- `test_link_project_config_noop_when_no_claude` — no config → no link, no error.\n- `test_link_project_config_skips_existing_tracked_entry` — tracked `.claude/` → not a\n  symlink after `add`; content intact (don't-clobber).\n- `test_link_project_config_includes_mcp_and_entire_when_present` — gitignored `.mcp.json`\n  and `.entire/` get symlinked; absent → no link.\n- `test_linked_config_is_gitignored_in_worktree` — `git status --porcelain` from the\n  worktree shows `.claude` is NOT untracked (proves `git add` won't stage it).\n- `test_remove_preserves_symlink_target` — after `worktree.remove`, `repo/.claude` and its\n  contents still exist (teardown follows the link to delete nothing real).\n\n`tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py` (fake-claude pattern): seed a gitignored\n`.claude/settings.json` in the test repo; the fake `claude` records whether\n`.claude/settings.json` is readable from its own cwd (`[ -r .claude/settings.json ]`).\nAssert the marker says \"present\", proving the orchestrator links config in before spawn\nwithout spending tokens. This asserts the *precondition* for hook loading (resolvability\nfrom worker cwd), not live hook firing.\n\nRegression: existing iteration/argv tests use repos with no `.claude/`, so\n`link_project_config` is a clean no-op; the full suite must stay green. Worker argv is\nuntouched, so the `test_orchestrator_debug_capture_*` argv assertions are unaffected.\n\n## Linear issue to create (first execution step)\n\nPlan mode is read-only, so creating this is the first step after approval (team **Personal /\nABA**, project **Autonomous cycle drain**). **One issue, not split:** the slices are tightly\ncoupled — the config key, the symlink function, and the orchestrator wiring must land\ntogether to be end-to-end testable and mergeable, and the whole change is ~200-300 LOC\n(incl. tests + docs), a single reviewable batch. The five ACs below are the sub-tasks in\nexecution order.\n\n**Title:** Workers inherit the repo's project config inside worktrees\n\n**Labels:** `repo:drain-cycle`, `model:sonnet`\n\n**Outcome / user story:**\n> As an operator running an unattended drain, I want each worker to load the same\n> project-scoped settings, hooks, agents and skills as a normal session at the repo root —\n> so the tooling I rely on interactively (e.g. entire's checkpointing) also works headless,\n> instead of silently dropping out because the worktree checkout omits gitignored `.claude/`.\n\n**Acceptance criteria (ordered sub-tasks):**\n1. **Config** — `repos.yml` accepts an optional `worktree_config_paths` list (default\n   `[.claude, .mcp.json]`); `repos.py` parses + validates it (`RepoConfigError` on\n   malformed); `tests/test_repos.py` covers default / custom / malformed.\n2. **Symlink helper** — `worktree.link_project_config(repo, worktree_path, names)` symlinks\n   present, not-already-checked-out entries; `tests/test_worktree.py` covers symlink, no-op,\n   skip-tracked, gitignored-in-worktree, and remove-preserves-target.\n3. **Wiring** — orchestrator calls it after `worktree.add`, inside the pre-spawn `try`,\n   passing `repos.worktree_config_paths`; integration test asserts the worker's cwd resolves\n   `.claude/settings.json`.\n4. **Docs** — all four surfaces in the Docs section (design-decisions §10 flip, README\n   debug-capture reframe, README config-key documentation, `repos.example.yml`).\n5. **Verify + ship** — full suite green; manual `DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1` parity check; then the\n   AGENTS.md completion sequence (review → fix → commit+push to main → Linear comment → Done).\n\n**Model:** Sonnet. I've completed the editorial pass. Here is the complete revised plan, followed by a summary of the changes.

```markdown
# Worktree config parity for drain-cycle workers

## Context

`drain-cycle` spawns each Claude Code worker with `cwd` set to a fresh git worktree
at `<target-repo>/.worktrees/<issue-id>/`. A git worktree checks out only *tracked*
files, and this repo gitignores all project-scoped Claude config — the `.claude/`
directory (settings, hooks, agents, skills, commands) and any root `.mcp.json`
(`.gitignore:23-24` ignore `.entire` and `.claude`). So the worktree has no `.claude/`:
Claude Code treats the worktree as the project root, finds no project config there, and
loads only user-scoped config (the operator's `~/.claude/`). Project hooks — for example
the checkpointing hooks that the `entire.io` tool registers in `.claude/settings.json` —
silently never fire. `docs/design-decisions.md` decision 10 records this as an open
question (decision 10 is titled "Headless workers lose project-scoped hooks; the cause is
named, not yet fixed"). The operator has now decided to fix it.

Goal: a worker in the worktree loads the same project-scoped settings, hooks, agents,
skills, and MCP config as a normal in-repo run on a non-worktree branch — true
environment parity.

**Decision:** symlink the repo's real config dirs and files into each worktree. A normal
in-repo run reads *and writes* the repo's actual config; symlinking reproduces that
exactly, so a stateful hook — such as the `entire.io` checkpointing hook, which links a
session to the commit it produces — works and persists, because the commit it links to
gets pushed. Accepted tradeoff: the worker runs `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, so it
shares — and could mutate — the live config dirs, exactly as a non-worktree run would.

The symlinked set is **configurable**. It defaults to `[.claude, .mcp.json]`, which suits
any repo. `.entire` is not a default, because not every repo installs the `entire.io`
tool; an operator who uses it adds `.entire` via config. See Configurability below.

**Rejected alternatives.** `--settings <repo>/.claude/settings.json` loads only one file
and leaves four other config surfaces broken: `settings.local.json`, project
agents/skills/commands, hook scripts whose paths are relative to `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`,
and `.mcp.json`. Copying the config (rather than symlinking) isolates the worker but
discards the `entire.io` checkpoint writes when the worktree is torn down. Tracking
`.claude/` in git would commit machine-specific config.

## Implementation

### 1. `drain_cycle/worktree.py` — new symlink step

Add a pure function next to the existing `add`/`remove` functions. It takes the names to
link; the default for those names lives in the config layer, `repos.py` (see
Configurability):

```python
def link_project_config(
    repo: Path, worktree_path: Path, names: Iterable[str]
) -> list[Path]:
    """Symlink gitignored project-scoped config from ``repo`` into the
    worktree so a worker sees the same settings/hooks/agents/skills/MCP as an
    interactive session at the repo root. Skips entries absent in ``repo`` or
    already present in the worktree (a tracked file git already checked out).
    Returns the links created."""
```

Behaviour, for each name in `names`:
- `source = repo.resolve() / name` — resolve `repo` to an absolute path so the link target
  stays valid regardless of the worker's cwd.
- Skip if `not source.exists()` — a clean no-op for repos without that config.
- Skip if `os.path.lexists(worktree_path / name)` — use `lexists`, not `exists`, so an
  entry git already checked out is never clobbered and a dangling link is still detected.
- Otherwise `os.symlink(source, worktree_path / name)`.

These symlinked entries are gitignored in the worktree (the worktree shares the repo's
tracked `.gitignore`), so the worker's `git add` never stages them. `.mcp.json` doesn't
exist in this repo (clean no-op here) but is included so the function is correct on repos
that do have one.

### 2. `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py` — wire it in

In `run()`, inside the existing pre-spawn `try` block (orchestrator.py:147-152), call the
new step right after the worktree is created:

```python
worktree_path = worktree.add(target_repo, identifier)
worktree.link_project_config(                              # NEW
    target_repo, worktree_path, repos.worktree_config_paths
)
linear.set_state(issue["id"], _IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME)
```

(`repos` is the loaded `Repos` object the orchestrator already holds to call
`repos.resolve(issue)`; it now also carries `worktree_config_paths`.)

Placing the call inside that `try` reuses the existing pre-spawn failure handling: any
error becomes a recorded "setup failed" halt with the planned worktree path
(orchestrator.py:153-174), so no new error branch is needed. Teardown needs no new code.
`git worktree remove` on a Done issue (orchestrator.py) deletes the worktree directory and
its *symlinks* but not the symlink targets, so the operator's real `.claude/` and
`.entire/` survive. On a halt, the worktree — with its live symlinks — is left in place
for inspection.

### Edge cases
- **No `.claude/` in the source repo** → no-op (the `exists()` guard).
- **Partially tracked `.claude/`** → the top-level `lexists` guard skips it, so git's
  checkout is never clobbered. The granularity is the top-level entry; per-file parity for
  a partially-tracked directory is out of scope (document it, don't code it — YAGNI).
- **Dangling or pre-existing link** → `lexists` detects it, so it is not created twice.
- **Untracked, non-ignored root `.mcp.json`** (no such repo today) → a worker running
  `git add .` could stage it; document that project MCP config should be either tracked or
  gitignored, rather than coding a guard against this.

### Configurability — `drain_cycle/repos.py`

The path set is an optional top-level key in the existing `repos.yml` — not a new file and
not a command-line flag. This follows the established config convention (design-decisions
decision 9 deliberately keeps configuration in YAML rather than adding an argument parser).
`repos.yml` already owns repo and worktree setup, is always loaded, and its `load()`
already ignores unknown top-level keys, so adding this key is backward-compatible.

```yaml
repos:
  drain-cycle: /Users/you/src/drain-cycle

worktree_config_paths:   # optional; defaults to [.claude, .mcp.json]
  - .claude
  - .mcp.json
  - .entire              # opt in if this repo uses the entire.io tool
```

- Add a module default `DEFAULT_WORKTREE_CONFIG_PATHS = (".claude", ".mcp.json")` and a
  field `worktree_config_paths: tuple[str, ...] = DEFAULT_WORKTREE_CONFIG_PATHS` on the
  frozen `Repos` dataclass.
- In `load()`, read the optional `data.get("worktree_config_paths")`. Absent → use the
  default. Present → it must be a non-empty list of relative-path strings; reject a
  non-list value, non-string entries, absolute paths, and `..` components, each raising
  `RepoConfigError` with a message naming the on-disk problem (the same contract as the
  existing `repos:` key validation).
- Update `docs/repos.example.yml` with the commented optional block shown above.

## Docs (all four doc surfaces are in scope)
- **`docs/design-decisions.md` decision 10** ("Headless workers lose project-scoped
  hooks; the cause is named, not yet fixed"): change the decision from "cause named, not
  yet fixed" to "decided and implemented". Rewrite the title accordingly (drop "not yet
  fixed"). Keep the cause explanation and the reproduction snippet; replace the
  open-question section with the chosen mechanism — symlink a configurable set of project
  config (default `.claude` and `.mcp.json`, with `.entire` opt-in via `repos.yml`) — plus
  why symlink beat copy and `--settings`, and the parity it restores with a non-worktree
  run.
- **`README.md` "Debug capture"** (lines 158-168): the current claim that "project-scoped
  hooks ... don't load inside the worktree" is now false. Reframe it: workers inherit
  project config via symlinks, so `--debug-file` / `DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1` now *confirms*
  parity rather than documenting a gap. Edit line 160's parenthetical and the cross-
  reference to decision 10.
- **`README.md` Install** (the `repos.yml` block, lines 48-57): document the optional
  `worktree_config_paths` key — what it does (which project config is symlinked into each
  worktree to give parity), its default `[.claude, .mcp.json]`, and that tool-specific
  directories like `.entire` are opt-in. Add a short companion note (in Prerequisites or
  as a new "Worktree config parity" line) so the behaviour is discoverable, not buried in
  the example.
- **`docs/repos.example.yml`**: add the commented optional `worktree_config_paths` block
  shown under Configurability, with `.entire` as a commented opt-in example.

## Tests

Test fixtures use a real git repo in a temp dir, a fake `claude` executable, and a
monkeypatched Linear client — the existing patterns in this suite; no real API tokens are
spent.

`tests/test_repos.py` — config parsing for the new key:
- `worktree_config_paths` absent → `Repos.worktree_config_paths == (".claude", ".mcp.json")`.
- present custom list (including `.entire`) → parsed verbatim.
- malformed (non-list, non-string entry, absolute path, or `..`) → `RepoConfigError`.

`tests/test_worktree.py` (reuse the `_init_repo` helper; calls pass explicit `names`):
- `test_link_project_config_symlinks_gitignored_claude` — seed `.claude/settings.json`,
  gitignore `.claude`; assert `(wt/".claude").is_symlink()`, that `readlink` points to
  `repo/.claude`, and that `settings.json` reads through the link.
- `test_link_project_config_noop_when_no_claude` — no config → no link and no error.
- `test_link_project_config_skips_existing_tracked_entry` — tracked `.claude/` → not a
  symlink after `add`, content intact (proves the don't-clobber guard).
- `test_link_project_config_includes_mcp_and_entire_when_present` — gitignored `.mcp.json`
  and `.entire/` get symlinked; when absent → no link.
- `test_linked_config_is_gitignored_in_worktree` — `git status --porcelain` run from the
  worktree shows `.claude` is NOT untracked (proves `git add` won't stage it).
- `test_remove_preserves_symlink_target` — after `worktree.remove`, `repo/.claude` and its
  contents still exist (teardown follows the link but deletes nothing real).

`tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py` (fake-claude pattern): seed a gitignored
`.claude/settings.json` in the test repo; the fake `claude` records whether
`.claude/settings.json` is readable from its own cwd (`[ -r .claude/settings.json ]`).
Assert the marker says "present", proving the orchestrator links the config in before
spawning the worker — without spending tokens. This asserts the *precondition* for hook
loading (the config is resolvable from the worker's cwd), not that hooks actually fire.

Regression: the existing iteration and argv tests use repos with no `.claude/`, so
`link_project_config` is a clean no-op for them; the full suite must stay green. The worker
argv is untouched, so the `test_orchestrator_debug_capture_*` argv assertions are
unaffected.

## Linear issue to create (first execution step)

Plan mode is read-only, so creating this issue is the first step after approval (team
**Personal / ABA**, project **Autonomous cycle drain**). **One issue, not split:** the
slices are tightly coupled — the config key, the symlink function, and the orchestrator
wiring must land together to be end-to-end testable and mergeable — and the whole change
is ~200-300 lines of code (including tests and docs), a single reviewable batch. The five
acceptance criteria below are the sub-tasks, in execution order.

**Title:** Workers inherit the repo's project config inside worktrees

**Labels:** `repo:drain-cycle`, `model:sonnet`

**Outcome / user story:**
> As an operator running an unattended cycle drain, I want each worker to load the same
> project-scoped settings, hooks, agents, and skills as a normal Claude Code session at the
> repo root. The worker runs in a git worktree, which checks out only tracked files, so any
> gitignored `.claude/` config is currently missing and the tooling I rely on
> interactively — for example the `entire.io` checkpointing hooks — silently never runs.
> Symlinking that config into the worktree restores parity, so the same tooling works
> headless.

**Acceptance criteria (ordered sub-tasks):**
1. **Config** — `repos.yml` accepts an optional `worktree_config_paths` list (default
   `[.claude, .mcp.json]`); `repos.py` parses and validates it (`RepoConfigError` on a
   malformed value); `tests/test_repos.py` covers the default, a custom list, and
   malformed input.
2. **Symlink helper** — `worktree.link_project_config(repo, worktree_path, names)` symlinks
   each entry that is present in the repo and not already checked out in the worktree;
   `tests/test_worktree.py` covers symlink creation, the no-op case, skip-tracked,
   gitignored-in-worktree, and remove-preserves-target.
3. **Wiring** — the orchestrator calls the helper after `worktree.add`, inside the existing
   pre-spawn `try` block, passing `repos.worktree_config_paths`; an integration test
   asserts that the worker's cwd can resolve `.claude/settings.json`.
4. **Docs** — update all four surfaces listed in the Docs section above (design-decisions
   decision 10 flip, README debug-capture reframe, README config-key documentation, and
   `repos.example.yml`).
5. **Verify and ship** — full test suite green; run the manual `DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1` parity
   check; then follow the AGENTS.md completion sequence (review → fix → commit and push to
   main → Linear comment → Done).

**Model:** Sonnet. The plan is prescriptive — file paths, function signatures, and test
names are all named — so ambiguity is low and Sonnet (the repo's cost-efficient default
worker model) is sufficient. Switch to `model:opus` only if the operator wants extra care
on the decision-10 and README prose rewrites.

**Size estimate (against the per-issue caps of 8M tokens / 20 minutes):** ~200-300 lines of
code across 4 source files, 3 test files, and 3 docs. Expected run is ~2-4M tokens and
~10-15 minutes of wall-clock time — comfortably inside the caps, with the doc rewrites the
most token-variable part.

## Verification
1. `uv run pytest` — all tests green (existing plus new).
2. Manual parity check on this repo:
   ```bash
   DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1 drain-cycle   # against a cycle with a repo:drain-cycle issue
   ```
   Then diff the per-issue `~/.drain-cycle/runs/*-<issue>.debug.log` against the baseline
   in design-decisions decision 10: the worktree run should now watch the project
   `.claude/settings.json` and fire the `entire.io` SessionStart/SessionEnd hooks ("Entire
   CLI will link this conversation..."), matching the repo-root column of the decision-10
   comparison table.
3. Confirm that removing a completed issue's worktree left `<repo>/.claude` and
   `<repo>/.entire` intact (`ls` after a Done run).

## Critical files
- `drain_cycle/worktree.py` — add `link_project_config(repo, worktree_path, names)`
- `drain_cycle/repos.py` — `DEFAULT_WORKTREE_CONFIG_PATHS` + `Repos.worktree_config_paths`
  field + `load()` parsing/validation of the optional key
- `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:148` — call the helper after `worktree.add`, inside the
  pre-spawn `try`
- `docs/repos.example.yml` — document the optional `worktree_config_paths` block
- `tests/test_repos.py`, `tests/test_worktree.py`, `tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py` — new tests
- `docs/design-decisions.md` decision 10, `README.md` "Debug capture" — change gap → fix

  "content": "# Worktree config parity for drain-cycle workers\n\n## Context\n\n`drain-cycle` spawns each Claude Code worker with `cwd` set to a fresh git worktree\nat `<target-repo>/.worktrees/<issue-id>/`. A git worktree checks out only *tracked*\nfiles, and this repo gitignores all project-scoped Claude config — the `.claude/`\ndirectory (settings, hooks, agents, skills, commands) and any root `.mcp.json`\n(`.gitignore:23-24` ignore `.entire` and `.claude`). So the worktree has no `.claude/`:\nClaude Code treats the worktree as the project root, finds no project config there, and\nloads only user-scoped config (the operator's `~/.claude/`). Project hooks — for example\nthe checkpointing hooks that the `entire.io` tool registers in `.claude/settings.json` —\nsilently never fire. `docs/design-decisions.md` decision 10 records this as an open\nquestion (decision 10 is titled \"Headless workers lose project-scoped hooks; the cause is\nnamed, not yet fixed\"). The operator has now decided to fix it.\n\nGoal: a worker in the worktree loads the same project-scoped settings, hooks, agents,\nskills, and MCP config as a normal in-repo run on a non-worktree branch — true\nenvironment parity.\n\n**Decision:** symlink the repo's real config dirs and files into each worktree. A normal\nin-repo run reads *and writes* the repo's actual config; symlinking reproduces that\nexactly, so a stateful hook — such as the `entire.io` checkpointing hook, which links a\nsession to the commit it produces — works and persists, because the commit it links to\ngets pushed. Accepted tradeoff: the worker runs `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, so it\nshares — and could mutate — the live config dirs, exactly as a non-worktree run would.\n\nThe symlinked set is **configurable**. It defaults to `[.claude, .mcp.json]`, which suits\nany repo. `.entire` is not a default, because not every repo installs the `entire.io`\ntool; an operator who uses it adds `.entire` via config. See Configurability below.\n\n**Rejected alternatives.** `--settings <repo>/.claude/settings.json` loads only one file\nand leaves four other config surfaces broken: `settings.local.json`, project\nagents/skills/commands, hook scripts whose paths are relative to `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`,\nand `.mcp.json`. Copying the config (rather than symlinking) isolates the worker but\ndiscards the `entire.io` checkpoint writes when the worktree is torn down. Tracking\n`.claude/` in git would commit machine-specific config.\n\n## Implementation\n\n### 1. `drain_cycle/worktree.py` — new symlink step\n\nAdd a pure function next to the existing `add`/`remove` functions. It takes the names to\nlink; the default for those names lives in the config layer, `repos.py` (see\nConfigurability):\n\n```python\ndef link_project_config(\n    repo: Path, worktree_path: Path, names: Iterable[str]\n) -> list[Path]:\n    \"\"\"Symlink gitignored project-scoped config from ``repo`` into the\n    worktree so a worker sees the same settings/hooks/agents/skills/MCP as an\n    interactive session at the repo root. Skips entries absent in ``repo`` or\n    already present in the worktree (a tracked file git already checked out).\n    Returns the links created.\"\"\"\n```\n\nBehaviour, for each name in `names`:\n- `source = repo.resolve() / name` — resolve `repo` to an absolute path so the link target\n  stays valid regardless of the worker's cwd.\n- Skip if `not source.exists()` — a clean no-op for repos without that config.\n- Skip if `os.path.lexists(worktree_path / name)` — use `lexists`, not `exists`, so an\n  entry git already checked out is never clobbered and a dangling link is still detected.\n- Otherwise `os.symlink(source, worktree_path / name)`.\n\nThese symlinked entries are gitignored in the worktree (the worktree shares the repo's\ntracked `.gitignore`), so the worker's `git add` never stages them. `.mcp.json` doesn't\nexist in this repo (clean no-op here) but is included so the function is correct on repos\nthat do have one.\n\n### 2. `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py` — wire it in\n\nIn `run()`, inside the existing pre-spawn `try` block (orchestrator.py:147-152), call the\nnew step right after the worktree is created:\n\n```python\nworktree_path = worktree.add(target_repo, identifier)\nworktree.link_project_config(                              # NEW\n    target_repo, worktree_path, repos.worktree_config_paths\n)\nlinear.set_state(issue[\"id\"], _IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME)\n```\n\n(`repos` is the loaded `Repos` object the orchestrator already holds to call\n`repos.resolve(issue)`; it now also carries `worktree_config_paths`.)\n\nPlacing the call inside that `try` reuses the existing pre-spawn failure handling: any\nerror becomes a recorded \"setup failed\" halt with the planned worktree path\n(orchestrator.py:153-174), so no new error branch is needed. Teardown needs no new code.\n`git worktree remove` on a Done issue (orchestrator.py) deletes the worktree directory and\nits *symlinks* but not the symlink targets, so the operator's real `.claude/` and\n`.entire/` survive. On a halt, the worktree — with its live symlinks — is left in place\nfor inspection.\n\n### Edge cases\n- **No `.claude/` in the source repo** → no-op (the `exists()` guard).\n- **Partially tracked `.claude/`** → the top-level `lexists` guard skips it, so git's\n  checkout is never clobbered. The granularity is the top-level entry; per-file parity for\n  a partially-tracked directory is out of scope (document it, don't code it — YAGNI).\n- **Dangling or pre-existing link** → `lexists` detects it, so it is not created twice.\n- **Untracked, non-ignored root `.mcp.json`** (no such repo today) → a worker running\n  `git add .` could stage it; document that project MCP config should be either tracked or\n  gitignored, rather than coding a guard against this.\n\n### Configurability — `drain_cycle/repos.py`\n\nThe path set is an optional top-level key in the existing `repos.yml` — not a new file and\nnot a command-line flag. 1	# Worktree config parity for drain-cycle workers
2	
3	## Context
4	
5	`drain-cycle` spawns each Claude Code worker with `cwd` set to a fresh git worktree
6	at `<target-repo>/.worktrees/<issue-id>/`. Git worktrees check out only *tracked*
7	files, and this repo blanket-gitignores project-scoped Claude config — `.claude/`
8	(settings, hooks, agents, skills, commands) plus a possible root `.mcp.json`
9	(`.gitignore:23-24` ignore `.entire` and `.claude`). So the worktree has no `.claude/`:
10	Claude Code resolves the project root to the worktree, finds nothing, and loads
11	user-scoped config only. Project hooks (entire.io's checkpointing, registered in
12	`.claude/settings.json`) silently never fire. `docs/design-decisions.md` §10 records
13	this as an open question; the operator has now decided to **fix it**.
14	
15	Goal: a worker in the worktree loads the same project-scoped settings/hooks/agents/
16	skills/MCP as a normal in-repo run on a non-worktree branch — true environment parity.
17	
18	**Decision (operator-confirmed):** symlink the repo's real config dirs/files into each
19	worktree. A normal in-repo run reads *and writes* the repo's actual config; symlinking
20	reproduces that exactly, so a stateful hook like entire's checkpointing works and persists
21	(it links to the commit, which gets pushed). Accepted tradeoff: the worker runs
22	`--dangerously-skip-permissions` and so shares — and could mutate — the live config dirs,
23	exactly as a non-worktree run would.
24	
25	The symlinked set is **configurable** (operator round-2 call). Default `[.claude, .mcp.json]`
26	— sensible for any repo. `.entire` is *not* a default (not everyone installs entire); this
27	operator adds it via config. See Configurability below.
28	
29	Rejected: `--settings <repo>/.claude/settings.json` loads only one file and leaves four
30	other surfaces broken (`settings.local.json`, project agents/skills/commands,
31	`$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`-relative hook scripts, `.mcp.json`). Also rejected: copy (isolates
32	the worker but discards entire's checkpoint writes on teardown) and tracking `.claude/`
33	in git (commits machine-specific config).
34	
35	## Implementation
36	
37	### 1. `drain_cycle/worktree.py` — new symlink step
38	
39	Add a pure function next to `add`/`remove` that takes the names to link (the default
40	lives in the config layer, `repos.py` — see Configurability):
41	
42	```python
43	def link_project_config(
44	    repo: Path, worktree_path: Path, names: Iterable[str]
45	) -> list[Path]:
46	    """Symlink gitignored project-scoped config from ``repo`` into the
47	    worktree so a worker sees the same settings/hooks/agents/skills/MCP as an
48	    interactive session at the repo root. Skips entries absent in ``repo`` or
49	    already present in the worktree (a tracked file git already checked out).
50	    Returns the links created."""
51	```
52	
53	Behavior, per name in `names`:
54	- `source = repo.resolve() / name` — resolve `repo` absolute so the link target stays
55	  valid regardless of the worker's cwd.
56	- Skip if `not source.exists()` (clean no-op for repos without that config).
57	- Skip if `os.path.lexists(worktree_path / name)` — `lexists`, not `exists`, so a
58	  tracked entry git already checked out is never clobbered and a dangling link is
59	  detected.
60	- Otherwise `os.symlink(source, worktree_path / name)`.
61	
62	These symlinked entries are gitignored in the worktree (the worktree shares the repo's
63	tracked `.gitignore`), so the worker's `git add` never stages them. `.mcp.json` doesn't
64	exist in this repo (clean no-op) but is included for correctness on other repos.
65	
66	### 2. `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py` — wire it in
67	
68	In `run()`, inside the existing pre-spawn `try` (orchestrator.py:147-152), call the new
69	step right after the worktree is created:
70	
71	```python
72	worktree_path = worktree.add(target_repo, identifier)
73	worktree.link_project_config(                              # NEW
74	    target_repo, worktree_path, repos.worktree_config_paths
75	)
76	linear.set_state(issue["id"], _IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME)
77	```
78	
79	(`repos` is the loaded `Repos` object the orchestrator already holds for
80	`repos.resolve(issue)`; it now also carries `worktree_config_paths`.)
81	
82	Placing it inside that `try` reuses the established failure contract: any error becomes a
83	recorded "setup failed" halt with the planned worktree path (orchestrator.py:153-174) —
84	no new error branch. Teardown needs no new code: `git worktree remove` on Done
85	(orchestrator.py) deletes the worktree dir and its *symlinks* but not their targets, so
86	the operator's real `.claude/`/`.entire/` survive. On halt the worktree, with live
87	symlinks, is left in place for inspection.
88	
89	### Edge cases
90	- **No `.claude/` in source repo** → no-op (the `exists()` guard).
91	- **Partially tracked `.claude/`** → the top-level `lexists` guard skips it, never
92	  clobbering git's checkout. Granularity is the top-level entry; per-file parity for
93	  partially-tracked dirs is out of scope (document, don't code — YAGNI).
94	- **Dangling/pre-existing link** → `lexists` detects it; not double-created.
95	- **Untracked, non-ignored root `.mcp.json`** (no such repo today) → a worker `git add .`
96	  could stage it; document that project MCP config should be tracked or gitignored rather
97	  than coding a check.
98	
99	### Configurability — `drain_cycle/repos.py`
100	
101	The path set is an optional top-level key in the existing `repos.yml`, not a new file or
102	a flag (matches design-decision 9: YAML only, no argparse). `repos.yml` already owns
103	repo/worktree setup, is always loaded, and its `load()` ignores unknown top-level keys
104	today — so this is a backward-compatible extension.
105	
106	```yaml
107	repos:
108	  drain-cycle: /Users/you/src/drain-cycle
109	
110	worktree_config_paths:   # optional; defaults to [.claude, .mcp.json]
111	  - .claude
112	  - .mcp.json
113	  - .entire              # this operator uses entire, so opts it in
114	```
115	
116	- Add a module default `DEFAULT_WORKTREE_CONFIG_PATHS = (".claude", ".mcp.json")` and a
117	  field `worktree_config_paths: tuple[str, ...] = DEFAULT_WORKTREE_CONFIG_PATHS` on the
118	  frozen `Repos` dataclass.
119	- In `load()`, read optional `data.get("worktree_config_paths")`. Absent → default. Present
120	  → must be a non-empty list of relative-path strings; reject non-list, non-string entries,
121	  absolute paths, and `..` components, each raising `RepoConfigError` with a message naming
122	  the on-disk problem (same contract as the existing `repos:` validation).
123	- Update `docs/repos.example.yml` with the commented optional block above.
124	
125	## Docs (all doc surfaces in scope)
126	- **`docs/design-decisions.md` §10**: flip from "the cause is named, not yet fixed" /
127	  "Open question — fix it, or accept it?" to **decided + implemented**. Reword the title
128	  ("...not yet fixed"). Keep the cause explanation and reproduction snippet; replace the
129	  open-question section with the chosen mechanism (symlink a configurable set of project
130	  config — default `.claude`/`.mcp.json`, `.entire` opt-in via `repos.yml`), why symlink
131	  beat copy and `--settings`, and the restored parity with a non-worktree run.
132	- **`README.md` "Debug capture"** (lines 158-168): the standing claim that "project-scoped
133	  hooks ... don't load inside the worktree" is now false. Reframe: workers inherit project
134	  config via symlinks, so `--debug-file`/`DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1` now *confirms* parity rather
135	  than documenting a gap. Edit line 160's parenthetical and the §10 cross-ref.
136	- **`README.md` Install** (the `repos.yml` block, lines 48-57): document the optional
137	  `worktree_config_paths` key — what it does (which project config is symlinked into each
138	  worktree for parity), its default `[.claude, .mcp.json]`, and that tool-specific dirs
139	  like `.entire` are opt-in. Add a short companion note (Prerequisites or a new "Worktree
140	  config parity" line) so the behaviour is discoverable, not buried in the example.
141	- **`docs/repos.example.yml`**: add the commented optional `worktree_config_paths` block
142	  shown under Configurability, with `.entire` as a commented opt-in example.
143	
144	## Tests (real git tmp repo + fake claude + monkeypatched Linear — existing patterns; no real tokens)
145	
146	`tests/test_repos.py` — config parsing for the new key:
147	- `worktree_config_paths` absent → `Repos.worktree_config_paths == (".claude", ".mcp.json")`.
148	- present custom list (incl. `.entire`) → parsed verbatim.
149	- malformed (non-list, non-string entry, absolute path, `..`) → `RepoConfigError`.
150	
151	`tests/test_worktree.py` (reuse `_init_repo`; calls pass explicit `names`):
152	- `test_link_project_config_symlinks_gitignored_claude` — seed `.claude/settings.json`,
153	  gitignore `.claude`; assert `(wt/".claude").is_symlink()`, `readlink` → `repo/.claude`,
154	  and `settings.json` reads through the link.
155	- `test_link_project_config_noop_when_no_claude` — no config → no link, no error.
156	- `test_link_project_config_skips_existing_tracked_entry` — tracked `.claude/` → not a
157	  symlink after `add`; content intact (don't-clobber).
158	- `test_link_project_config_includes_mcp_and_entire_when_present` — gitignored `.mcp.json`
159	  and `.entire/` get symlinked; absent → no link.
160	- `test_linked_config_is_gitignored_in_worktree` — `git status --porcelain` from the
161	  worktree shows `.claude` is NOT untracked (proves `git add` won't stage it).
162	- `test_remove_preserves_symlink_target` — after `worktree.remove`, `repo/.claude` and its
163	  contents still exist (teardown follows the link to delete nothing real).
164	
165	`tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py` (fake-claude pattern): seed a gitignored
166	`.claude/settings.json` in the test repo; the fake `claude` records whether
167	`.claude/settings.json` is readable from its own cwd (`[ -r .claude/settings.json ]`).
168	Assert the marker says "present", proving the orchestrator links config in before spawn
169	without spending tokens. This asserts the *precondition* for hook loading (resolvability
170	from worker cwd), not live hook firing.
171	
172	Regression: existing iteration/argv tests use repos with no `.claude/`, so
173	`link_project_config` is a clean no-op; the full suite must stay green. Worker argv is
174	untouched, so the `test_orchestrator_debug_capture_*` argv assertions are unaffected.
175	
176	## Linear issue to create (first execution step)
177	
178	Plan mode is read-only, so creating this is the first step after approval (team **Personal /
179	ABA**, project **Autonomous cycle drain**). **One issue, not split:** the slices are tightly
180	coupled — the config key, the symlink function, and the orchestrator wiring must land
181	together to be end-to-end testable and mergeable, and the whole change is ~200-300 LOC
182	(incl. tests + docs), a single reviewable batch. The five ACs below are the sub-tasks in
183	execution order.
184	
185	**Title:** Workers inherit the repo's project config inside worktrees
186	
187	**Labels:** `repo:drain-cycle`, `model:sonnet`
188	
189	**Outcome / user story:**
190	> As an operator running an unattended drain, I want each worker to load the same
191	> project-scoped settings, hooks, agents and skills as a normal session at the repo root —
192	> so the tooling I rely on interactively (e.g. entire's checkpointing) also works headless,
193	> instead of silently dropping out because the worktree checkout omits gitignored `.claude/`.
194	
195	**Acceptance criteria (ordered sub-tasks):**
196	1. **Config** — `repos.yml` accepts an optional `worktree_config_paths` list (default
197	   `[.claude, .mcp.json]`); `repos.py` parses + validates it (`RepoConfigError` on
198	   malformed); `tests/test_repos.py` covers default / custom / malformed.
199	2. **Symlink helper** — `worktree.link_project_config(repo, worktree_path, names)` symlinks
200	   present, not-already-checked-out entries; `tests/test_worktree.py` covers symlink, no-op,
201	   skip-tracked, gitignored-in-worktree, and remove-preserves-target.
202	3. **Wiring** — orchestrator calls it after `worktree.add`, inside the pre-spawn `try`,
203	   passing `repos.worktree_config_paths`; integration test asserts the worker's cwd resolves
204	   `.claude/settings.json`.
205	4. **Docs** — all four surfaces in the Docs section (design-decisions §10 flip, README
206	   debug-capture reframe, README config-key documentation, `repos.example.yml`).
207	5. **Verify + ship** — full suite green; manual `DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1` parity check; then the
208	   AGENTS.md completion sequence (review → fix → commit+push to main → Linear comment → Done).
209	
210	**Model:** Sonnet. The plan is prescriptive (file paths, signatures, test names all named),
211	so ambiguity is low and Sonnet — the repo's cost-efficient default — is sufficient. Bump to
212	`model:opus` only if the operator wants extra care on the §10 / README prose rewrites.
213	
214	**Size estimate (against the 8M-token / 20-min per-issue caps):** ~200-300 LOC across 4
215	source files + 3 test files + 3 docs. Expected run ~2-4M tokens and ~10-15 min wall-clock —
216	comfortably inside the caps, with the doc rewrites the most token-variable part.
217	
218	## Verification
219	1. `uv run pytest` — all tests green (existing + new).
220	2. Manual parity check on this repo:
221	   ```bash
222	   DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1 drain-cycle   # against a cycle with a repo:drain-cycle issue
223	   ```
224	   Then diff the per-issue `~/.drain-cycle/runs/*-<issue>.debug.log` against the §10
225	   baseline: the worktree run should now watch project `.claude/settings.json` and fire
226	   entire's SessionStart/SessionEnd hooks ("Entire CLI will link this conversation...") —
227	   matching the repo-root column of the §10 table.
228	3. Confirm a completed issue's worktree removal left `<repo>/.claude` and `<repo>/.entire`
229	   intact (`ls` after a Done run).
230	
231	## Critical files
232	- `drain_cycle/worktree.py` — add `link_project_config(repo, worktree_path, names)`
233	- `drain_cycle/repos.py` — `DEFAULT_WORKTREE_CONFIG_PATHS` + `Repos.worktree_config_paths`
234	  field + `load()` parsing/validation of the optional key
235	- `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:148` — call it after `worktree.add`, inside the pre-spawn try
236	- `docs/repos.example.yml` — document the optional `worktree_config_paths` block
237	- `tests/test_repos.py`, `tests/test_worktree.py`, `tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py` — new tests

calls pass explicit `names`):\n- `test_link_project_config_symlinks_gitignored_claude` — seed `.claude/settings.json`,\n  gitignore `.claude`; assert `(wt/\".claude\").is_symlink()`, that `readlink` points to\n  `repo/.claude`, and that `settings.json` reads through the link.\n- `test_link_project_config_noop_when_no_claude` — no config → no link and no error.\n- `test_link_project_config_skips_existing_tracked_entry` — tracked `.claude/` → not a\n  symlink after `add`, content intact (proves the don't-clobber guard).\n- `test_link_project_config_includes_mcp_and_entire_when_present` — gitignored `.mcp.json`\n  and `.entire/` get symlinked; the fake `claude` records whether\n`.claude/settings.json` is readable from its own cwd (`[ -r .claude/settings.json ]`).\nAssert the marker says \"present\", proving the orchestrator links the config in before\nspawning the worker — without spending tokens. This asserts the *precondition* for hook\nloading (the config is resolvable from the worker's cwd), not that hooks actually fire.\n\nRegression: the existing iteration and argv tests use repos with no `.claude/`, so\n`link_project_config` is a clean no-op for them; the full suite must stay green. The worker\nargv is untouched, so the `test_orchestrator_debug_capture_*` argv assertions are\nunaffected.\n\n## Linear issue to create (first execution step)\n\nPlan mode is read-only, so creating this issue is the first step after approval (team\n**Personal / ABA**, project **Autonomous cycle drain**). **One issue, not split:** the\nslices are tightly coupled — the config key, the symlink function, and the orchestrator\nwiring must land together to be end-to-end testable and mergeable — and the whole change\nis ~200-300 lines of code (including tests and docs), a single reviewable batch. The five\nacceptance criteria below are the sub-tasks, in execution order.\n\n**Title:** Workers inherit the repo's project config inside worktrees\n\n**Labels:** `repo:drain-cycle`, `model:sonnet`\n\n**Outcome / user story:**\n> As an operator running an unattended cycle drain, I want each worker to load the same\n> project-scoped settings, hooks, agents, and skills as a normal Claude Code session at the\n> repo root. The worker runs in a git worktree, which checks out only tracked files, so any\n> gitignored `.claude/` config is currently missing and the tooling I rely on\n> interactively — for example the `entire.io` checkpointing hooks — silently never runs.\n> Symlinking that config into the worktree restores parity, so the same tooling works\n> headless.\n\n**Acceptance criteria (ordered sub-tasks):**\n1. **Config** — `repos.yml` accepts an optional `worktree_config_paths` list (default\n   `[.claude, .mcp.json]`); `repos.py` parses and validates it (`RepoConfigError` on a\n   malformed value); `tests/test_repos.py` covers the default, a custom list, and\n   malformed input.\n2. **Symlink helper** — `worktree.link_project_config(repo, worktree_path, names)` symlinks\n   each entry that is present in the repo and not already checked out in the worktree;\n   `tests/test_worktree.py` covers symlink creation, the no-op case, skip-tracked,\n   gitignored-in-worktree, and remove-preserves-target.\n3. **Wiring** — the orchestrator calls the helper after `worktree.add`, inside the existing\n   pre-spawn `try` block, passing `repos.worktree_config_paths`; an integration test\n   asserts that the worker's cwd can resolve `.claude/settings.json`.\n4. **Docs** — update all four surfaces listed in the Docs section above (design-decisions\n   decision 10 flip, README debug-capture reframe, README config-key documentation, and\n   `repos.example.yml`).\n5. **Verify and ship** — full test suite green; run the manual `DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1` parity\n   check; then follow the AGENTS.md completion sequence (review → fix → commit and push to\n   main → Linear comment → Done).\n\n**Model:** Sonnet. Switch to `model:opus` only if the operator wants extra care\non the decision-10 and README prose rewrites.\n\n**Size estimate (against the per-issue caps of 8M tokens / 20 minutes):** ~200-300 lines of\ncode across 4 source files, 3 test files, and 3 docs. Manual parity check on this repo:\n   ```bash\n   DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1 drain-cycle   # against a cycle with a repo:drain-cycle issue\n   ```\n   Then diff the per-issue `~/.drain-cycle/runs/*-<issue>.debug.log` against the baseline\n   in design-decisions decision 10: the worktree run should now watch the project\n   `.claude/settings.json` and fire the `entire.io` SessionStart/SessionEnd hooks (\"Entire\n   CLI will link this conversation...\"), matching the repo-root column of the decision-10\n   comparison table.\n3. Confirm that removing a completed issue's worktree left `<repo>/.claude` and\n   `<repo>/.entire` intact (`ls` after a Done run).\n\n## Critical files\n- `drain_cycle/worktree.py` — add `link_project_config(repo, worktree_path, names)`\n- `drain_cycle/repos.py` — `DEFAULT_WORKTREE_CONFIG_PATHS` + `Repos.worktree_config_paths`\n  field + `load()` parsing/validation of the optional key\n- `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:148` — call the helper after `worktree.add`, inside the\n  pre-spawn `try`\n- `docs/repos.example.yml` — document the optional `worktree_config_paths` block\n- `tests/test_repos.py`, `tests/test_worktree.py`, `tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py` — new tests\n- `docs/design-decisions.md` decision 10, `README.md` \"Debug capture\" — change gap → fix\n"

  "plan": "# Worktree config parity for drain-cycle workers\n\n## Context\n\n`drain-cycle` spawns each Claude Code worker with `cwd` set to a fresh git worktree\nat `<target-repo>/.worktrees/<issue-id>/`. A git worktree checks out only *tracked*\nfiles, and this repo gitignores all project-scoped Claude config — the `.claude/`\ndirectory (settings, hooks, agents, skills, commands) and any root `.mcp.json`\n(`.gitignore:23-24` ignore `.entire` and `.claude`). So the worktree has no `.claude/`:\nClaude Code treats the worktree as the project root, finds no project config there, and\nloads only user-scoped config (the operator's `~/.claude/`). Project hooks — for example\nthe checkpointing hooks that the `entire.io` tool registers in `.claude/settings.json` —\nsilently never fire. `docs/design-decisions.md` decision 10 records this as an open\nquestion (decision 10 is titled \"Headless workers lose project-scoped hooks; the cause is\nnamed, not yet fixed\"). The operator has now decided to fix it.\n\nGoal: a worker in the worktree loads the same project-scoped settings, hooks, agents,\nskills, and MCP config as a normal in-repo run on a non-worktree branch — true\nenvironment parity.\n\n**Decision:** symlink the repo's real config dirs and files into each worktree. A normal\nin-repo run reads *and writes* the repo's actual config; symlinking reproduces that\nexactly, so a stateful hook — such as the `entire.io` checkpointing hook, which links a\nsession to the commit it produces — works and persists, because the commit it links to\ngets pushed. Accepted tradeoff: the worker runs `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, so it\nshares — and could mutate — the live config dirs, exactly as a non-worktree run would.\n\nThe symlinked set is **configurable**. It defaults to `[.claude, .mcp.json]`, which suits\nany repo. `.entire` is not a default, because not every repo installs the `entire.io`\ntool; an operator who uses it adds `.entire` via config. See Configurability below.\n\n**Rejected alternatives.** `--settings <repo>/.claude/settings.json` loads only one file\nand leaves four other config surfaces broken: `settings.local.json`, project\nagents/skills/commands, hook scripts whose paths are relative to `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`,\nand `.mcp.json`. Copying the config (rather than symlinking) isolates the worker but\ndiscards the `entire.io` checkpoint writes when the worktree is torn down. Tracking\n`.claude/` in git would commit machine-specific config.\n\n## Implementation\n\n### 1. `drain_cycle/worktree.py` — new symlink step\n\nAdd a pure function next to the existing `add`/`remove` functions. It takes the names to\nlink; the default for those names lives in the config layer, `repos.py` (see\nConfigurability):\n\n```python\ndef link_project_config(\n    repo: Path, worktree_path: Path, names: Iterable[str]\n) -> list[Path]:\n    \"\"\"Symlink gitignored project-scoped config from ``repo`` into the\n    worktree so a worker sees the same settings/hooks/agents/skills/MCP as an\n    interactive session at the repo root. Skips entries absent in ``repo`` or\n    already present in the worktree (a tracked file git already checked out).\n    Returns the links created.\"\"\"\n```\n\nBehaviour, for each name in `names`:\n- `source = repo.resolve() / name` — resolve `repo` to an absolute path so the link target\n  stays valid regardless of the worker's cwd.\n- Skip if `not source.exists()` — a clean no-op for repos without that config.\n- Skip if `os.path.lexists(worktree_path / name)` — use `lexists`, not `exists`, so an\n  entry git already checked out is never clobbered and a dangling link is still detected.\n- Otherwise `os.symlink(source, worktree_path / name)`.\n\nThese symlinked entries are gitignored in the worktree (the worktree shares the repo's\ntracked `.gitignore`), so the worker's `git add` never stages them. `.mcp.json` doesn't\nexist in this repo (clean no-op here) but is included so the function is correct on repos\nthat do have one.\n\n### 2. `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py` — wire it in\n\nIn `run()`, inside the existing pre-spawn `try` block (orchestrator.py:147-152), call the\nnew step right after the worktree is created:\n\n```python\nworktree_path = worktree.add(target_repo, identifier)\nworktree.link_project_config(                              # NEW\n    target_repo, worktree_path, repos.worktree_config_paths\n)\nlinear.set_state(issue[\"id\"], _IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME)\n```\n\n(`repos` is the loaded `Repos` object the orchestrator already holds to call\n`repos.resolve(issue)`; it now also carries `worktree_config_paths`.)\n\nPlacing the call inside that `try` reuses the existing pre-spawn failure handling: any\nerror becomes a recorded \"setup failed\" halt with the planned worktree path\n(orchestrator.py:153-174), so no new error branch is needed. Teardown needs no new code.\n`git worktree remove` on a Done issue (orchestrator.py) deletes the worktree directory and\nits *symlinks* but not the symlink targets, so the operator's real `.claude/` and\n`.entire/` survive. On a halt, the worktree — with its live symlinks — is left in place\nfor inspection.\n\n### Edge cases\n- **No `.claude/` in the source repo** → no-op (the `exists()` guard).\n- **Partially tracked `.claude/`** → the top-level `lexists` guard skips it, so git's\n  checkout is never clobbered. The granularity is the top-level entry; per-file parity for\n  a partially-tracked directory is out of scope (document it, don't code it — YAGNI).\n- **Dangling or pre-existing link** → `lexists` detects it, so it is not created twice.\n- **Untracked, non-ignored root `.mcp.json`** (no such repo today) → a worker running\n  `git add .` could stage it; document that project MCP config should be either tracked or\n  gitignored, rather than coding a guard against this.\n\n### Configurability — `drain_cycle/repos.py`\n\nThe path set is an optional top-level key in the existing `repos.yml` — not a new file and\nnot a command-line flag. This follows the established config convention (design-decisions\ndecision 9 deliberately keeps configuration in YAML rather than adding an argument parser).\n`repos.yml` already owns repo and worktree setup, is always loaded, and its `load()`\nalready ignores unknown top-level keys, so adding this key is backward-compatible.\n\n```yaml\nrepos:\n  drain-cycle: /Users/you/src/drain-cycle\n\nworktree_config_paths:   # optional; defaults to [.claude, .mcp.json]\n  - .claude\n  - .mcp.json\n  - .entire              # opt in if this repo uses the entire.io tool\n```\n\n- Add a module default `DEFAULT_WORKTREE_CONFIG_PATHS = (\".claude\", \".mcp.json\")` and a\n  field `worktree_config_paths: tuple[str, ...] = DEFAULT_WORKTREE_CONFIG_PATHS` on the\n  frozen `Repos` dataclass.\n- In `load()`, read the optional `data.get(\"worktree_config_paths\")`. ## Approved Plan (edited by user):
# Worktree config parity for drain-cycle workers

## Context

`drain-cycle` spawns each Claude Code worker with `cwd` set to a fresh git worktree
at `<target-repo>/.worktrees/<issue-id>/`. A git worktree checks out only *tracked*
files, and this repo gitignores all project-scoped Claude config — the `.claude/`
directory (settings, hooks, agents, skills, commands) and any root `.mcp.json`
(`.gitignore:23-24` ignore `.entire` and `.claude`). So the worktree has no `.claude/`:
Claude Code treats the worktree as the project root, finds no project config there, and
loads only user-scoped config (the operator's `~/.claude/`). Project hooks — for example
the checkpointing hooks that the `entire.io` tool registers in `.claude/settings.json` —
silently never fire. `docs/design-decisions.md` decision 10 records this as an open
question (decision 10 is titled "Headless workers lose project-scoped hooks; the cause is
named, not yet fixed"). The operator has now decided to fix it.

Goal: a worker in the worktree loads the same project-scoped settings, hooks, agents,
skills, and MCP config as a normal in-repo run on a non-worktree branch — true
environment parity.

**Decision:** symlink the repo's real config dirs and files into each worktree. A normal
in-repo run reads *and writes* the repo's actual config; symlinking reproduces that
exactly, so a stateful hook — such as the `entire.io` checkpointing hook, which links a
session to the commit it produces — works and persists, because the commit it links to
gets pushed. Accepted tradeoff: the worker runs `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, so it
shares — and could mutate — the live config dirs, exactly as a non-worktree run would.

The symlinked set is **configurable**. It defaults to `[.claude, .mcp.json]`, which suits
any repo. `.entire` is not a default, because not every repo installs the `entire.io`
tool; an operator who uses it adds `.entire` via config. See Configurability below.

**Rejected alternatives.** `--settings <repo>/.claude/settings.json` loads only one file
and leaves four other config surfaces broken: `settings.local.json`, project
agents/skills/commands, hook scripts whose paths are relative to `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`,
and `.mcp.json`. Copying the config (rather than symlinking) isolates the worker but
discards the `entire.io` checkpoint writes when the worktree is torn down. Tracking
`.claude/` in git would commit machine-specific config.

## Implementation

### 1. `drain_cycle/worktree.py` — new symlink step

Add a pure function next to the existing `add`/`remove` functions. It takes the names to
link; the default for those names lives in the config layer, `repos.py` (see
Configurability):

```python
def link_project_config(
    repo: Path, worktree_path: Path, names: Iterable[str]
) -> list[Path]:
    """Symlink gitignored project-scoped config from ``repo`` into the
    worktree so a worker sees the same settings/hooks/agents/skills/MCP as an
    interactive session at the repo root. Skips entries absent in ``repo`` or
    already present in the worktree (a tracked file git already checked out).
    Returns the links created."""
```

Behaviour, for each name in `names`:
- `source = repo.resolve() / name` — resolve `repo` to an absolute path so the link target
  stays valid regardless of the worker's cwd.
- Skip if `not source.exists()` — a clean no-op for repos without that config.
- Skip if `os.path.lexists(worktree_path / name)` — use `lexists`, not `exists`, so an
  entry git already checked out is never clobbered and a dangling link is still detected.
- Otherwise `os.symlink(source, worktree_path / name)`.

These symlinked entries are gitignored in the worktree (the worktree shares the repo's
tracked `.gitignore`), so the worker's `git add` never stages them. `.mcp.json` doesn't
exist in this repo (clean no-op here) but is included so the function is correct on repos
that do have one.

### 2. `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py` — wire it in

In `run()`, inside the existing pre-spawn `try` block (orchestrator.py:147-152), call the
new step right after the worktree is created:

```python
worktree_path = worktree.add(target_repo, identifier)
worktree.link_project_config(                              # NEW
    target_repo, worktree_path, repos.worktree_config_paths
)
linear.set_state(issue["id"], _IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME)
```

(`repos` is the loaded `Repos` object the orchestrator already holds to call
`repos.resolve(issue)`; it now also carries `worktree_config_paths`.)

Placing the call inside that `try` reuses the existing pre-spawn failure handling: any
error becomes a recorded "setup failed" halt with the planned worktree path
(orchestrator.py:153-174), so no new error branch is needed. Teardown needs no new code.
`git worktree remove` on a Done issue (orchestrator.py) deletes the worktree directory and
its *symlinks* but not the symlink targets, so the operator's real `.claude/` and
`.entire/` survive. On a halt, the worktree — with its live symlinks — is left in place
for inspection.

### Edge cases
- **No `.claude/` in the source repo** → no-op (the `exists()` guard).
- **Partially tracked `.claude/`** → the top-level `lexists` guard skips it, so git's
  checkout is never clobbered. The granularity is the top-level entry; per-file parity for
  a partially-tracked directory is out of scope (document it, don't code it — YAGNI).
- **Dangling or pre-existing link** → `lexists` detects it, so it is not created twice.
- **Untracked, non-ignored root `.mcp.json`** (no such repo today) → a worker running
  `git add .` could stage it; document that project MCP config should be either tracked or
  gitignored, rather than coding a guard against this.

### Configurability — `drain_cycle/repos.py`

The path set is an optional top-level key in the existing `repos.yml` — not a new file and
not a command-line flag. This follows the established config convention (design-decisions
decision 9 deliberately keeps configuration in YAML rather than adding an argument parser).
`repos.yml` already owns repo and worktree setup, is always loaded, and its `load()`
already ignores unknown top-level keys, so adding this key is backward-compatible.

```yaml
repos:
  drain-cycle: /Users/you/src/drain-cycle

worktree_config_paths:   # optional; defaults to [.claude, .mcp.json]
  - .claude
  - .mcp.json
  - .entire              # opt in if this repo uses the entire.io tool
```

- Add a module default `DEFAULT_WORKTREE_CONFIG_PATHS = (".claude", ".mcp.json")` and a
  field `worktree_config_paths: tuple[str, ...] = DEFAULT_WORKTREE_CONFIG_PATHS` on the
  frozen `Repos` dataclass.
- In `load()`, read the optional `data.get("worktree_config_paths")`. Absent → use the
  default. Present → it must be a non-empty list of relative-path strings; reject a
  non-list value, non-string entries, absolute paths, and `..` components, each raising
  `RepoConfigError` with a message naming the on-disk problem (the same contract as the
  existing `repos:` key validation).
- Update `docs/repos.example.yml` with the commented optional block shown above.

## Docs (all four doc surfaces are in scope)
- **`docs/design-decisions.md` decision 10** ("Headless workers lose project-scoped
  hooks; the cause is named, not yet fixed"): change the decision from "cause named, not
  yet fixed" to "decided and implemented". Rewrite the title accordingly (drop "not yet
  fixed"). Keep the cause explanation and the reproduction snippet; replace the
  open-question section with the chosen mechanism — symlink a configurable set of project
  config (default `.claude` and `.mcp.json`, with `.entire` opt-in via `repos.yml`) — plus
  why symlink beat copy and `--settings`, and the parity it restores with a non-worktree
  run.
- **`README.md` "Debug capture"** (lines 158-168): the current claim that "project-scoped
  hooks ... don't load inside the worktree" is now false. Reframe it: workers inherit
  project config via symlinks, so `--debug-file` / `DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1` now *confirms*
  parity rather than documenting a gap. Edit line 160's parenthetical and the cross-
  reference to decision 10.
- **`README.md` Install** (the `repos.yml` block, lines 48-57): document the optional
  `worktree_config_paths` key — what it does (which project config is symlinked into each
  worktree to give parity), its default `[.claude, .mcp.json]`, and that tool-specific
  directories like `.entire` are opt-in. Add a short companion note (in Prerequisites or
  as a new "Worktree config parity" line) so the behaviour is discoverable, not buried in
  the example.
- **`docs/repos.example.yml`**: add the commented optional `worktree_config_paths` block
  shown under Configurability, with `.entire` as a commented opt-in example.

## Tests

Test fixtures use a real git repo in a temp dir, a fake `claude` executable, and a
monkeypatched Linear client — the existing patterns in this suite; no real API tokens are
spent.

`tests/test_repos.py` — config parsing for the new key:
- `worktree_config_paths` absent → `Repos.worktree_config_paths == (".claude", ".mcp.json")`.
- present custom list (including `.entire`) → parsed verbatim.
- malformed (non-list, non-string entry, absolute path, or `..`) → `RepoConfigError`.

`tests/test_worktree.py` (reuse the `_init_repo` helper; calls pass explicit `names`):
- `test_link_project_config_symlinks_gitignored_claude` — seed `.claude/settings.json`,
  gitignore `.claude`; assert `(wt/".claude").is_symlink()`, that `readlink` points to
  `repo/.claude`, and that `settings.json` reads through the link.
- `test_link_project_config_noop_when_no_claude` — no config → no link and no error.
- `test_link_project_config_skips_existing_tracked_entry` — tracked `.claude/` → not a
  symlink after `add`, content intact (proves the don't-clobber guard).
- `test_link_project_config_includes_mcp_and_entire_when_present` — gitignored `.mcp.json`
  and `.entire/` get symlinked; when absent → no link.
- `test_linked_config_is_gitignored_in_worktree` — `git status --porcelain` run from the
  worktree shows `.claude` is NOT untracked (proves `git add` won't stage it).
- `test_remove_preserves_symlink_target` — after `worktree.remove`, `repo/.claude` and its
  contents still exist (teardown follows the link but deletes nothing real).

`tests/test_orchestrator_iteration.py` (fake-claude pattern): seed a gitignored
`.claude/settings.json` in the test repo; the fake `claude` records whether
`.claude/settings.json` is readable from its own cwd (`[ -r .claude/settings.json ]`).
Assert the marker says "present", proving the orchestrator links the config in before
spawning the worker — without spending tokens. This asserts the *precondition* for hook
loading (the config is resolvable from the worker's cwd), not that hooks actually fire.

Regression: the existing iteration and argv tests use repos with no `.claude/`, so
`link_project_config` is a clean no-op for them; the full suite must stay green. The worker
argv is untouched, so the `test_orchestrator_debug_capture_*` argv assertions are
unaffected.

## Linear issue to create (first execution step)

Plan mode is read-only, so creating this issue is the first step after approval (team
**Personal / ABA**, project **Autonomous cycle drain**). **One issue, not split:** the
slices are tightly coupled — the config key, the symlink function, and the orchestrator
wiring must land together to be end-to-end testable and mergeable — and the whole change
is ~200-300 lines of code (including tests and docs), a single reviewable batch. The five
acceptance criteria below are the sub-tasks, in execution order.

**Title:** Workers inherit the repo's project config inside worktrees

**Labels:** `repo:drain-cycle`, `model:sonnet`

**Outcome / user story:**
> As an operator running an unattended cycle drain, I want each worker to load the same
> project-scoped settings, hooks, agents, and skills as a normal Claude Code session at the
> repo root. The worker runs in a git worktree, which checks out only tracked files, so any
> gitignored `.claude/` config is currently missing and the tooling I rely on
> interactively — for example the `entire.io` checkpointing hooks — silently never runs.
> Symlinking that config into the worktree restores parity, so the same tooling works
> headless.

**Acceptance criteria (ordered sub-tasks):**
1. **Config** — `repos.yml` accepts an optional `worktree_config_paths` list (default
   `[.claude, .mcp.json]`); `repos.py` parses and validates it (`RepoConfigError` on a
   malformed value); `tests/test_repos.py` covers the default, a custom list, and
   malformed input.
2. **Symlink helper** — `worktree.link_project_config(repo, worktree_path, names)` symlinks
   each entry that is present in the repo and not already checked out in the worktree;
   `tests/test_worktree.py` covers symlink creation, the no-op case, skip-tracked,
   gitignored-in-worktree, and remove-preserves-target.
3. **Wiring** — the orchestrator calls the helper after `worktree.add`, inside the existing
   pre-spawn `try` block, passing `repos.worktree_config_paths`; an integration test
   asserts that the worker's cwd can resolve `.claude/settings.json`.
4. **Docs** — update all four surfaces listed in the Docs section above (design-decisions
   decision 10 flip, README debug-capture reframe, README config-key documentation, and
   `repos.example.yml`).
5. **Verify and ship** — full test suite green; run the manual `DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1` parity
   check; then follow the AGENTS.md completion sequence (review → fix → commit and push to
   main → Linear comment → Done).

**Model:** Sonnet. The plan is prescriptive — file paths, function signatures, and test
names are all named — so ambiguity is low and Sonnet (the repo's cost-efficient default
worker model) is sufficient. Switch to `model:opus` only if the operator wants extra care
on the decision-10 and README prose rewrites.

**Size estimate (against the per-issue caps of 8M tokens / 20 minutes):** ~200-300 lines of
code across 4 source files, 3 test files, and 3 docs. Expected run is ~2-4M tokens and
~10-15 minutes of wall-clock time — comfortably inside the caps, with the doc rewrites the
most token-variable part.

## Verification
1. `uv run pytest` — all tests green (existing plus new).
2. Manual parity check on this repo:
   ```bash
   DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1 drain-cycle   # against a cycle with a repo:drain-cycle issue
   ```
   Then diff the per-issue `~/.drain-cycle/runs/*-<issue>.debug.log` against the baseline
   in design-decisions decision 10: the worktree run should now watch the project
   `.claude/settings.json` and fire the `entire.io` SessionStart/SessionEnd hooks ("Entire
   CLI will link this conversation..."), matching the repo-root column of the decision-10
   comparison table.
3. Confirm that removing a completed issue's worktree left `<repo>/.claude` and
   `<repo>/.entire` intact (`ls` after a Done run).

## Critical files
- `drain_cycle/worktree.py` — add `link_project_config(repo, worktree_path, names)`
- `drain_cycle/repos.py` — `DEFAULT_WORKTREE_CONFIG_PATHS` + `Repos.worktree_config_paths`

1	"""Thin wrapper around ``git worktree``.
2	
3	Each issue gets ``.worktrees/<issue-identifier>/`` branched off ``main`` per
4	README §3, used once, then removed on Done.
5	
6	``git worktree`` stderr is captured and surfaced in the raised
7	``RuntimeError`` on failure. The orchestrator's pre-spawn try/except
8	threads the message into the runlog's ``halt_reason`` so the operator
9	sees git's actual diagnostic (dirty tree, branch already exists,
10	missing ``main``) rather than just a non-zero exit code.
11	"""
12	from __future__ import annotations
13	
14	import subprocess
15	from pathlib import Path
16	
17	BASE_BRANCH = "main"
18	WORKTREE_DIR = ".worktrees"
19	
20	
21	def add(repo: Path, identifier: str) -> Path:
22	    """Create a worktree branched off ``main`` for ``identifier``.
23	
24	    Returns the absolute path to the new worktree.
25	    """
26	    worktree_path = repo / WORKTREE_DIR / identifier
27	    _run_git(
28	        ["worktree", "add", "-b", identifier, str(worktree_path), BASE_BRANCH],
29	        cwd=repo,
30	    )
31	    return worktree_path
32	
33	
34	def remove(repo: Path, worktree_path: Path) -> None:
35	    """Remove a worktree previously created by :func:`add`."""
36	    _run_git(["worktree", "remove", str(worktree_path)], cwd=repo)
37	
38	
39	def _run_git(args: list[str], *, cwd: Path) -> None:
40	    result = subprocess.run(
41	        ["git", *args],
42	        cwd=cwd,
43	        check=False,
44	        capture_output=True,
45	        text=True,
46	    )
47	    if result.returncode != 0:
48	        raise RuntimeError(
49	            f"git {' '.join(args)} failed (exit {result.returncode}): "
50	            f"{result.stderr.strip() or result.stdout.strip() or '<no output>'}"
51	        )
52	
```

1	"""Per-issue target-repo resolution.
2	
3	The orchestrator used to be single-repo by construction: every worktree
4	landed under ``Path.cwd()``. Cycles in this workspace span multiple repos
5	by design — ``linear-workflow.md`` makes "Affected repos" part of the
6	six initiative-readiness fields, and the Ops slot deliberately holds
7	cross-repo maintenance issues — so each issue now carries a
8	``repo:<name>`` label and ``~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml`` maps the name to
9	an absolute path on disk.
10	
11	Two distinct error types so the two halt paths can branch cleanly:
12	
13	* ``RepoConfigError`` — startup-time problems (missing file, malformed
14	  YAML, empty, wrong top-level shape). The CLI catches it before any
15	  Linear traffic, prints to stderr, exits 1, and does **not** write a
16	  run-log entry — there is no cycle to log against yet.
17	* ``RepoResolutionError`` — per-issue problems (no ``repo:`` label,
18	  multiple ``repo:`` labels, name not in config, resolved path missing).
19	  The orchestrator catches it as a pre-spawn halt: write a run-log
20	  entry, print the matching ``Halt:`` line, exit 1. No revert is
21	  attempted because no Linear state was moved yet.
22	"""
23	from __future__ import annotations
24	
25	from dataclasses import dataclass
26	from pathlib import Path
27	from typing import Any
28	
29	import yaml
30	
31	_CONFIG_DISPLAY = "~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml"
32	_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX = "repo:"
33	
34	_MISSING_CONFIG_HINT = (
35	    "create it mapping each repo: label to an absolute path, e.g.:\n\n"
36	    "  repos:\n"
37	    "    drain-cycle: /Users/you/src/drain-cycle\n"
38	    "    pde-skills:  /Users/you/src/pde-skills\n\n"
39	    "see docs/repos.example.yml for a template."
40	)
41	
42	
43	class RepoConfigError(RuntimeError):
44	    """``repos.yml`` is missing or malformed."""
45	
46	
47	class RepoResolutionError(RuntimeError):
48	    """An issue cannot be mapped to a target repo."""
49	
50	
51	def default_config_path() -> Path:
52	    """Resolved per call so tests can redirect via ``monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", ...)``."""
53	    return Path.home() / ".drain-cycle" / "repos.yml"
54	
55	
56	@dataclass(frozen=True)
57	class Repos:
58	    """Loaded ``repos.yml`` mapping (``name`` → absolute ``Path``)."""
59	
60	    mapping: dict[str, Path]
61	
62	    def resolve(self, issue: dict[str, Any]) -> Path:
63	        labels = [
64	            label
65	            for label in issue.get("labels", [])
66	            if label.startswith(_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX)
67	        ]
68	        if not labels:
69	            raise RepoResolutionError(f"no {_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX} label on issue")
70	        if len(labels) > 1:
71	            names = ", ".join(sorted(label.removeprefix(_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX) for label in labels))
72	            raise RepoResolutionError(f"multiple {_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX} labels: {names}")
73	        name = labels[0].removeprefix(_REPO_LABEL_PREFIX)
74	        try:
75	            path = self.mapping[name]
76	        except KeyError:
77	            raise RepoResolutionError(
78	                f'repo "{name}" not in {_CONFIG_DISPLAY}'
79	            ) from None
80	        if not path.exists():
81	            raise RepoResolutionError(f"resolved path {path} does not exist")
82	        return path
83	
84	
85	def load(path: Path | None = None) -> Repos:
86	    """Read ``repos.yml`` and return a validated ``Repos``.
87	
88	    Tilde-prefixed paths inside the file are expanded against ``$HOME``.
89	    Every failure mode raises ``RepoConfigError`` with a message that
90	    names the on-disk problem so the operator can fix it without
91	    re-reading source.
92	    """
93	    if path is None:
94	        path = default_config_path()
95	    if not path.exists():
96	        raise RepoConfigError(
97	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} not found at {path}\n{_MISSING_CONFIG_HINT}"
98	        )
99	    try:
100	        data = yaml.safe_load(path.read_text())
101	    except yaml.YAMLError as exc:
102	        raise RepoConfigError(
103	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} is not valid YAML: {exc}"
104	        ) from None
105	    if data is None:
106	        raise RepoConfigError(f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} is empty")
107	    if not isinstance(data, dict) or "repos" not in data:
108	        raise RepoConfigError(
109	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} must have a top-level 'repos:' key"
110	        )
111	    block = data["repos"]
112	    if not isinstance(block, dict) or not block:
113	        raise RepoConfigError(
114	            f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} 'repos:' must be a non-empty mapping"
115	        )
116	    mapping: dict[str, Path] = {}
117	    for name, value in block.items():
118	        if not isinstance(value, str):
119	            raise RepoConfigError(
120	                f"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} entry {name!r}: path must be a string"
121	            )
122	        mapping[str(name)] = Path(value).expanduser()
123	    return Repos(mapping=mapping)
124	
```
