1	"""Drain a cycle by iterating over its sorted Todo/Backlog issues.
2	
3	Halt-on-not-Done, the orchestrator-owned Todo→In-Progress transition, the
4	run-log artefact, and the inspectable-halt UX all live here. The halt-message
5	helper ``_halt_message`` is the single source of truth for the operator-facing
6	halt string — emitted both on stderr and into the run-log entry's
7	``halt_reason`` field.
8	"""
9	from __future__ import annotations
10	
11	import os
12	import sys
13	from datetime import datetime, timezone
14	from pathlib import Path
15	
16	from . import linear, model, prompt, runlog, worker, worktree
17	from .limits import Limits, check_cycle
18	from .repos import RepoResolutionError, Repos
19	
20	_DONE_STATE_TYPE = "completed"
21	_IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME = "In Progress"
22	_CLAUDE_CMD = ["claude", "-p", "--dangerously-skip-permissions"]
23	_DEBUG_ENV_VAR = "DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG"
24	"""Opt-in switch for per-issue ``--debug-file`` capture. Any non-empty value
25	turns it on; the worker then writes each session's startup diagnostics
26	(settings sources, plugins, MCP servers, hooks) beside the run log. Off by
27	default — the diagnostic exists for one-shot investigation, not steady state.
28	See ``docs/design-decisions.md`` §10."""
29	_UNRESOLVED_WORKTREE_DISPLAY = "<unresolved>"
30	"""Worktree-path placeholder for the pre-spawn resolution-halt path.
31	No path has been chosen yet — the issue couldn't be mapped to a target
32	repo — so the run-log entry and stderr halt line carry this marker
33	rather than a misleading fake path."""
34	
35	
36	def _now_iso() -> str:
37	    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()
38	
39	
40	def _halt_message(identifier: str, state_name: str, worktree_path: Path) -> str:
41	    """Single source of truth for the halt UX.
42	
43	    The same string lands on stderr (the operator's grep anchor) and in
44	    the run-log entry's ``halt_reason`` field — so kill-condition tooling
45	    reads the same human-readable explanation the operator saw at halt
46	    time.
47	    """
48	    return f"Halt: {identifier} (final state: {state_name}) at {worktree_path}"
49	
50	
51	def _revert_to_pre_halt_state(
52	    issue_id: str, *, target_state_name: str, pre_revert_state_name: str
53	) -> tuple[str, str | None]:
54	    """Restore Linear state on halt; return ``(state_to_report, error_msg)``.
55	
56	    The orchestrator transitions issues Todo→In Progress before spawning the
57	    agent. When the run halts, that In-Progress flag leaves the issue outside
58	    ``_PENDING_STATE_TYPES`` so a re-run silently skips it. This helper
59	    reverses the transition and re-fetches to confirm.
60	
61	    On revert success, returns the refreshed state name and ``None``.
62	    On revert failure, returns ``pre_revert_state_name`` (the state the
63	    issue is actually still in, so the operator can find it) plus the
64	    exception message — non-fatal. A failed refresh after a
65	    successful revert falls back to ``target_state_name``, since we trust
66	    the mutation landed even if the read-back didn't.
67	    """
68	    try:
69	        linear.set_state(issue_id, target_state_name)
70	    except Exception as exc:
71	        return pre_revert_state_name, str(exc)
72	    try:
73	        refreshed = linear.get_issue(issue_id)
74	    except Exception:
75	        return target_state_name, None
76	    return refreshed["state"]["name"], None
77	
78	
79	def _worker_log_fields(result: worker.WorkerResult) -> dict[str, object]:
80	    """Map a ``WorkerResult`` onto the run-log entry's usage fields.
81	
82	    Shared by all three worker-backed ``append_entry`` calls (timeout
83	    halt, Done, not-Done halt) so the recorded usage shape can't drift
84	    between branches.
85	    """
86	    return {
87	        "duration_seconds": result.duration_seconds,
88	        "model": result.model,
89	        "usage": result.usage,
90	        "cost_usd": result.cost_usd,
91	        "num_turns": result.num_turns,
92	        "session_id": result.session_id,
93	        "is_error": result.is_error,
94	    }
95	
96	
97	def _debug_enabled() -> bool:
98	    """Whether per-issue ``--debug-file`` capture is switched on.
99	
100	    Read from the environment (``os.environ`` already carries any
101	    ``~/.drain-cycle/.env`` value loaded at CLI startup), so the operator
102	    turns it on for one investigative run with ``DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1
103	    drain-cycle`` and leaves it off otherwise.
104	    """
105	    return bool(os.environ.get(_DEBUG_ENV_VAR))
106	
107	
108	def run(repos: Repos, limits: Limits | None = None) -> int:
109	    if limits is None:
110	        limits = Limits()
111	    debug = _debug_enabled()
112	    cycle_id = linear.current_cycle_id()
113	    log = runlog.RunLog(cycle_id=cycle_id)
114	    issues = linear.pending_issues(cycle_id)
115	    if not issues:
116	        print(f"Cycle {cycle_id} has no Todo/Backlog issues — nothing to do.")
117	        return 0
118	
119	    for issue in issues:
120	        identifier = issue["identifier"]
121	        print(f"drain-cycle: picked {identifier}: {issue['title']}", file=sys.stderr)
122	
123	        started_at = _now_iso()
124	        try:
125	            target_repo = repos.resolve(issue)
126	        except RepoResolutionError as exc:
127	            # Pre-spawn resolution halt: no Linear state was moved, so no
128	            # revert is attempted. The worktree path is the ``<unresolved>``
129	            # placeholder since no repo was chosen.
130	            state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
131	            halt_reason = (
132	                f"{_halt_message(identifier, state_name, Path(_UNRESOLVED_WORKTREE_DISPLAY))}"
133	                f" — {exc}"
134	            )
135	            log.append_entry(
136	                issue_identifier=identifier,
137	                started_at=started_at,
138	                finished_at=_now_iso(),
139	                exit_code=-1,
140	                final_linear_state=state_name,
141	                REDACTED,
142	                halt_reason=halt_reason,
143	            )
144	            print(halt_reason, file=sys.stderr)
145	            return 1
146	
147	        try:
148	            worktree_path = worktree.add(target_repo, identifier)
149	            # Orchestrator owns the Todo→In Progress half so the lifecycle
150	            # doesn't depend on the spawned agent's compliance. The agent
151	            # still owns the …→Done half via Linear MCP — see prompt.py tail.
152	            linear.set_state(issue["id"], _IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME)
153	        except Exception as exc:
154	            # Convert any pre-spawn failure into a recorded halt rather than
155	            # a traceback: write a run-log entry with the planned worktree
156	            # path, print the halt message, exit non-zero. Subsequent issues
157	            # are not attempted — same contract as a spawn-time halt.
158	            planned_path = target_repo / worktree.WORKTREE_DIR / identifier
159	            state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
160	            halt_reason = (
161	                f"{_halt_message(identifier, state_name, planned_path)}"
162	                f" — setup failed: {exc}"
163	            )
164	            log.append_entry(
165	                issue_identifier=identifier,
166	                started_at=started_at,
167	                finished_at=_now_iso(),
168	                exit_code=-1,
169	                final_linear_state=state_name,
170	                worktree_path=str(planned_path),
171	                halt_reason=halt_reason,
172	            )
173	            print(halt_reason, file=sys.stderr)
174	            return 1
175	
176	        agent_prompt = prompt.build(issue, worktree_path)
177	        worker_model = model.resolve(issue)
178	
179	        debug_file = log.debug_path(identifier) if debug else None
180	        if debug_file is not None:
181	            print(
182	                f"drain-cycle: {identifier} debug capture → {debug_file}",
183	                file=sys.stderr,
184	            )
185	
186	        result = worker.run_issue(
187	            claude_cmd=_CLAUDE_CMD,
188	            model=worker_model,
189	            prompt=agent_prompt,
190	            cwd=worktree_path,
191	            token_limit=limits.per_issue_tokens,
192	            time_limit_seconds=limits.per_issue_seconds,
193	            cost_limit_usd=limits.per_issue_cost_usd,
194	            debug_file=debug_file,
195	        )
196	        finished_at = _now_iso()
197	
198	        if result.breach is not None:
199	            # The worker crossed a per-issue cap (tokens or time) and was
200	            # process-group killed (grandchildren reaped). Same revert + halt
201	            # contract as a not-Done exit, with the recorded usage of the
202	            # killed session and the breached cap named in the halt reason.
203	            original_state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
204	            effective_state, revert_error = _revert_to_pre_halt_state(
205	                issue["id"],
206	                target_state_name=original_state_name,
207	                pre_revert_state_name=_IN_PROGRESS_STATE_NAME,
208	            )
209	            halt_reason = (
210	                f"{_halt_message(identifier, effective_state, worktree_path)}"
211	                f" — {result.breach.describe()}"
212	            )
213	            if revert_error is not None:
214	                halt_reason += (
215	                    f"; revert to {original_state_name!r} failed: {revert_error}"
216	                )
217	            log.append_entry(
218	                issue_identifier=identifier,
219	                started_at=started_at,
220	                finished_at=finished_at,
221	                exit_code=result.exit_code,
222	                final_linear_state=effective_state,
223	                worktree_path=str(worktree_path),
224	                halt_reason=halt_reason,
225	                **_worker_log_fields(result),
226	            )
227	            print(halt_reason, file=sys.stderr)
228	            return 1
229	
230	        refreshed = linear.get_issue(issue["id"])
231	        post_spawn_state = refreshed["state"]["name"]
232	        is_done = refreshed["state"]["type"] == _DONE_STATE_TYPE
233	
234	        if is_done:
235	            # Append unconditionally for every attempted issue.
236	            log.append_entry(
237	                issue_identifier=identifier,
238	                started_at=started_at,
239	                finished_at=finished_at,
240	                exit_code=result.exit_code,
241	                final_linear_state=post_spawn_state,
242	                worktree_path=str(worktree_path),
243	                halt_reason=None,
244	                **_worker_log_fields(result),
245	            )
246	            worktree.remove(target_repo, worktree_path)
247	            print(f"drain-cycle: {identifier} done; worktree removed.", file=sys.stderr)
248	
249	            # Cycle-wide circuit breaker: every issue may stay under its own
250	            # per-issue caps while their sum drains the quota. Check the
251	            # running totals (which now include this Done issue) before
252	            # spawning the next one; on breach, stop the cycle.
253	            cycle_breach = check_cycle(
254	                limits,
255	                tokens=log.cycle_tokens_cumulative(),
256	                cost_usd=log.cycle_cost_usd(),
257	                seconds=log.cycle_duration_seconds(),
258	            )
259	            if cycle_breach is not None:
260	                halt_reason = f"Halt: {cycle_breach.describe()}"
261	                log.set_cycle_halt(halt_reason)
262	                print(halt_reason, file=sys.stderr)
263	                return 1
264	            continue
265	
266	        # Not-Done halt: revert to the pre-halt state so a re-run picks
267	        # this issue back up instead of silently skipping it.
268	        original_state_name = issue["state"]["name"]
269	        effective_state, revert_error = _revert_to_pre_halt_state(
270	            issue["id"],
271	            target_state_name=original_state_name,
272	            pre_revert_state_name=post_spawn_state,
273	        )
274	        halt_reason = _halt_message(identifier, effective_state, worktree_path)
275	        if revert_error is not None:
276	            halt_reason += (
277	                f" — revert to {original_state_name!r} failed: {revert_error}"
278	            )
279	        # halt_reason carries the same string also printed to stderr below
280	        # so the on-disk and terminal surfaces cannot drift.
281	        log.append_entry(
282	            issue_identifier=identifier,
283	            started_at=started_at,
284	            finished_at=finished_at,
285	            exit_code=result.exit_code,
286	            final_linear_state=effective_state,
287	            worktree_path=str(worktree_path),
288	            halt_reason=halt_reason,
289	            **_worker_log_fields(result),
290	        )
291	        print(halt_reason, file=sys.stderr)
292	        return 1
293	
294	    return 0
295

1	"""Tests for ``drain_cycle.repos``.
2	
3	Two distinct exception types must remain distinct: ``RepoConfigError``

7	every variant of message tail called out in the issue spec.

1	"""Tests for the ``worktree`` git wrapper.
2	
3	The happy-path is exercised end-to-end by ``test_orchestrator_iteration``.
4	What these tests pin is the failure mode: git's stderr must be captured
5	and surfaced in the raised ``RuntimeError`` so the operator (and the
6	runlog ``halt_reason`` written by the orchestrator) sees what actually
7	went wrong, not just a non-zero exit code.
8	"""
9	from __future__ import annotations
10	
11	import subprocess
12	from pathlib import Path
13	
14	import pytest
15	
16	from drain_cycle import worktree
17	
18	
19	def _init_repo(repo: Path) -> None:
20	    subprocess.run(["git", "init", "-b", "main"], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True)
21	    subprocess.run(["git", "config", "user.email", "t@x"], cwd=repo, check=True)
22	    subprocess.run(["git", "config", "user.name", "T"], cwd=repo, check=True)
23	    (repo / "README.md").write_text("seed\n")
24	    subprocess.run(["git", "add", "."], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True)
25	    subprocess.run(["git", "commit", "-m", "seed"], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True)
26	
27	
28	def test_add_failure_raises_with_git_stderr_in_message(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
29	    """``git worktree add`` fails when the branch name is already taken.
30	    The raised RuntimeError must carry git's stderr so the operator can
31	    diagnose without re-running by hand."""
32	    repo = tmp_path / "repo"
33	    repo.mkdir()
34	    _init_repo(repo)
35	
36	    # First add succeeds, taking the branch "ABA-X".
37	    worktree.add(repo, "ABA-X")
38	    # Second add fails — same branch.
39	    with pytest.raises(RuntimeError) as excinfo:
40	        worktree.add(repo, "ABA-X")
41	
42	    msg = str(excinfo.value)
43	    # The message names the operation and includes git's actual complaint
44	    # (typically "already exists" or "already checked out").
45	    assert "worktree add" in msg
46	    assert "ABA-X" in msg
47	
48	
49	def test_remove_failure_raises_with_git_stderr_in_message(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
50	    """``git worktree remove`` on a non-existent path fails. The raised
51	    RuntimeError must carry git's stderr."""
52	    repo = tmp_path / "repo"
53	    repo.mkdir()
54	    _init_repo(repo)
55	
56	    bogus = repo / ".worktrees" / "never-existed"
57	    with pytest.raises(RuntimeError) as excinfo:
58	        worktree.remove(repo, bogus)
59	
60	    msg = str(excinfo.value)
61	    assert "worktree remove" in msg
62	    # Either git's own "is not a working tree" message or the path itself.
63	    assert str(bogus) in msg or "working tree" in msg.lower()
64	
```

{
  "title": "Workers inherit the repo's project config inside worktrees",
  "team": "Personal",
  "project": "Autonomous cycle drain",
  "labels": [
    "repo:drain-cycle",
    "model:sonnet"

  "state": "In Progress",
  "cycle": "2",
  "description": "## Outcome / user story\n\nAs an operator running an unattended cycle drain, I want each worker to load the same project-scoped settings, hooks, agents, and skills as a normal Claude Code session at the repo root. The worker runs in a git worktree, which checks out only tracked files, so any gitignored `.claude/` config is currently missing and the tooling I rely on interactively — for example the `entire.io` checkpointing hooks — silently never runs. Symlinking that config into the worktree restores parity, so the same tooling works headless.\n\n## Approach\n\nSymlink a configurable set of the repo's real project-scoped config dirs/files into each worktree after it's created. The set defaults to `[.claude, .mcp.json]` and is overridable via an optional `worktree_config_paths` key in `repos.yml` (`.entire` is opt-in, since not every repo installs the `entire.io` tool). Symlinks — not copies — so a stateful hook reads and writes the repo's actual config exactly as a non-worktree run does, and teardown (`git worktree remove`) deletes the links but not their targets.\n\n## Acceptance criteria (ordered sub-tasks)\n\n1. **Config** — `repos.yml` accepts an optional `worktree_config_paths` list (default `[.claude, .mcp.json]`); `repos.py` parses and validates it (`RepoConfigError` on a malformed value); `tests/test_repos.py` covers the default, a custom list, and malformed input.\n2. **Symlink helper** — `worktree.link_project_config(repo, worktree_path, names)` symlinks each entry that is present in the repo and not already checked out in the worktree; `tests/test_worktree.py` covers symlink creation, the no-op case, skip-tracked, gitignored-in-worktree, and remove-preserves-target.\n3. **Wiring** — the orchestrator calls the helper after `worktree.add`, inside the existing pre-spawn `try` block, passing `repos.worktree_config_paths`; an integration test asserts that the worker's cwd can resolve `.claude/settings.json`.\n4. **Docs** — update all four surfaces: design-decisions decision 10 flip (gap → fix), README \"Debug capture\" reframe, README Install config-key documentation, and `docs/repos.example.yml`.\n5. **Verify and ship** — full test suite green; run the manual `DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1` parity check; then follow the AGENTS.md completion sequence (review → fix → commit and push to main → Linear comment → Done).\n\n## Size\n\n~200-300 lines of code across 4 source files, 3 test files, and 3 docs. Expected ~2-4M tokens and ~10-15 minutes wall-clock — inside the per-issue caps (8M tokens / 20 min)."
}
```

Now the validation logic in `load()`. I'll add a helper and call it:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_016Cawto96KjMauHLfNrEnip
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/repos.py",
  "old_string": "def load(path: Path | None = None) -> Repos:",
  "new_string": "def _parse_worktree_config_paths(value: Any) -> tuple[str, ...]:\n    \"\"\"Validate the optional ``worktree_config_paths`` key.\n\n    ``None`` (key absent) yields the default. A present value must be a\n    non-empty list of relative-path strings — entries are symlinked into a\n    worktree, so an absolute path or one containing ``..`` could point outside\n    the repo and is rejected. Every failure raises ``RepoConfigError`` naming\n    the offending entry.\n    \"\"\"\n    if value is None:\n        return DEFAULT_WORKTREE_CONFIG_PATHS\n    if not isinstance(value, list) or not value:\n        raise RepoConfigError(\n            f\"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} 'worktree_config_paths' must be a non-empty list\"\n        )\n    entries: list[str] = []\n    for entry in value:\n        if not isinstance(entry, str) or not entry.strip():\n            raise RepoConfigError(\n                f\"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} 'worktree_config_paths' entry {entry!r}: \"\n                \"must be a non-empty string\"\n            )\n        if Path(entry).is_absolute():\n            raise RepoConfigError(\n                f\"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} 'worktree_config_paths' entry {entry!r}: \"\n                \"must be a relative path, not absolute\"\n            )\n        if \"..\" in Path(entry).parts:\n            raise RepoConfigError(\n                f\"{_CONFIG_DISPLAY} 'worktree_config_paths' entry {entry!r}: \"\n                \"must not contain '..'\"\n            )\n        entries.append(entry)\n    return tuple(entries)\n\n\ndef load(path: Path | None = None) -> Repos:"
}
```

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/worktree.py",
  "old_string": "def remove(repo: Path, worktree_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Remove a worktree previously created by :func:`add`.\"\"\"\n    _run_git([\"worktree\", \"remove\", str(worktree_path)], cwd=repo)",
  "new_string": "def link_project_config(\n    repo: Path, worktree_path: Path, names: Iterable[str]\n) -> list[Path]:\n    \"\"\"Symlink gitignored project-scoped config from ``repo`` into the worktree.\n\n    A git worktree checks out only tracked files, so gitignored project config\n    (``.claude/`` settings/hooks/agents/skills, a root ``.mcp.json``) is absent.\n    Linking the repo's real entries in gives a worker the same settings, hooks,\n    agents, skills, and MCP config as an interactive session at the repo root —\n    and because the link points at the live dir, a stateful hook reads and\n    writes the repo's actual config exactly as a non-worktree run would.\n\n    For each name: skip it if absent in ``repo`` (a clean no-op for repos\n    without that config) or if something already occupies that path in the\n    worktree (a tracked entry git checked out, or a pre-existing link). The\n    check uses ``os.path.lexists`` so a dangling link counts as present and is\n    never clobbered. Returns the links created.\n    \"\"\"\n    created: list[Path] = []\n    repo = repo.resolve()\n    for name in names:\n        source = repo / name\n        if not source.exists():\n            continue\n        link = worktree_path / name\n        if os.path.lexists(link):\n            continue\n        os.symlink(source, link)\n        created.append(link)\n    return created\n\n\ndef remove(repo: Path, worktree_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Remove a worktree previously created by :func:`add`.\"\"\"\n    _run_git([\"worktree\", \"remove\", str(worktree_path)], cwd=repo)"
}

1	"""Drain-the-cycle iteration test for the orchestrator.
2	
3	The walking skeleton covered one issue end-to-end. This test pins the
4	behaviour that *every* sorted Todo/Backlog issue is processed in order, each
5	in its own worktree, and that every worktree is removed when the spawned
6	session signals completion.
7	
8	What we substitute and why:
9	
10	* Linear: stubbed in-process. The orchestrator imports the linear module by
11	  attribute, so monkey-patching ``cycle_id`` / ``pending_issues`` / ``get_issue``
12	  on it is sufficient. We do **not** stub the GraphQL transport — the layer
13	  under test here is the orchestrator loop, not the wire format (the wire
14	  format is exercised separately).
15	* Spawned ``claude -p``: replaced with a real shell script via
16	  ``_CLAUDE_CMD``. The script writes the basename of its cwd (the issue
17	  identifier — that's how the orchestrator names worktrees) into a shared
18	  marker file. The stubbed ``get_issue`` reads that file to decide which
19	  issues are Done. This satisfies the spec's "no-op script that calls back
20	  into the stubbed Linear" — the callback path is the marker file rather
21	  than an in-process call, because the script runs in a separate process.
22	* Git: a real ``git init`` repo with one commit on ``main``. ``git worktree``
23	  is exercised for real; this is the cheapest way to be sure the orchestrator
24	  doesn't paper over a worktree problem.
25	"""
26	from __future__ import annotations
27	
28	import subprocess
29	from pathlib import Path
30	
31	import pytest
32	
33	from drain_cycle import linear, orchestrator, repos, runlog
34	
35	
36	def _issue(
37	    identifier: str,
38	    priority: int,
39	    sort_order: float,
40	    *,
41	    repo_name: str = "test-repo",
42	) -> dict:
43	    return {
44	        "id": f"id-{identifier}",
45	        "identifier": identifier,
46	        "title": f"Title for {identifier}",
47	        "description": f"Body for {identifier}",
48	        "priority": priority,
49	        "sortOrder": sort_order,
50	        "state": {"type": "unstarted", "name": "Todo"},
51	        "labels": [f"repo:{repo_name}"],
52	    }
53	
54	
55	def _init_repo(repo: Path) -> None:
56	    """Create a real git repo with one commit on ``main`` for worktree tests."""
57	    subprocess.run(["git", "init", "-b", "main"], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True)
58	    subprocess.run(
59	        ["git", "config", "user.email", "test@example.com"], cwd=repo, check=True
60	    )
61	    subprocess.run(["git", "config", "user.name", "Test"], cwd=repo, check=True)
62	    (repo / "README.md").write_text("seed\n")
63	    subprocess.run(["git", "add", "."], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True)
64	    subprocess.run(
65	        ["git", "commit", "-m", "seed"], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True
66	    )
67	
68	
69	def test_orchestrator_drains_every_issue_in_sorted_order(
70	    tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch
71	) -> None:
72	    repo = tmp_path / "repo"
73	    repo.mkdir()
74	    _init_repo(repo)
75	    monkeypatch.chdir(repo)
76	    monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", str(tmp_path))
77	
78	    # Three issues — chosen so a "naïve" iteration order (input order) and
79	    # the priority sort disagree, proving the loop consumes the *sorted*
80	    # list rather than the unsorted one.
81	    raw_issues = [
82	        _issue("ABA-X", priority=3, sort_order=2.0),  # Medium
83	        _issue("ABA-Y", priority=1, sort_order=1.0),  # Urgent — runs first
84	        _issue("ABA-Z", priority=2, sort_order=3.0),  # High
85	    ]
86	    sorted_issues = linear._sort_pending_issues(raw_issues)
87	    expected_order = [i["identifier"] for i in sorted_issues]
88	    assert expected_order == ["ABA-Y", "ABA-Z", "ABA-X"]
89	
90	    issues_by_id = {i["id"]: i for i in raw_issues}
91	    done_marker = tmp_path / "done-identifiers.txt"
92	
93	    def fake_current_cycle_id() -> str:
94	        return "stub-cycle"
95	
96	    def fake_pending_issues(cycle_id: str) -> list[dict]:
97	        # Re-sort each call (mirrors the real client, which sorts the wire
98	        # response) and exclude anything the fake claude script has already
99	        # marked Done — so an accidental re-fetch can't re-feed the same
100	        # issue back into the loop.
101	        completed = _completed_identifiers(done_marker)
102	        return linear._sort_pending_issues(
103	            [i for i in raw_issues if i["identifier"] not in completed]
104	        )
105	
106	    def fake_get_issue(issue_id: str) -> dict:
107	        issue = issues_by_id[issue_id]
108	        if issue["identifier"] in _completed_identifiers(done_marker):
109	            return {**issue, "state": {"type": "completed", "name": "Done"}}
110	        return issue
111	
112	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "current_cycle_id", fake_current_cycle_id)
113	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "pending_issues", fake_pending_issues)
114	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "get_issue", fake_get_issue)
115	    # set_state is exercised by tests/test_orchestrator_set_state.py; here it's
116	    # a no-op so this test stays focused on iteration order + worktree cleanup.
117	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "set_state", lambda issue_id, state_name: None)
118	
119	    fake_claude = _write_fake_claude_script(tmp_path, done_marker)
120	    monkeypatch.setattr(orchestrator, "_CLAUDE_CMD", [str(fake_claude)])
121	
122	    exit_code = orchestrator.run(repos.Repos(mapping={"test-repo": repo}))
123	
124	    assert exit_code == 0
125	    # The script appends the worktree-basename (== issue identifier) on each
126	    # run, so the file contents are the exact processing order.
127	    assert done_marker.read_text().splitlines() == expected_order
128	    # Every worktree was removed on success.
129	    worktrees_dir = repo / ".worktrees"
130	    assert not worktrees_dir.exists() or not any(worktrees_dir.iterdir())
131	    # ``git worktree list`` should show only the main checkout.
132	    listed = subprocess.run(
133	        ["git", "worktree", "list", "--porcelain"],
134	        cwd=repo,
135	        check=True,
136	        capture_output=True,
137	        text=True,
138	    ).stdout
139	    assert listed.count("worktree ") == 1
140	
141	
142	def test_orchestrator_passes_resolved_model_to_worker(
143	    tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch
144	) -> None:
145	    """The model resolved from the issue's ``model:`` label reaches the
146	    spawned command as ``--model <resolved>`` — default Sonnet when absent,
147	    the labelled override otherwise."""
148	    repo = tmp_path / "repo"
149	    repo.mkdir()
150	    _init_repo(repo)
151	    monkeypatch.chdir(repo)
152	    monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", str(tmp_path))
153	
154	    default_issue = _issue("ABA-DEF", priority=1, sort_order=1.0)
155	    opus_issue = _issue("ABA-OPUS", priority=2, sort_order=2.0)
156	    opus_issue["labels"] = ["repo:test-repo", "model:opus"]
157	    raw_issues = [default_issue, opus_issue]
158	
159	    issues_by_id = {i["id"]: i for i in raw_issues}
160	    done_marker = tmp_path / "done-identifiers.txt"
161	    argv_dir = tmp_path / "argv"
162	    argv_dir.mkdir()
163	
164	    def fake_pending_issues(cycle_id: str) -> list[dict]:
165	        completed = _completed_identifiers(done_marker)
166	        return linear._sort_pending_issues(
167	            [i for i in raw_issues if i["identifier"] not in completed]
168	        )
169	
170	    def fake_get_issue(issue_id: str) -> dict:
171	        issue = issues_by_id[issue_id]
172	        if issue["identifier"] in _completed_identifiers(done_marker):
173	            return {**issue, "state": {"type": "completed", "name": "Done"}}
174	        return issue
175	
176	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "current_cycle_id", lambda: "stub-cycle")
177	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "pending_issues", fake_pending_issues)
178	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "get_issue", fake_get_issue)
179	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "set_state", lambda issue_id, state_name: None)
180	
181	    fake_claude = _write_argv_capturing_claude_script(tmp_path, done_marker, argv_dir)
182	    monkeypatch.setattr(orchestrator, "_CLAUDE_CMD", [str(fake_claude)])
183	
184	    exit_code = orchestrator.run(repos.Repos(mapping={"test-repo": repo}))
185	    assert exit_code == 0
186	
187	    assert _model_arg(argv_dir / "ABA-DEF.txt") == "claude-sonnet-4-6"
188	    assert _model_arg(argv_dir / "ABA-OPUS.txt") == "claude-opus-4-7"
189	
190	
191	def _captured_argv_for_one_issue(
192	    tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch, identifier: str
193	) -> list[str]:
194	    """Drain a single-issue cycle through a real worktree + argv-capturing
195	    fake ``claude``, returning the spawned argv. ``HOME`` / cwd / Linear
196	    stubs are wired here so the debug-capture tests only assert on the argv;
197	    the caller sets ``DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG`` (or not) before calling."""
198	    repo = tmp_path / "repo"
199	    repo.mkdir()
200	    _init_repo(repo)
201	    monkeypatch.chdir(repo)
202	    monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", str(tmp_path))
203	
204	    issue = _issue(identifier, priority=1, sort_order=1.0)
205	    raw_issues = [issue]
206	    issues_by_id = {i["id"]: i for i in raw_issues}
207	    done_marker = tmp_path / "done-identifiers.txt"
208	    argv_dir = tmp_path / "argv"
209	    argv_dir.mkdir()
210	
211	    def fake_pending_issues(cycle_id: str) -> list[dict]:
212	        completed = _completed_identifiers(done_marker)
213	        return [i for i in raw_issues if i["identifier"] not in completed]
214	
215	    def fake_get_issue(issue_id: str) -> dict:
216	        issue = issues_by_id[issue_id]
217	        if issue["identifier"] in _completed_identifiers(done_marker):
218	            return {**issue, "state": {"type": "completed", "name": "Done"}}
219	        return issue
220	
221	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "current_cycle_id", lambda: "stub-cycle")
222	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "pending_issues", fake_pending_issues)
223	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "get_issue", fake_get_issue)
224	    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, "set_state", lambda issue_id, state_name: None)
225	
226	    fake_claude = _write_argv_capturing_claude_script(tmp_path, done_marker, argv_dir)
227	    monkeypatch.setattr(orchestrator, "_CLAUDE_CMD", [str(fake_claude)])
228	
229	    assert orchestrator.run(repos.Repos(mapping={"test-repo": repo})) == 0
230	    return (argv_dir / f"{identifier}.txt").read_text().splitlines()
231	
232	
233	def test_orchestrator_debug_capture_passes_debug_file_beside_runlog(
234	    tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch
235	) -> None:
236	    """With ``DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG`` set, the spawned session gets a
237	    ``--debug-file`` whose path is a sibling of the run log, named per
238	    issue and timestamped with the run."""
239	    monkeypatch.setenv(orchestrator._DEBUG_ENV_VAR, "1")
240	
241	    argv = _captured_argv_for_one_issue(tmp_path, monkeypatch, "ABA-DBG")
242	
243	    assert "--debug-file" in argv
244	    debug_arg = Path(argv[argv.index("--debug-file") + 1])
245	    assert debug_arg.parent == runlog.runs_dir()
246	    assert debug_arg.name.startswith("stub-cycle-")
247	    assert debug_arg.name.endswith("-ABA-DBG.debug.log")
248	
249	
250	def test_orchestrator_omits_debug_file_when_capture_off(
251	    tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch
252	) -> None:
253	    """Default (no ``DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG``): no ``--debug-file`` reaches the
254	    spawned session."""
255	    monkeypatch.delenv(orchestrator._DEBUG_ENV_VAR, raising=False)
256	
257	    argv = _captured_argv_for_one_issue(tmp_path, monkeypatch, "ABA-NODBG")
258	
259	    assert "--debug-file" not in argv
260	
261	
262	def _model_arg(argv_file: Path) -> str:
263	    """Return the token following ``--model`` in a captured argv file."""
264	    argv = argv_file.read_text().splitlines()
265	    return argv[argv.index("--model") + 1]
266	
267	
268	def _write_argv_capturing_claude_script(
269	    tmp_path: Path, done_marker: Path, argv_dir: Path
270	) -> Path:
271	    """A ``claude -p`` stand-in that records its argv (one token per line)
272	    to ``argv_dir/<identifier>.txt`` and marks the issue Done."""
273	    script = tmp_path / "fake-claude-argv.sh"
274	    script.write_text(
275	        "#!/bin/sh\n"
276	        'id="$(basename "$PWD")"\n'
277	        f': > "{argv_dir}/$id.txt"\n'
278	        f'for a in "$@"; do printf "%s\\n" "$a" >> "{argv_dir}/$id.txt"; done\n'
279	        f'printf "%s\\n" "$id" >> "{done_marker}"\n'
280	    )
281	    script.chmod(0o755)
282	    return script
283	
284	
285	def _completed_identifiers(marker: Path) -> set[str]:
286	    if not marker.exists():
287	        return set()
288	    return {line for line in marker.read_text().splitlines() if line}
289	
290	
291	def _write_fake_claude_script(tmp_path: Path, done_marker: Path) -> Path:
292	    """A no-op stand-in for ``claude -p``.
293	
294	    Records the basename of its cwd (the issue identifier — orchestrator
295	    names worktrees ``.worktrees/<identifier>/``) into ``done_marker``.
296	    The stubbed ``get_issue`` reads this file to learn which issues the
297	    'agent' has completed.
298	    """
299	    script = tmp_path / "fake-claude.sh"
300	    script.write_text(
301	        "#!/bin/sh\n"
302	        f'printf "%s\\n" "$(basename "$PWD")" >> "{done_marker}"\n'
303	    )
304	    script.chmod(0o755)
305	    return script
306	
```

```
120	
121	- *CLI flags to override limits per invocation.* Deferred. The acceptance criteria require only defaults + `limits.yml`, and `cli.main` is a deliberately minimal exact-match dispatcher (decision in `cli.py`); adding an argument parser to thread per-run overrides is scope the single operator can cover by editing `limits.yml`. Revisit if a use case for one-off overrides appears.
122	- *Kill on the cycle cap mid-stream (pass cycle-so-far totals into the worker).* Rejected as unnecessary: the per-issue cap (8M) is below the cycle cap (30M), so a single issue can't cross the cycle cap before crossing its own; checking between issues catches the aggregate case without coupling the worker to cycle state.
123	- *A separate hardcoded outer timeout kept alongside the configurable caps.* Rejected — two time concepts where one suffices (see above).
124	
125	## 10. Headless workers lose project-scoped hooks; the cause is named, not yet fixed
126	
127	The operator noticed entire.io's checkpointing didn't take effect during a headless drain. The hypothesis was that the worker's worktree cwd (`.worktrees/<id>`) diverges from an interactive session at the repo root. A reproduction run confirmed the divergence and sharpened the cause — and this round names the constraint rather than fixing it (observability-first: no blind fix to hook/plugin loading).
128	
129	**What was observed (claude 2.1.150).** Running `claude -p --debug-file <path>` from the repo root vs. from a fresh `git worktree` of the same repo, then diffing the debug logs:
130	
131	| | Repo root | Worktree |
132	|---|---|---|
133	| Settings files watched | user `~/.claude/settings.json` + **project** `.claude/settings.json` + `.claude/settings.local.json` | **user only** |
134	| Project `.claude/settings.json` | loaded | "Broken symlink or missing file encountered" |
135	| entire.io hooks | SessionStart + SessionEnd fire ("Entire CLI will link this conversation to your next commit") | **absent — zero references** |
136	| User-scoped plugins (crit, hookify, agent-skills, security-guidance) | "Registered 7 hooks from 15 plugins" | **identical: "Registered 7 hooks from 15 plugins"** |
137	
138	**The cause is project-scoped registration in a gitignored file — not cwd alone, and not plugins generally.** entire registers its hooks in the project-scoped `.claude/settings.json`. That file is gitignored (`.gitignore` ends with `.claude`). A `git worktree` is a fresh checkout of *tracked* files only, and git reports the worktree directory as its own `--show-toplevel`, so Claude Code resolves the project root to the worktree and finds no `.claude/` there. User-scoped plugins and MCP servers — registered under `~/.claude/` — are cwd-independent and load identically in both, which is why the symptom looked like "some plugins" rather than "all hooks": only the project-scoped ones drop out. The original hypothesis (worktree cwd) was right that cwd is involved, but the operative mechanism is the gitignored project-settings file, not cwd by itself; were `.claude/settings.json` tracked, the worktree checkout would carry it.
139	
140	**Reproduction step (one-shot).** From a target repo with project-scoped hooks registered in `.claude/settings.json`:
141	
142	```bash
143	# Repo root — interactive-equivalent project root
144	claude -p --debug-file /tmp/root.debug.log --model claude-sonnet-4-6 \
145	  --max-budget-usd 0.50 "Reply with exactly: ok"
146	
147	# Fresh worktree — the worker's actual cwd
148	git worktree add -b repro .worktrees/repro main
149	( cd .worktrees/repro && claude -p --debug-file /tmp/worktree.debug.log \
150	    --model claude-sonnet-4-6 --max-budget-usd 0.50 "Reply with exactly: ok" )
151	git worktree remove --force .worktrees/repro && git branch -D repro
152	
153	# Diff the loaded settings/hooks. The worktree run is missing the project
154	# settings file and any hook registered in it.
155	grep -iE 'settings.json|Registered .* hooks|<your-plugin-name>' /tmp/root.debug.log
156	grep -iE 'settings.json|Registered .* hooks|<your-plugin-name>' /tmp/worktree.debug.log
157	```
158	
159	The same capture is wired into the worker as an opt-in: `DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1 drain-cycle` passes `--debug-file` to every spawned session, landing one `<run-log-stem>-<issue>.debug.log` per issue beside the run log in `~/.drain-cycle/runs/`. It is off by default — the diagnostic is for one-shot investigation, not steady-state overhead, and debug output goes to the file rather than stderr so the usage parser's stream is unaffected.
160	
161	**Open question — fix it, or accept it?** A fix would be cheap (any of: track `.claude/settings.json` so worktrees inherit it; register entire at the user scope; copy/symlink the repo's `.claude/` into each worktree at spawn; or pass `--settings <repo>/.claude/settings.json` to the worker). The reason none ships yet is upstream of the mechanism: it isn't obvious headless checkpointing is even *wanted*. entire's value is linking a conversation to the commit it produced — but a `drain-cycle` worktree is a throwaway branch removed on Done. Linking a session to a branch that's about to be deleted may be pointless or actively confusing. Until the operator decides whether project-scoped hooks *should* run inside throwaway worktrees, the divergence is a documented constraint, not a defect: workers run with user-scoped context only, and anything a target repo registers solely in its gitignored `.claude/` will not be present.
162	
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/docs/design-decisions.md",
  "old_string": "**Open question — fix it, or accept it?** A fix would be cheap (any of: track `.claude/settings.json` so worktrees inherit it; register entire at the user scope; copy/symlink the repo's `.claude/` into each worktree at spawn; or pass `--settings <repo>/.claude/settings.json` to the worker). The reason none ships yet is upstream of the mechanism: it isn't obvious headless checkpointing is even *wanted*. entire's value is linking a conversation to the commit it produced — but a `drain-cycle` worktree is a throwaway branch removed on Done. Linking a session to a branch that's about to be deleted may be pointless or actively confusing. Until the operator decides whether project-scoped hooks *should* run inside throwaway worktrees, the divergence is a documented constraint, not a defect: workers run with user-scoped context only, and anything a target repo registers solely in its gitignored `.claude/` will not be present.",
  "new_string": "**Decision — symlink a configurable set of project config into each worktree.** The earlier hesitation was upstream of the mechanism: it wasn't obvious headless checkpointing was even *wanted*, since a `drain-cycle` worktree is a throwaway branch. That resolved in favour of wanting it — the checkpoint links a session to the commit it produces, and that commit is pushed to `main` before the worktree is removed, so the link outlives the branch. The operator wants the same project tooling headless as interactively.\n\nAfter `worktree.add`, the orchestrator calls `worktree.link_project_config`, which symlinks the repo's real project-config entries into the new worktree. Because each link points at the live dir, the worker reads *and writes* the repo's actual config exactly as a non-worktree run does — so a stateful hook like entire's checkpointing works and persists. Teardown needs no special handling: `git worktree remove` deletes the worktree directory and its symlinks but not the link targets, so the repo's real `.claude/` and `.entire/` survive. The symlinked entries are gitignored in the worktree (it shares the repo's tracked `.gitignore`), so the worker's `git add` never stages them.\n\nThe linked set is configurable. It defaults to `[.claude, .mcp.json]` — sensible for any repo — and is overridden by an optional `worktree_config_paths` list in `repos.yml`. `.entire` is not a default, because not every repo uses entire.io; an operator who does adds it there. Entries must be relative paths without `..`, since they are resolved inside the repo and linked into the worktree.\n\nSymlink beat the alternatives. Passing `--settings <repo>/.claude/settings.json` loads only one file and leaves four other surfaces broken: `settings.local.json`, project agents/skills/commands, hook scripts whose paths are relative to `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`, and `.mcp.json`. Copying the config (rather than linking) isolates the worker but discards entire's checkpoint writes when the worktree is removed. Tracking `.claude/` in git would commit machine-specific config. The accepted tradeoff: a worker runs `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, so it shares — and could mutate — the live config dirs, exactly as a non-worktree run would (decision 2)."
}
```

No comment-convention violations. I have enough to deliver the review.

## Review Summary

**Verdict:** APPROVE WITH RECOMMENDATIONS (no Critical or Required blockers; one Required-adjacent risk worth a guard, see below — I'd merge after acknowledging it)

**Overview:** The change gives headless workers project-config parity by symlinking a configurable set of the repo's real `.claude/` / `.mcp.json` (and opt-in extras) into each worktree after `worktree.add`. The implementation is small, the validation is sound, the symlink/teardown semantics are correct, and the tests genuinely assert behaviour. All 139 tests pass. My one substantive concern is an unstated correctness assumption (configured paths must be gitignored) that, when violated, fails in two unpleasant ways.

### Critical Issues
None.

### Required Issues

- **The whole design rests on an unvalidated assumption: every configured `worktree_config_paths` entry is gitignored in the target repo.** I verified both failure modes empirically. If an operator adds a path that is *not* gitignored and *not* already tracked-and-checked-out:
  1. The worker's `git add -A` stages the symlink (`A .claude`), polluting the commit that gets pushed to main.
  2. `git worktree remove` then refuses with `fatal: ... contains modified or untracked files` and exits 128.

  The design-decisions doc states the gitignore property as a fact ("The symlinked entries are gitignored in the worktree ... so the worker's `git add` never stages them"), but nothing in the code *enforces* or *checks* it — it's true for the two defaults in a typical repo, and silently false otherwise. This is exactly the kind of assumption that should be named and guarded rather than assumed (eng-principles universal #4). Recommended fix, smallest first: at minimum document the precondition prominently in `repos.example.yml` and the README ("each entry must be gitignored in the target repo"). Better: have `link_project_config` skip (or warn on) a `name` that is tracked-but-absent / not-ignored, since the symlink for such a path is actively harmful. `drain_cycle/worktree.py:56-64`, `docs/design-decisions.md` §10.

- **`worktree.remove` at `orchestrator.py:253` is unguarded, and this change raises the probability it throws.** `remove()` raises `RuntimeError` on non-zero git exit; the call on the Done path is outside any try/except, so a teardown failure becomes an uncaught traceback *after* the issue is already recorded Done in Linear and the run log — the worst place to crash. This call is pre-existing (not introduced here), so I'm not blocking on it, but the new untracked-symlink surface is a new way to trigger it (see the Required item above). Recommended fix: wrap the `worktree.remove` on the success path so a teardown failure is recorded as a halt/warning rather than crashing the drain mid-cycle. `drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:253`.

### Suggestions (Nit / Optional)

- **Optional — `link_project_config` failing mid-loop leaves a partial set of symlinks.** If `os.symlink` raises on the 2nd of 3 names (e.g. permission/FS error), the first link already exists and the call is inside the pre-spawn try, so it becomes a "setup failed" halt. The worktree is then orphaned with a partial link set and is never torn down (teardown only runs on Done). This is an acceptable contract — a halt is the right outcome and the operator gets git's path in the halt reason — but the orphaned worktree + partial links is mild operator cleanup debt. Worth a sentence in the design doc noting the partial-state-on-halt behaviour. `drain_cycle/worktree.py:54-65`.

- **Nit — `source.exists()` follows symlinks, `os.path.lexists(link)` does not; the asymmetry is intentional and correct, but undocumented for the source side.** If the repo's own `.claude` were a dangling symlink, `source.exists()` returns False and it's silently skipped — almost certainly the right behaviour, but the docstring only explains the `lexists` choice on the link side. `drain_cycle/worktree.py:58,61`.

- **Optional — validation rejects `..` and absolute paths but not a single-segment name that resolves to something surprising via a symlinked repo subpath.** Out of scope for this change and low-risk given the operator owns `repos.yml`; noting only for completeness. The current validation (non-list, empty, non-string, empty-string, absolute, `..`) is appropriately complete for the threat model (operator-controlled config, not untrusted input).

### What's Done Well

- **The symlink target is absolute** (`repo.resolve() / name`), so it resolves correctly from the worker's worktree cwd regardless of where the worker runs — I verified `os.readlink` returns the absolute repo path. This sidesteps the relative-target footgun entirely.
- **Teardown safety is real, not assumed.** I reproduced `git worktree remove` against a live symlink: it deletes the link and leaves the target's contents intact. The `test_remove_preserves_symlink_target` test proves this and is not a tautology — it writes `"keep me\n"`, removes the worktree, and re-reads the repo file.
- **The orchestrator integration test proves its claimed precondition.** I confirmed independently that a gitignored `.claude/settings.json` is *absent* from an unlinked worktree, so the probe's `"present"` result can only come from the symlink the orchestrator created. Strong, behaviour-level assertion.
- **`test_skips_existing_tracked_entry` exercises the real tracked-checkout path** (commits `.claude`, lets `git worktree add` materialise it, asserts not-a-symlink) — it genuinely tests the `lexists` skip branch rather than mocking it.
- Validation error messages name the offending entry with `!r`, matching the existing `RepoConfigError` house style; tests assert on the message text, so a regression in wording is caught.
- No comment-convention violations: no ticket IDs, SHAs, or fix-history narration in any added comment or docstring (sample-data IDs `ABA-A`/`ABA-CFG` correctly used only as test fixtures).

### Verification Story

- **Tests reviewed:** Yes. 9 new tests across the three surfaces; all assert behaviour, not implementation. The orchestrator probe test and the teardown test are the load-bearing ones and both hold up. The repos.py validation tests cover every rejection branch (non-list, empty, non-string, absolute, `..`).
- **Build verified:** Yes — `uv run pytest -q` → 139 passed in 14.3s.
- **Security checked:** Yes. Path-traversal validation is complete for the operator-controlled threat model (absolute, `..`, empty/non-string all rejected). Symlink target is absolute and confined to the repo; no escape via the configured names. The one security-adjacent note is that a worker runs `--dangerously-skip-permissions` and now reads/writes the repo's *live* config dirs through the link — but that's the explicitly accepted tradeoff documented in design-decisions §10 (decision 2), not a regression.

**Relevant files:**
- `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/worktree.py` (`link_project_config`, lines 36-65; unguarded `remove` at 68-70)
- `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/orchestrator.py` (link call 153-155; unguarded teardown at 253)
- `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/repos.py` (`_parse_worktree_config_paths`, 95-128)
- `/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/docs/design-decisions.md` (§10 — the gitignore claim that should be flagged as a precondition)

The defaults (`.claude`, `.mcp.json`) and `.entire` are gitignored in a typical repo, so the symlink is invisible to git in the worktree (it shares the repo's tracked `.gitignore`) and the worker's `git add` never stages it. A non-ignored, untracked entry would be the opposite: the worker would stage the symlink into the commit it pushes, and `git worktree remove` would then refuse the dirty worktree. The link step doesn't enforce this — it skips a name only when it's absent in the repo or already present in the worktree — so the requirement is documented (`repos.yml` comment, README) rather than coded. The link step is also not transactional: if `os.symlink` fails partway through the set, the already-created links remain and the failure surfaces as a pre-spawn \"setup failed\" halt, leaving the worktree in place for inspection."

{
  "title": "Guard worktree teardown on the Done path so a removal failure doesn't crash the drain",
  "team": "Personal",
  "project": "Autonomous cycle drain",
  "labels": [
    "repo:drain-cycle"
  ],
  "description": "## Problem\n\nOn the Done path, `orchestrator.run()` calls `worktree.remove(target_repo, worktree_path)` outside any try/except. `worktree.remove` raises `RuntimeError` on a non-zero `git worktree remove` exit. So a teardown failure becomes an uncaught traceback **after** the issue is already recorded Done in Linear and written to the run log — the worst place to crash, because the drain dies mid-cycle with the bookkeeping already committed.\n\nThis is a pre-existing gap, surfaced during the code review of \"Workers inherit the repo's project config inside worktrees\". That change slightly raises the probability of a teardown failure: if an operator configures a `worktree_config_paths` entry that is neither gitignored nor tracked, the worker's `git add` stages the symlink and `git worktree remove` then refuses the dirty worktree (exit 128). The config precondition is now documented, but the unguarded teardown remains.\n\n## Outcome\n\nA teardown failure on the Done path is recorded (run-log note / warning on stderr) and the drain continues or halts cleanly, rather than crashing with a traceback after the issue is already Done.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n1. Wrap the success-path `worktree.remove` so a `RuntimeError` is caught and converted to a recorded outcome (warning + run-log field, or a clean halt — pick per the existing halt contract), not an uncaught traceback.\n2. Test covering a `worktree.remove` failure on the Done path that asserts the drain doesn't crash and the failure is recorded.\n\n## Notes\n\nLow priority / backlog — pre-existing behaviour, not a regression. Slot into a future cycle deliberately."
}
```

Symlink a configurable\nset of the repo's real project config into each worktree after creation, so a\nheadless worker loads the same settings/hooks/agents/skills/MCP as a session\nat the repo root. Because the link points at the live dir, a stateful hook\nreads and writes the repo's actual config exactly as a non-worktree run does;\nteardown removes the link, not the target.\n\nThe set defaults to [.claude, .mcp.json] and is overridable via an optional\nworktree_config_paths key in repos.yml. Entries must be relative paths\nwithout '..' and gitignored in the target repo.\nEOF\ngit log --oneline -1",

}

{
  "issueId": "ABA-239",
  "body": "Shipped. Symlinks a configurable set of the repo's project config into each worktree after `worktree.add`, so a headless worker loads the same settings/hooks/agents/skills/MCP as a session at the repo root. Pushed to `main` as `3582f03`.\n\n**What landed**\n- `repos.py` — optional `worktree_config_paths` key (default `(\".claude\", \".mcp.json\")`), validated in `load()` (rejects non-list, empty, non-string, absolute paths, `..`); new field on the `Repos` dataclass.\n- `worktree.py` — `link_project_config(repo, worktree_path, names)`: skip-if-absent / skip-if-already-present (`lexists`) / else `os.symlink`; absolute link target.\n- `orchestrator.py` — calls it after `worktree.add` inside the existing pre-spawn `try` (errors → recorded \"setup failed\" halt).\n- Docs — design-decisions §10 flipped gap→fix, README \"Debug capture\" reframe + Install config-key documentation, `repos.example.yml` block.\n- Tests — 9 new (6 worktree, 5 repos validation, 1 orchestrator integration). Full suite: **139 passed**.\n\n**Code review (`/code-review-and-quality`, fresh-context reviewer)** — verdict: Approve.\n- **Critical: 0 · Required: 0.**\n- **1 Required-adjacent — fixed.** The design assumes every configured path is gitignored in the target repo; a non-ignored entry would be staged by the worker's `git add` and would block `git worktree remove`. The plan chose document-not-code for this; I made the precondition explicit in README, `repos.example.yml`, and §10, and noted the link step's non-transactional / partial-state-on-halt behaviour.\n- **Verified by the reviewer:** absolute symlink target resolves from the worker cwd; `git worktree remove` deletes the link not the target (`test_remove_preserves_symlink_target` is not a tautology); the orchestrator integration test proves its precondition; no comment-convention violations.\n\n**Deferred**\n- Unguarded `worktree.remove` on the Done path (pre-existing; reviewer not blocking) — tracked as **ABA-240** (backlog), since this change slightly raises the odds of a teardown failure.\n- Docstring-asymmetry nit and validation-completeness note — left per plan scope (validation is complete for the operator-controlled threat model)."
}

{"id":"ABA-239","title":"Workers inherit the repo's project config inside worktrees","description":"## Outcome / user story\n\nAs an operator running an unattended cycle drain, I want each worker to load the same project-scoped settings, hooks, agents, and skills as a normal Claude Code session at the repo root. The worker runs in a git worktree, which checks out only tracked files, so any gitignored `.claude/` config is currently missing and the tooling I rely on interactively — for example the `entire.io` checkpointing hooks — silently never runs. Symlinking that config into the worktree restores parity, so the same tooling works headless.\n\n## Approach\n\nSymlink a configurable set of the repo's real project-scoped config dirs/files into each worktree after it's created. The set defaults to `[.claude, .mcp.json]` and is overridable via an optional `worktree_config_paths` key in `repos.yml` (`.entire` is opt-in, since not every repo installs the `entire.io` tool). Symlinks — not copies — so a stateful hook reads and writes the repo's actual config exactly as a non-worktree run does, and teardown (`git worktree remove`) deletes the links but not their targets.\n\n## Acceptance criteria (ordered sub-tasks)\n\n1. **Config** — `repos.yml` accepts an optional `worktree_config_paths` list (default `[.claude, .mcp.json]`); `repos.py` parses and validates it (`RepoConfigError` on a malformed value); `tests/test_repos.py` covers the default, a custom list, and malformed input.\n2. **Symlink helper** — `worktree.link_project_config(repo, worktree_path, names)` symlinks each entry that is present in the repo and not already checked out in the worktree; `tests/test_worktree.py` covers symlink creation, the no-op case, skip-tracked, gitignored-in-worktree, and remove-preserves-target.\n3. **Wiring** — the orchestrator calls the helper after `worktree.add`, inside the existing pre-spawn `try` block, passing `repos.worktree_config_paths`; an integration test asserts that the worker's cwd can resolve `.claude/settings.json`.\n4. **Docs** — update all four surfaces: design-decisions decision 10 flip (gap → fix), README \"Debug capture\" reframe, README Install config-key documentation, and `docs/repos.example.yml`.\n5. **Verify and ship** — full test suite green; run the manual `DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1` parity check; then follow the AGENTS.md completion sequence (review → fix → commit and push to main → Linear comment → Done).\n\n## Size\n\n\\~200-300 lines of code across 4 source files, 3 test files, and 3 docs. Expected \\~2-4M tokens and \\~10-15 minutes wall-clock — inside the per-issue caps (8M tokens / 20 min).","priority":{"value":0,"name":"No priority"},"url":"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-239/workers-inherit-the-repos-project-config-inside-worktrees","gitBranchName":"anton/aba-239-workers-inherit-the-repos-project-config-inside-worktrees","createdAt":"2026-05-23T14:45:38.780Z","updatedAt":"2026-05-23T14:59:55.968Z","archivedAt":null,"completedAt":"2026-05-23T14:59:55.940Z","startedAt":"2026-05-23T14:45:38.905Z","canceledAt":null,"dueDate":null,"slaStartedAt":null,"slaMediumRiskAt":null,"slaHighRiskAt":null,"slaBreachesAt":null,"slaType":"all","status":"Done","statusType":"completed","labels":["model:sonnet","repo:drain-cycle"],"attachments":[],"documents":[],"createdBy":"Anton Babushkin","createdById":"720d0535-dfc6-465f-8111-d1a3e6eab786","project":"Autonomous cycle drain - eliminate manual shepherding","projectId":"3b5c5e13-355a-4e70-b943-6251d7f14baf","team":"Personal","teamId":"c20f56da-f121-4653-bf6c-dc1c8aac21c1","cycleId":"3fb7ba8a-ff55-49c0-bf35-b879d3a4b83c"}

> AGENT

Done. **ABA-239** is shipped and marked Done.

## What landed

Workers spawned in a git worktree now inherit the repo's project-scoped config, closing the gap documented in design-decisions §10 (project hooks like entire.io's checkpointing silently never loaded headless).

- **`repos.py`** — optional `worktree_config_paths` key in `repos.yml` (default `(".claude", ".mcp.json")`), validated in `load()` (rejects non-list, empty, non-string, absolute paths, `..`); new field on the frozen `Repos` dataclass.
- **`worktree.py`** — `link_project_config(repo, worktree_path, names)`: per name, skip-if-absent / skip-if-already-present (`lexists`) / else `os.symlink` with an absolute target.
- **`orchestrator.py`** — calls it after `worktree.add` inside the existing pre-spawn `try`, so any failure becomes a recorded "setup failed" halt.
- **Docs** — §10 flipped gap→fix (+ precondition and partial-state notes), README Debug-capture reframe and Install config-key documentation, `repos.example.yml` block.
- **Tests** — 9 new; full suite **139 passed**.

Committed and pushed to `main` as `3582f03` (rebased cleanly over a concurrent README formatting fix).

## Review outcome

Fresh-context reviewer: **Approve**, 0 Critical / 0 Required. The one Required-adjacent finding — configured paths must be gitignored or they pollute the worker's commit and block teardown — I fixed by making the precondition explicit across all three doc surfaces (the plan had chosen document-not-code for this case).

## Tracked separately

- **ABA-240** (backlog) — the pre-existing unguarded `worktree.remove` on the Done path, which this change slightly raises the odds of triggering.

One step from the plan is yours to run, since it spawns a real `--dangerously-skip-permissions` session and spends tokens: the manual `DRAIN_CYCLE_DEBUG=1 drain-cycle` parity check, then diff the debug log against the §10 baseline to confirm entire's SessionStart/SessionEnd hooks now fire in the worktree.
